## Code-level defects found during #07 on `toolguard/tools/clarity.py` (2026-08-12)

Comments-only pass -- **nothing below was fixed.** Rows are lettered `CL*`; the numeric sequence and `AE`/`C`/`CA`/`D`/`DG`/`DL`/`E`/`EL`/`GA`/`HR`/`INS`/`IU`/`LH`/`M`/`MI`/`N`/`OP`/`P`/`R`/`RA`/`RD`/`RS`/`S`/`SA`/`T`/`TCV`/`TL`/`UR`/`V` are already claimed by concurrent per-file passes. Every row was executed live against the module on this branch, 2026-08-12, from in-memory `Configuration` objects (no config discovery, no real file read).

| # | defect | where | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CL1** | **A user-facing explanation string describes a resolution that cannot happen.** `_explain`'s ask branch reads *"a more-specific allow bypasses the ask, while a more-specific **(or broad, then collapsing)** ask gates it"*. The parenthetical is false in both readings of "broad". Executed 2026-08-12 through `api.decide` on a single layer: allow `git status:*` + blanket ask `*` -> **`allow`**; allow `git status:*` + broad ask `git:*` -> **`allow`**; allow `*` + blanket ask `*` -> **`allow`**. Only allow `git:*` + *more-specific* ask `git status:*` -> `ask`. A blanket `*`-class ask is stripped by `is_universal_pattern` before `resolve_allow_ask` is reached, and a non-blanket but broader ask loses the `_literal_prefix_specificity` comparison, so a broad ask never gates anything. "Collapsing" appears to be a survival of the pre-TOO-15 model that the module docstring also carried (see the report for that correction) -- a blanket ask does not collapse to anything, it makes its layer decline to decide and the cascade runs on. | `toolguard/tools/clarity.py:_explain`, the `ask-overlaps-allow` return | Strings are code, so untouched. The accurate sentence is the one the parenthetical interrupts: *"a more-specific allow bypasses the ask, a more-specific ask gates it"*. Note the text is not merely reworded prose -- it is what the maintenance skill and `security_audit` show a user about a rule they are about to change, so a reader acting on it would expect their broad ask to be doing gating work that it is not. `annotate._annotation_text`'s short form ("more-specific rule wins") is already correct and is unaffected. |
| **CL2** | **`test_same_specificity_layers_are_not_cross_layer` passes regardless of the behaviour it claims to pin.** The Given says "two DIFFERENT files at the SAME specificity", but `_make_provenance` hardcodes `path=Path("/fake/.claude/toolguard_hook.toml")` and varies only `specificity`, so the two layers get **equal** `Provenance` objects (verified: `same.layers[0].provenance == same.layers[1].provenance` is `True`). `per_layer_rules` builds `prov_to_tool_layer = {tl.provenance: tl for tl in tool_layers}`, so the second layer overwrites the first: `permission_layers('Bash')` correctly returns `[(('git:*',), ()), ((), ('git push:*',))]` while `per_layer_rules` returns `[((), ('git push:*',)), ((), ('git push:*',))]` -- **the allow is gone before the analyser sees it.** `assertEqual([], ...)` therefore cannot fail, guard or no guard. Re-run with the two provenances given distinct paths (still specificity 0), the fixture does exercise the guard and still yields `[]`, so **the claimed behaviour is true** -- only the evidence is missing. | `test/unit/test_tools_clarity.py:245-259` (`_make_provenance`, `:18-26`) | Fix is one line in the helper -- `path=Path(f"/fake/{specificity}/.claude/toolguard_hook.toml")`, as `test_ask_resolution._prov` already does -- plus an explicit distinct path in this one test. `test/` was frozen for this pass. Worth pairing with CL3, which is the underlying reason the collapse is silent. |
| **CL3** | **`per_layer_rules` keys its layer lookup by whole-`Provenance` equality, so two layers sharing a provenance silently collapse to one.** `prov_to_tool_layer = {tl.provenance: tl for tl in tool_layers}` (`config_access.py:118`) discards all but the last `ToolPatternLayer` for any repeated key, and the `for layer in config.layers` loop below then hands the SAME rules back for each of them. Measured in CL2's fixture: two layers in, two `LayerRules` out, both carrying the second layer's rules and neither carrying the first's. Real discovery is believed safe -- a `.local.toml` and its regular sibling share `specificity` but differ in `path`, which is a `Provenance` field -- so the reachable damage today is to hand-built configs in tests and tooling, which is exactly where it bit CL2. | `toolguard/tools/config_access.py:per_layer_rules:118` (frozen for this pass; adjacent to `CA1`) | Same root cause as `AE3` (`with_layer_rules_replaced` matching `Provenance` by whole-dataclass equality) seen from the read side rather than the write side, and worth fixing with it. A zip over `config.layers` and its own `permission_layers` entry, or keying on `provenance.path`, removes the silent overwrite; failing that, the collapse deserves to be loud. |
| **CL4** | **The `hasattr(f.provenance, "describe")` guard in the sort key is dead.** `find_confusing_interactions` sorts on `f.provenance.describe() if hasattr(f.provenance, "describe") else str(f.provenance)`, but `InteractionFinding.provenance` is annotated `Provenance`, `Provenance.describe` is defined unconditionally (`config_types.py:45`), and both construction sites in this module pass `layer.provenance` / `allow_layer.provenance` straight through. Nothing in the package or the suite builds a finding with anything else. The tell is two lines down in the same key: `f.guard_provenance.describe()` is called with no such guard, so the two provenance fields are defended inconsistently in one expression. | `toolguard/tools/clarity.py:find_confusing_interactions`, the `sort` key | Cosmetic, but it costs three lines of an already dense lambda and invites a reader to believe a duck-typed provenance is supported. Delete the conditional. No prose was written about it -- an unreachable branch wants removing, not documenting. |
| **CL5** | **A multi-section finding names only the first overlapping guard in each section, in the field and in the explanation.** `by_section["deny"][0]` / `by_section["ask"][0]` pick the first, and everything after it is dropped. Executed 2026-08-12 with allow `git:*` against denies `git push:*`, `git reset:*` and asks `git commit:*`, `git rebase:*`: `guard_pattern` is `'git push:* / git commit:*'` and the explanation names neither `git reset:*` nor `git rebase:*`. | `toolguard/tools/clarity.py:_same_layer_findings`, the multi-section loop | **Low, and deliberately filed as low**: the dropped guards each still produce their own pairwise `deny-shadows-allow` / `ask-overlaps-allow` finding, so nothing vanishes from the report -- the multi-section row is a summary and reads as one. Filed only because the field's shape (`'<deny> / <ask>'`, now documented on `InteractionFinding.guard_pattern`) implies exactly two guards, and a consumer that grew to parse it would be wrong on a three-deny layer. Prefer widening the summary over silently truncating if this is ever touched. |

### Rejected during this pass, with evidence

- **The brief's "if this file promises the finding locates the other layer, it does not"** -- **rejected as stated; the promise is kept here and broken downstream.** `InteractionFinding.guard_provenance` is set on every `cross-layer-dependent` finding and carries the guard layer's full `Provenance`: executed 2026-08-12 on a project(0)+user(1) config, the finding reports `allow='gh:*'` at specificity 0 with `guard_provenance.specificity == 1`. The loss is entirely in `security_audit`, which drops the field when it builds its `RankedFinding` -- already recorded as `SA5`, including the `_finding_key` collision. So the docstring's claim about this dataclass was true and was kept (reworded only to stop calling `provenance` "the layer holding **both** rules", which *was* false for cross-layer findings -- see the report). Nothing added here pointing at `security_audit`: that is an outward claim about another module, and `SA5` is its home.
- **"`InteractionFinding` has no `severity` and no `impact` field"** -- **true, and there was nothing to correct.** No comment in this module mentions severity, ranking or impact; the only adjacent phrase was the module docstring's "clarity is a first-class concern alongside security", which asserts nothing about the record's fields and was cut anyway as rhetoric that had drifted from its own paragraph. The clarity severity and the impact string are authored in `security_audit`, as the brief says.
- **The `per_layer_rules` native-ask blind spot (`CA1`) as prose in this file** -- **confirmed and deliberately not written here.** Executed 2026-08-12 on a native layer holding `permissions.ask = ["Bash(git push:*)"]` alongside `allow = ["Bash(git:*)"]`: `per_layer_rules` returns `ask=()` and `find_confusing_interactions` returns **zero** findings, where a toolguard layer with the same rules yields an `ask-overlaps-allow`. So the coverage hole is real and reaches this module's output. It is not documented here because `config_access.LayerRules.ask` and `per_layer_rules` now state it at the source, and a second copy in the consumer is the one-fact-one-home failure -- it would also be the kind of outward claim rule 0 says rots silently.
- **`security_audit` analysing only `sorted(BUILTIN_TOOLS)` (`SA1`)** -- checked, nothing to correct. `find_confusing_interactions` takes an explicit `tool` argument and makes no claim about which tools a caller passes it; the coverage gap is a property of the call site, which is where `SA1` records it.
- **Tickets 17/18 (`match_command` over- and under-matching) bearing on the overlap claims** -- checked, and no claim here depends on them. Detection is textual token-prefix containment via `pattern_overlap.prefixes_overlap`, which already documents that containment is *sufficient but not necessary* for two patterns to share a command, and the module docstring now says overlap is decided TEXTUALLY, ignoring precedence. Adding a second statement of the sufficiency caveat here would duplicate `prefixes_overlap`'s own docstring.
- **The `Args:`/`Returns:` blocks on the three `_explain*` helpers** -- cut, not shortened. Each described a `str` return as "a calibrated explanation" and re-stated parameters already named by the signature (`allow: The allow body involved`). The one genuinely non-obvious parameter, `_explain_cross_layer`'s `allow_more_specific`, kept a single line -- but stating only the mechanic (which layer carries the **lower** specificity index, 0 = project) rather than the old text's "a lower specificity distance", which names no direction at all.
- **A pointer to `technical-notes.md` for the within-file resolution model** -- rejected. The corrected model is three clauses and fits inline, `technical-notes.md` has no clarity/resolution-semantics section to point at, and `test/unit/test_ask_resolution.py:4` points *at this docstring* by name ("Toolguard's documented within-file model (see the clarity analyzer docstring)"), so moving it would break an existing cross-reference to satisfy nothing.

### Comment defect in a file outside this pass's scope

- `test/unit/test_tools_clarity.py:4-6` -- the module docstring still says *"These verify **the first detector**: a DEFAULT allow rule whose command-space overlaps a deny or ask rule in the same config layer"*, which describes one of the three families the file actually tests. `TestMultiSectionInteraction` and `TestCrossLayerInteraction` sit directly below it, and the same "first slice / first detector" narrative was the stalest text in `clarity.py`'s own docstring. It also carries "calibrated explanation", the module's pet adjective, which the source no longer uses. `test/` was frozen for this pass. Fix alongside CL2's fixture bug.

### Refactoring candidate surfaced by the comment sweep

| # | function | signal | shape of the fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CL-R1** | `clarity._explain`, `_explain_multi_section`, `_explain_cross_layer` | Four `kind` values, four explanation shapes, and the mapping is spread over three functions with three different dispatch styles: `_explain` branches on `kind` internally, `_explain_multi_section` is selected by the caller, and `_explain_cross_layer` branches on `section` **and** `allow_more_specific` for four returns of its own. Seven near-identical f-strings in ~60 lines, all of them re-stating one of the three resolution rules the module docstring states once. `annotate._annotation_text` is a fifth branch on the same four `kind` values in another module, and it and `_explain*` are the only two places that must agree on what the kinds mean. | One `kind -> template` table, or one `explain(finding)` entry point taking the finding it describes; the caller then picks a `kind` and never picks a wording. The four-way `cross-layer` split is the part that pays off -- `(section, allow_more_specific)` is a 2x2 that reads as a table and does not read as four returns. Doing this would also make CL1 a one-line fix in one place rather than a hunt through prose. |
| **CL-R2** | `clarity._cross_layer_findings` | Four nested `for`s and three `if`s over a pre-built `entries` list of 3-tuples, with two `_`-discarded elements per unpack (`for allow_layer, allows, _` then `for guard_layer, _, guards`) -- the tuple exists to be half-ignored twice. The `None`-token skip is written twice in this function and twice more in `_same_layer_findings`, four spellings of "not prefix-comparable, move on". | A small frozen record (allow-side and guard-side views of a layer) instead of the 3-tuple, and one helper yielding the overlapping `(allow, guard)` pairs for a given allow-list and guard-list -- which `_same_layer_findings` also wants, since it runs the identical double loop with the identical `None` guards. That leaves each detector as the part that is genuinely different: which layers it pairs, and which explanation it attaches. |

## Code-level defects found during #07 on `toolguard/tools/takeover_audit.py` (2026-08-12)

Comments-only pass -- **nothing below was fixed.** Rows are lettered `TK*`; the numeric sequence and `AE`/`C`/`CA`/`CL`/`D`/`DG`/`DL`/`E`/`EL`/`GA`/`HR`/`INS`/`IU`/`LH`/`M`/`MI`/`N`/`OP`/`P`/`R`/`RA`/`RD`/`RS`/`S`/`SA`/`T`/`TCV`/`TL`/`UR`/`V` are already claimed by concurrent per-file passes. Every row was executed live against the module on this branch, 2026-08-12, from in-memory `Configuration` objects (no config discovery, no real file read).

| # | defect | where | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TK1** | **`uncovered-blanket-allow` can never mean what its impact string says: the allow it names is stripped at runtime regardless.** The finding reads raw, wrapper-INTACT membership (`perm not in raw_ignored`), but takeover's actual filtering in `Configuration.permission_layers` compares `entry.stripped_pattern` against `TakeoverConfig.normalized_ignored_patterns()` -- and that set strips wrappers, so it is **always exactly `{'*'}`** whenever takeover is on: the built-in `_DEFAULT_IGNORED_ALLOW_PATTERNS` seed `Bash(*)` unconditionally and levels can only union onto it, never remove. Every native `Tool(*)` allow therefore strips to `*` and is filtered, whatever tool it names. Executed 2026-08-12 on the fixture the finding is written for (takeover ON, native `allow = ["mcp__custom__tool(*)"]`, `ignored_allow_patterns = []`): `normalized_ignored_patterns() == {'*'}`; `audit_takeover` returns a **HIGH `uncovered-blanket-allow`**; and `permission_layers('mcp__custom__tool')` returns `allow=()` for the native layer -- the allow is gone. The same config with takeover OFF returns `allow=('*',)`, confirming the filtering is what removes it. So the impact text -- *"this uncovered one remains live in the permission evaluation and can bypass toolguard rules for the corresponding tool"* -- is false in every reachable configuration. | `toolguard/tools/takeover_audit.py:_get_blanket_allows_in_native` + the `uncovered-blanket-allow` `impact` string | Strings are code, so untouched; the docstring's old defence of the raw-form check (*"the normalised check ... would **wrongly** suppress the finding"*) was cut, since the normalised check is precisely what production does. **Decide which is right before touching either side.** Two coherent outcomes: (a) the auditor is wrong and should compare stripped forms, which makes the finding unreachable and it should be deleted; (b) the *runtime* is too permissive -- one blanket `Bash(*)` in the defaults silently covers a blanket allow for every other tool including MCP ones -- in which case the finding is a real early warning and `normalized_ignored_patterns` needs tool scoping. (b) is the security-relevant reading and is worth a ticket of its own. Related to `SA1` only in that `security_audit` republishes this impact text verbatim. |
| **TK2** | **`loose-no-match-fallback` checks the legacy `[takeover_mode]` alias, not the effective setting.** It tests `takeover.no_match_fallback`, which `Configuration.takeover_mode()` populates from `[takeover_mode].no_match_fallback` only (`config.py:833`), defaulting to `'ask'`. The value actually used to decide is `Configuration.resolved_no_match_fallback()`, where the **top-level** key wins outright over the alias. Both directions were executed 2026-08-12. False positive: top-level `no_match_fallback = "deny"` and no `[takeover_mode]` section -> `resolved_no_match_fallback() == 'deny'` but `takeover.no_match_fallback == 'ask'` and the audit emits `loose-no-match-fallback`, telling a correctly hardened user their fail-closed setting is loose. False negative: top-level `"allow"` + `[takeover_mode].no_match_fallback = "deny"` -> `resolved_no_match_fallback() == 'allow'` and the audit stays **silent**. The remediation string compounds it by telling the user to set the value in `[takeover_mode]`, i.e. in the losing location. | `toolguard/tools/takeover_audit.py:audit_takeover`, Invariant 4 (`takeover.no_match_fallback != expected_fallback`) | One-line fix (`config.resolved_no_match_fallback() != "deny"`), but it moves the check from raw to normalized, so `'warn_deny'` would then arrive as `'allow_with_warning'` -- still non-`'deny'`, still flagged, no behaviour lost. **Untested in either direction:** `test/unit/test_tools_takeover_audit.py:_toolguard_layer` always writes `no_match_fallback` into the `[takeover_mode]` section, so all four `TestLooseNoMatchFallback` cases exercise only the alias path and none would notice the fix or the bug. Invariant 5 already reads `config.resolved_undecidable_fallback()`; this is the same check one generation behind. |
| **TK3** | **A regex/alternation `PreToolUse` matcher registers nothing, producing false-positive CRITICAL findings.** `_get_registered_toolguard_tools` returns matcher strings verbatim and `audit_takeover` compares them to tool names by set intersection, special-casing only `'*'` and `''`. Executed 2026-08-12 with `governed_tools = ["Bash", "Read"]` and a single native hook entry `{"matcher": "Bash|Read", "command": "~/.local/bin/toolguard"}`: two **CRITICAL** `hook-not-registered` findings, one per tool, on a setup where toolguard is in fact hooked for both. Alternation matchers are a real form in this repo's own settings -- `.claude/settings.json:5` uses `"matcher": "Edit|Write|Bash"` -- so this is not hypothetical, merely not the documented path (`docs/configuration.md` and `docs/takeover-mode.md` both show one matcher per tool). | `toolguard/tools/takeover_audit.py:_get_registered_toolguard_tools` and the `governed_set & registered_tools` intersection | Severity of the *bug* is low (it over-reports, never under-reports) but the finding it produces is CRITICAL and its remediation tells the user to add a matcher they already have, which is the kind of false alarm that gets an analyser ignored wholesale -- the same argument the module already makes for not flagging `undecidable_fallback = 'deny'`. Fix is to treat a matcher as a regex when it is not a bare tool name (`re.fullmatch(matcher, tool)`), matching how Claude Code reads it. Worth confirming Claude Code's matcher semantics first rather than assuming. |
| **TK4** | **An explicitly empty `governed_tools = []` audits as the four defaults.** `Configuration.governed_tools()` returns `DEFAULT_GOVERNED_TOOLS` whenever *nothing* was configured, and an empty list is indistinguishable from absent in its accumulator (`if not seen`). Executed 2026-08-12: a layer with `governed_tools: []` and no hooks yields four CRITICAL `hook-not-registered` findings for `Bash`/`Read`/`Write`/`Edit`. Filed here because `takeover_audit` is where a user meets the consequence, but the behaviour is `config.py`'s. | `toolguard/config.py:governed_tools` (frozen), surfaced by `takeover_audit.audit_takeover` | Low, and possibly intended -- "empty means unconfigured" is a defensible reading for a security default, and the alternative (empty means govern nothing) is the dangerous one. Recorded so the decision is explicit rather than incidental; if it is intended, `governed_tools`' docstring should say "unset **or empty**" rather than "when no level configures any governed tool". |

### Rejected during this pass, with evidence

- **The brief's "if this file's prose implies callers may pass a divergent takeover state, check it"** -- checked, nothing to correct. The only prose about the parameter was `audit_takeover`'s *"Pre-resolved takeover configuration (optional; read from `config.takeover_mode()` when not provided)"*, which states the mechanism and implies nothing about divergence; `effective_takeover_state` likewise. Its sole production caller (`security_audit`) passes `effective_takeover_state(config)`, i.e. the same object, so no divergence is reachable -- but that is a fact about `security_audit` and `danger.py`, and it stays where those live. What *was* cut was the wrapper's self-description, *"Thin convenience wrapper ... to make the entry point consistent with the audit module's API surface"* -- an unverifiable rationale on a one-line body.
- **`config_access.per_layer_rules`' native-ask blind spot (`CA1`)** -- not applicable. This module does not import `config_access` and reads `layer.content` directly; it never looks at `permissions.ask` at all.
- **Tickets 17/18 (`match_command` over/under-matching)** -- checked, no claim here depends on pattern matching. The only pattern operation in the module is `strip_tool_wrapper(perm) == "*"`, an exact-equality test on the stripped body, and `strip_tool_wrapper('*')` returns `'*'`, so a bare unwrapped `*` counts too -- which the rewritten docstring now says, where the old one claimed a blanket allow was a `Tool(*)` form.
- **The `'deny'`-not-flagged rationale on Invariant 5** -- kept, not cut. *"strictly more conservative than the `'ask'` default, and a finding on a safe configuration would train users to ignore findings"* is verifiable against the check (`resolved_undecidable in ("allow_with_warning", "allow")` passes both `'ask'` and `'deny'`) and is the one piece of judgement in the module a reader could not derive.
- **The eleven-line TOO-19 narrative on Invariants 4 and 5** -- cut wholesale, not shortened. It argued that a blanket `!= 'deny'` test "needs NO code change" for values added by a ticket, and that `'allow'` is flagged in the same set as `'allow_with_warning'` because it is "STRICTLY less safe" -- the first is one clause of consequence dressed as a decision record, the second is already the `impact` string's entire argument, at length, three lines below.

### Comment defect in a frozen file

- `test/unit/test_tools_takeover_audit.py:2-11` -- the module docstring's list of what the file verifies names five behaviours and omits `partial-hook-registration`, which has three cases of its own inside `TestHookNotRegistered` (`:276`, `:300`, `:317`) asserting the CRITICAL-under-takeover / HIGH-otherwise split. Same shape as the defect this pass fixed in the source module, whose own "Invariants checked" list numbered 1-5 and silently omitted invariant 1b while `audit_takeover`'s docstring said it *"Checks all four invariants"* -- three enumerations of the same finding set, three different counts, none of them six.

### Refactoring candidate surfaced by the comment sweep

| # | function | signal | shape of the fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TK-R1** | `takeover_audit.audit_takeover` | ~265 lines, six `AuditFinding(...)` constructions, and **six numbered comment blocks** -- `Invariant 1a`, `1b`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5` -- each introducing a self-contained block that reads its own inputs and appends its own finding. This is the standard's worked signal twice over: the module docstring *enumerates* what the function does, and the body's comments cluster one per branch. Two of the blocks additionally choose between two long impact strings on `takeover.enabled`, and a third does the same on the resolved fallback value, so the prose-to-logic ratio inside them is roughly 4:1. | Six private `_check_*(config, takeover) -> List[AuditFinding]` functions and a delegating `audit_takeover` that concatenates and sorts -- each comment block becomes the docstring it already reads as, and the module docstring's per-finding list becomes a table of contents that maps one-to-one onto the functions. The impact-string ternaries want to go with them: each is a two-branch `takeover.enabled` choice that reads as a table, and pulling them out is what would make `TK1`'s false impact text visible as a single wrong string rather than a paragraph inside a 245-line body. |

## Code-level defects found during #07 on the `tools/` dev-instrument tier -- `coverage_stdlib.py`, `generated_files.py`, `check_doc_links.py`, `comment_hygiene.py` (2026-08-12)

Comments-only pass -- **nothing below was fixed.** Rows are lettered `TI*`; the numeric sequence and `AE`/`C`/`CA`/`CL`/`D`/`DG`/`DL`/`E`/`EL`/`GA`/`HR`/`INS`/`IU`/`LH`/`M`/`MI`/`N`/`OP`/`P`/`R`/`RA`/`RD`/`RS`/`S`/`SA`/`T`/`TCV`/`TK`/`TL`/`UR`/`V` are already claimed by concurrent per-file passes. Every row was executed live against this branch, 2026-08-12.

| # | defect | where | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TI1** | **The sweep's own acceptance gate never examined two of the files it was used to clear.** `_changed_python_files` shells out to `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=AM <ref> -- '*.py'`, which reports **tracked files only**. `tools/comment_hygiene.py` and `tools/generated_files.py` are both untracked on this branch (`git status --porcelain tools/` -> `?? tools/comment_hygiene.py`, `?? tools/generated_files.py`), and neither appears in the command's output, executed 2026-08-12. So `--compare-against HEAD` printed a clean result for them by never looking, and it prints nothing to say so -- the omission is indistinguishable from a pass. A deleted file is skipped twice over (`--diff-filter=AM` excludes `D`, and `compare_code_shape` additionally `continue`s on `not new_path.exists()`). | `tools/comment_hygiene.py:_changed_python_files` | The docstrings now state this, but stating a blind spot is not closing it. Two candidate fixes: enumerate `SWEPT_TREES` and report any `*.py` the diff did not cover as its own `ShapeChange` reason (`"not compared (untracked)"`), or refuse to exit clean when a swept file is untracked. The first is strictly better -- it makes the gate's coverage visible in the same report as its verdict. Until then, any "zero drift" claim in this ticket covers only tracked files; this pass verified its two untracked files by reversing all 22 of its own edits in memory and comparing `code_shape()` of reconstruction against current (both `SAME`), which is not a repeatable check anyone else can run. |
| **TI2** | **`_repo_basenames()` rglobs the virtualenv, so almost any filename resolves and `--stale-references` under-reports.** It is `REPO_ROOT.rglob("*.py")` filtered only on `__pycache__`, and `.venv` sits under `REPO_ROOT` (`sys.prefix == /home/arnon/projects/toolguard/.venv`, executed 2026-08-12). Every site-packages basename therefore lands in the "real file in the repo" set that `find_stale_path_references` uses to suppress a hit, so a docstring naming a deleted module whose name collides with any installed package's file is silently cleared. | `tools/comment_hygiene.py:_repo_basenames` | Add `.venv` (and any other virtualenv dir) to the exclusion alongside `__pycache__`, or reuse `_iter_python_files` over `SWEPT_TREES` instead of `rglob` from the root. Not documented in the docstring, deliberately -- this is a bug, and writing it up as intended behaviour would entrench it. Bounded impact: the scan is report-only, so the cost is false negatives in reconnaissance, never a wrong assertion. |
| **TI3** | **`coverage_stdlib.py` does not exclude the stdlib; it excludes the venv.** `ignoredirs=[sys.prefix, sys.exec_prefix]` -- under `uv run` both are `/home/arnon/projects/toolguard/.venv`, while the stdlib is at `/home/arnon/.local/share/uv/python/cpython-3.14.5-linux-x86_64-gnu/lib/python3.14`, i.e. under `sys.base_prefix`. Executed 2026-08-12: `stdlib under sys.prefix? False`, `stdlib under sys.exec_prefix? False`, `purelib under sys.prefix? True`. So `trace` traces the whole standard library and the `cover/` output carries it. | `tools/coverage_stdlib.py:5-9` | The comment asserting otherwise (`# skip the stdlib itself`) was **deleted** by this pass rather than corrected, per the standard's rule against supplying a replacement rationale for code you did not write. `.claude/rules/testing.md` describes the runner without claiming stdlib exclusion, so no doc is wrong -- but the intent is unambiguous and the fix is `sys.base_prefix`/`sys.base_exec_prefix` alongside the existing two. Low priority: the documented workflow (`grep -rn '>>>>>>' cover/ \| grep toolguard`) filters the noise, at the cost of tracing time and a much larger `cover/`. |
| **TI4** | **`check_doc_links.py` never sees a top-level `#` heading, so a link aimed at one is reported as a bad anchor.** `HEADING_RE = r"^(#{2,6})\s+(.*)"` matches `##`..`######` only. Executed 2026-08-12: `'# Title'` -> no match; **149 `h1` headings** exist across the 25 documents `collect_docs` gathers, and none of them contributes an anchor. No link targets one today (`check()` returns 0 problems), so this is latent, not live. | `tools/check_doc_links.py:HEADING_RE` | Fix is `#{1,6}`. The docstring now names the limitation, which is the right holding action for a checker whose whole value is that a report can be trusted -- a false "bad anchor" on a document title is exactly the finding that gets the tool run once and abandoned. Worth confirming GitHub does emit an anchor for `h1` before changing it. |
| **TI5** | **Two argparse `help` strings understate their flags** -- strings, so untouched. `--compare-against`: *"List every changed \*.py file whose code shape moved relative to GIT\_REF"*, but `compare_code_shape` also reports files it could **not** compare (`"file added (no prior version to compare)"`, `"old/new version does not parse"`), which are not shape movements and read as failures in the same list. `--stale-references`: *"Report .py-looking paths and dotted module.symbol references that don't resolve"* omits that whole classes are skipped rather than judged (stdlib roots, non-package double-backtick spans, short-form Sphinx roles) and that the scan asserts nothing. | `tools/comment_hygiene.py:_parse_args` | Both are cosmetic against the module docstring, which now states the real contract. Fix when the strings are next touched for another reason. |
| **TI6** | **`DOC_GLOBS` contains no globs** -- it is a tuple of five literal filenames (`README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `llms.txt`, `technical-notes.md`, `CLAUDE.md`); the globbing happens in `DOC_DIRS`' `rglob("*.md")`. A name, hence code, hence untouched. | `tools/check_doc_links.py:48` | Rename to `DOC_FILES`. Trivial, and the `#:` comment above it is accurate either way. |
| **TI7** | **`architecture_fitness.py` carries a second, fuller copy of `generated_files.py`'s rationale** -- *"detected by CONTENT, never by a hardcoded filename -- a hardcoded path silently stops protecting a second generated file the moment one is added"* (`:42-50`) restates what the lifted module's own docstring says. One fact, two homes, and they will diverge. `architecture_fitness.py` is frozen (uncommitted non-sweep changes), so only the `generated_files.py` side was trimmed. | `tools/architecture_fitness.py:42-50` (frozen) vs `tools/generated_files.py:2-5` | The fact belongs with the implementation. When `architecture_fitness.py` unfreezes, cut its "Generated code" paragraph down to what a reader of *that* file needs -- which trees are excluded from which mode -- and let the detection rule live where the detector is. |

### Rejected during this pass, with evidence

- **The brief's "if `check_doc_links.py` would catch those [stale docstring pointers], its docstring should say what class of pointer it checks and what it does not."** -- it would not catch any of them, and the premise does not hold. `LINK_RE` matches only `[text](target#anchor)` markdown links, and `collect_docs` reads only the five named documents plus `docs/` and `skills/`. It never opens a `.py` file and never inspects prose. The `installer.py` and `takeover_audit.py` failures the brief cites are English pointers inside Python docstrings; the tool that looks at those is `comment_hygiene.py --stale-references`, and even that resolves only `.py` paths and dotted `module.symbol` tokens, not sentences like *"see X's docstring"*. Adding a "what it does not check" clause to `check_doc_links.py` would have been speculative prose defending against a confusion nobody has had -- the title line already says *"every internal `[text](file#anchor)` link in the documentation"*. Recorded because the class of pointer that keeps failing in this ticket has **no** mechanical checker, which is a real gap and not this file's to fill.
- **The brief's guess that `coverage_stdlib.py` at 18 lines "may well be a fourth" nothing-substantive file** -- rejected. Its single comment was false (`TI3`).
- **The `#:` comment on `LINK_RE` claiming underscore omission "was the 2026-07-23 bug"** -- the causal attribution is false and was cut; the mechanical consequence was verified and kept. `check_doc_links.py` was created 2026-07-30 (`b8ad025`, the only commit touching that character class), i.e. **after** both dated bugs in its own module docstring, so its anchor class never omitted underscores. What the 2026-07-23 bug describes is anchors *written into the docs* with underscores stripped -- a different artefact. The surviving half was executed 2026-08-12: with the class narrowed to `[A-Za-z0-9-]+`, `[x](README.md#no_match_fallback--ask-default)` yields `[]` from `findall`, against one match with the class intact, and a link that does not match is never checked and never reported.
- **`check_doc_links.py`'s "A heading only yields a clean slug when it has no separator punctuation at all"** -- false, and refuted by the parenthetical example attached to it. Executed 2026-08-12: `"Phase 0: Preflight"` -> `phase-0-preflight`, `"Phase 0; Preflight"` -> `phase-0-preflight`, `"Phase 0, Preflight"` -> `phase-0-preflight`, `"Phase 0 (beta)"` -> `phase-0-beta` -- all four carry separator punctuation and all four are clean. The run appears when the punctuation has a space on **each** side (`"Phase 0 : Preflight"` -> `phase-0--preflight`), or when a literal hyphen survives next to a space (the module's own `` `-c` `` example). The sentence was replaced with the verified positive form rather than a repaired universal.
- **The three pointers to "the module docstring's usage note"** (`SWEPT_TREES`, the `--stale-references` section banner, `find_stale_symbol_references`) -- all dangling. The module docstring contained no such note: no mention of `technical-notes.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `.pyscn.toml`, "Stage 5", or report-only status. Two were deleted; the third now resolves, because the rewritten module docstring describes `--stale-references` explicitly. Flagged as the same failure the standard names -- *"replacing an inline explanation with 'see technical-notes.md' when that section does not contain it"* -- occurring here in a file written during this very ticket.
- **`comment_hygiene.py`'s "it never certifies a real code change as prose-only"** -- an unverifiable guarantee headline over a true mechanical sentence, the exact shape the brief predicts. Cut and replaced with what the mechanism does. See the report for the measured equivalence classes; the two that matter are that `# noqa: F401` and `# type: ignore[assignment]` removals both compare **SAME**, and that untracked files are never compared at all (`TI1`).

### Refactoring candidate surfaced by the comment sweep

| # | function | signal | shape of the fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TI-R1** | `comment_hygiene.find_stale_symbol_references` | ~45 lines whose body is five sequential skip-guards before the actual resolution, **three of them carrying their own multi-line comment block** explaining a different exemption (stdlib roots, bare double-backtick spans, short-form Sphinx roles) and a fourth explaining the longest-prefix-first ordering. The comments cluster one per guard, which is the standard's unnumbered form of the signal -- and the docstring cannot state the function's contract without the hedge *"several shapes are skipped rather than judged -- see the guards in the body"*, because the contract is the guards. | A `_should_skip_reference(reference, is_role_ref) -> bool` (or a small tuple of named predicates) lifting the four exemptions out, leaving a loop that reads *find the module, check the symbol*. Each comment block becomes the docstring of the predicate it already describes, and `find_stale_symbol_references`' docstring gets to name the exemptions by function name instead of pointing at a body. |

## TSI — from the comment sweep of `tools/touch_set_inventory.py` (2026-08-12)

Editor pass on the M2 blind-predictor input tool. 809 -> 769 lines, zero code-shape drift, 2733 tests OK. Everything below was executed, not read.

### TSI: code-level defects (comments-only sweep could not fix these)

| # | defect | evidence | where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TSI1** | **`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]` under-states the gitignore subset, and it is the target of a cross-module pointer.** It lists four unsupported things (nested cascading, negation, global/repo-config excludes, `git` subprocess) but does **not** say that an *anchored* pattern loses globbing entirely. `_is_gitignored` compares a pattern containing `/` **literally** against the relative POSIX path -- `fnmatch` is never reached on that branch. Executed 2026-08-12: `_is_gitignored(Path("docs/a.py"), ["docs/*.py"])` -> `False`; `["build/*"]` vs `build/a.py` -> `False`; `["**/foo.py"]` vs `a/b/foo.py` -> `False`. `tools/touch_set_score.py`'s own `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS` item 8 points here for *"exactly what subset of gitignore syntax is and is not supported"*, so the gap is inherited by the scorer's published limitations too. **No impact on the TOO-45 measurement itself**: this repo's `.gitignore` was checked line by line and contains no wildcard inside an anchored pattern (`/.claude`, `logs`, `tmp/`, `skills/debug-issue/`, `.github/code-review-graph.instruction.md` all resolve correctly). The matcher's own docstring now states the literal-comparison behaviour; the string could not be touched under the comments-only rule. | `tools/touch_set_inventory.py:KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]`, `_is_gitignored`; `tools/touch_set_score.py:224` |
| **TSI2** | **`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS` does not mention that gitignored files vanish from the report with no trace.** Not wrong, but the omission is what let the module docstring carry a *"Never silently skip anything"* headline (see TSI4). Executed: a tree with `.gitignore` = `ignored_dir/` yields `modules=['kept.py']`, `parse_failures=['broken.py: invalid syntax ...']` -- `ignored_dir/mod.py` appears in neither, and nothing in the report names it. This is deliberate and correct behaviour (a filename can leak the requirement to a blind predictor), but the inventory's completeness is load-bearing for `touch_set_score`, so it belongs in the published limitations. | `tools/touch_set_inventory.py:KNOWN_LIMITATIONS`, `run_inventory` |
| **TSI3** | **The `except OSError` fallback in `discover_python_files` is effectively dead on Linux, and its comment named a case that does not raise.** `Path.resolve()` defaults to `strict=False`, which does not raise for a broken symlink *or* a symlink loop. Executed 2026-08-12: broken symlink -> `/tmp/.../nope.py` (no exception); `a -> b -> a` loop -> `/tmp/.../loop_a.py` (no exception). The comment's *"(e.g. a broken symlink)"* example was cut; the branch itself is code and was left alone. Decide whether to keep it as defensive or drop it. | `tools/touch_set_inventory.py:discover_python_files` |

### TSI: false claims found and cut (all pre-existing; every one reached outside its own function)

- **`run_inventory`: "Skips anything matched by ... `.gitignore` (D12) and any file that cannot even be read (D11) ... both named explicitly in `parse_failures` rather than silently absent."** False on the gitignore half -- gitignored files are named nowhere (see TSI2 for the executed output). The classic stranded-antecedent shape: the `parse_failures` clause is true only of the read/parse half it got joined to. Rewritten to the read/parse half alone.
- **Module docstring: "Never silently skip anything"** -- a section *headline* directly above a true mechanical sentence, and false as a universal for exactly the reason above: discovery drops `EXCLUDED_DIR_NAMES` directories and gitignore matches with no record. Retitled *"Failures are named, not folded away"* and the discovery-side exclusions stated explicitly.
- **Module docstring: "including this very docstring's own worked example (`RuleEntry.allow_in_auto_mode`, a dataclass property, in an earlier tree layout)"** -- a dangling self-reference: this docstring contains no worked example. The `RuleEntry.allow_in_auto_mode` example lives in `tools/touch_set_score.py`'s docstring. The symbol itself never existed in `toolguard/` (`git log -S` finds it only in `test/unit/test_touch_set_inventory.py` and `test/unit/test_change_role_classifier.py` fixtures, and in the canary-experiment reports, where it is a `bool = False` dataclass **field**, not a property). Whole paragraph cut with the rest of the exit-1 history.
- **`_collect_qualnames`: "Assignment targets are deliberately scanned ONLY at module and class scope, never inside a function body."** False universal. Executed on a fixture with `class C: field; def m(): class Inner: nested_field` -> qualnames include `C.m.Inner.nested_field`, an assignment lexically inside a function body. The true rule is *module body and any class body*, and the docstring now also records the gap the old wording hid: a module-level assignment nested inside a block (`if TYPE_CHECKING:`) is **not** collected -- executed, `if True: CONDITIONAL = 4` produces no `CONDITIONAL` qualname.
- **`_nearest_locations`: "cutoff chosen empirically to avoid noise on short names."** Unsupported invented rationale -- `0.6` is `difflib.get_close_matches`'s own default (`signature: (word, possibilities, n=3, cutoff=0.6)`). Deleted rather than replaced with a different reason, per the standard's `once_per_store.py` precedent.
- **The `change_role_classifier` avoidance block: "see tools/touch_set_score.py's module docstring for why: an adversarial review found that engine's role labels anti-correlated with code quality, and after that finding the TOO-45 M2 instrument suite was redesigned to depend on it nowhere at all."** A pointer to text that does not exist -- `tools/touch_set_score.py` never mentions `change_role_classifier` or role labels anywhere (grep: zero hits); its adversarial review is about surprise *count vs rate* Monte Carlo. And the outward claim is contradicted by `reports/micro-canary-protocol.md:71`, which has the M2 protocol still using `change_role_classifier` for the DECISION/WRITE/CONDUIT/CEREMONY labels. The nearest true statement found (`reports/surprise-factor-protocol.md:104`) is about *"this ticket's abandoned scorers"*, not this module. Cut to one line recording the deliberate non-dependency, with no rationale.
- **`_first_line`: "The first physical line of *docstring* after `ast.get_docstring`'s own dedent/clean."** Not what it does -- the body strips first, so leading blank lines are skipped. Executed: `_first_line("\n\nlead blank\nmore")` -> `'lead blank'`, not `''`. Now "the first non-blank line".
- **`build_report`: "so the two presentations can never drift apart."** The unverifiable-guarantee headline shape. `print_text_report` renders a chosen subset of the dict and formats it independently; sharing the dict does not stop presentation drift. Cut to what the function returns.
- **`_is_gitignored`: "a pattern with an internal `/` is ... matched against the full relative POSIX path."** Vague in the direction that reads as "matched" i.e. globbed. Replaced with the executed behaviour (literal comparison, `docs/*.py` does not exclude `docs/a.py`) -- see TSI1.

### TSI: cut without replacement

- **The bolded audit paragraph** (*"This guarantee was verified, not just claimed ... 170 files opened, zero outside the tree, zero under `.git`, zero subprocess/socket/exec events"*). The measurement is real and corroborated -- `reports/micro-canary-protocol.md:198` records *"170 file opens, none outside the tree, none under `.git`, no subprocess, no VCS path"* -- but it is a point-in-time measurement stated as a standing property, the `annotate.py` "Design guarantee" shape, and it had already picked up embellishment (`socket/exec` events are not in the source record). The hazard it protects (never add `--old`/`--new`) survives in the mechanical sentence above it; the history stays in the protocol report. Two behavioural tests (`test_module_never_imports_subprocess`, `test_load_gitignore_patterns_only_reads_the_local_file_no_other_io`) enforce the part that matters.
- **`D5` / `D11` / `D12` increment labels** throughout (seven sites). The test classes still carry them (`TestD5WidenedQualnameCollection`, `TestD11SafeReadText`, `TestD12Gitignore`), which is where a label belongs -- a test names what it tests. Nothing pointed at the source docstrings by label.
- **The seven-line justification on the `normalize_location` import** (*"...exactly the 'check for existing code before writing it again' discipline this suite is held to"*), the `pyproject.toml` "NOT shipped" cross-reference, the "an earlier version exited 1 on any invalid location" history, and `_safe_read_text`'s four-case exception enumeration.

### TSI: rejected from the brief, with evidence

- **"If it claims ASCII output"** -- it does not, and there is nothing to correct. Executed: a module docstring of `"café — naïve"` survives `build_report` unchanged (`café — naïve`), and no comment in the file asserts an ASCII property. Unlike `rule_apply.py:467` and `mining.py:331`, no such claim was ever made here.
- **Asymmetric normalisation between predictions and tree locations** -- suspected and checked, not a defect. `validate_predictions` runs `normalize_location` over predicted locations but stores tree locations raw. Benign: `normalize_path_text` is the identity for a POSIX relative path with no `./` or leading `/`, and `normalize_qualname_text`'s NFKC pass is a no-op on names from `ast`, since CPython's tokenizer already NFKC-normalises identifiers. Nothing added.

### TSI: refactoring candidate

| # | function | signal | shape of the fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TSI-R1** | `touch_set_inventory._is_gitignored` | 12 lines carrying two entirely different matching rules -- anchored literal-prefix comparison and per-component `fnmatch` -- selected by an `anchored` flag computed mid-loop, after a trailing-slash strip that changes which rule applies. Its docstring needs three paragraphs and two worked examples to be accurate (and the pre-sweep one-paragraph version was wrong, TSI1). The commentary clusters exactly on the branch split. | `_matches_anchored(posix, pat)` and `_matches_component(parts, pat)`, with the loop reduced to strip-trailing-slash, pick, delegate. Each function's rule becomes its own one-line docstring and the wildcard gap (TSI1) is stated where it is implemented, not in a paragraph that has to disclaim the other branch. |

## TSS — from the comment sweep of `tools/touch_set_score.py` (2026-08-12)

Editor pass on the M2 scorer -- the tool that produced the numbers the TOO-45 measurement conclusions rest on. 1138 -> 1096 lines, zero code-shape drift on this file, `ruff format`/`check` clean, 2733 tests OK. Everything below was executed against the module, not read off it.

### TSS: code-level defects (comments-only sweep could not fix these)

| # | defect | evidence | where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TSS1** | **The tool prints "NO SCORE, RATE, OR RATIO IS COMPUTED HERE" three lines above a computed ratio.** `build_report` evaluates `f"{total_predictions / total_actual:.2f}x actual count"` -- the only division anywhere in the module (`grep " / "` -> one hit, line 678 pre-sweep) -- and publishes it as `location_counts.predicted_to_actual_ratio`. Executed: `build_report(...)` -> `{'predicted_unique': 2, 'actual_unique': 1, 'predicted_to_actual_ratio': '2.00x actual count'}`. **Four separate frozen strings assert the opposite**: `print_text_report`'s banner (*"NO SCORE, RATE, OR RATIO IS COMPUTED HERE ... Every number below is either a plain input count or the length of the list printed with it"*), `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]` (*"This tool computes no rate, ratio, or score of any kind"*), `_FORMAT_HELP` (*"This tool computes NO score, rate, or ratio. It prints lists."*), and the argparse `description` (*"no score, rate, or ratio of any kind"*). A live run shows the banner and `predicted/actual : 1.00x actual count` in the same screenful. The ratio is deliberate -- `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[1]` says it exists so prediction volume is visible -- so the fix is to the four blanket denials, not to the field. The docstrings now state it precisely; the strings could not be touched under the comments-only rule. | `tools/touch_set_score.py`: `build_report`, `print_text_report`, `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]`, `_FORMAT_HELP`, `_parse_args` |
| **TSS2** | **Two-judge mode never reports a duplicate location, and two frozen strings promise it does.** `main` calls `_dedupe` on each judge's file and discards both ambiguous maps (`j1_unique, _ = _dedupe(j1_entries)`); `reconcile_two_judges` then emits unique locations, so `build_evidence`'s own `_dedupe` finds nothing and `ambiguous_actuals` is always `{}`. Executed 2026-08-12 with judge 1 listing `dup.py` twice: `_dedupe(judge1)` ambiguous map `{'dup.py': 2}`; after reconciliation `build_evidence(...).ambiguous_actuals` -> `{}`; the same duplicate through the single-judge path -> `{'dup.py': 2}`. `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[6]` claims duplicates *"are reported explicitly in the ambiguous_\* buckets ... never silently merged away without being named"*, and `print_text_report`'s AMBIGUOUS ACTUALS banner promises *"BOTH judges' kinds shown for each duplicate so a real disagreement buried in a duplicate is never invisible"* -- unreachable, since the only path that populates `kind_2` is the path that empties the bucket. Fix is in `main`: merge the two ambiguous maps and pass them through. An inline comment now names the loss at the exact line. | `tools/touch_set_score.py:main`, `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[6]`, `print_text_report` |
| **TSS3** | **`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[2]` calls `graining_check` "the fraction of bare-path vs. qualname-suffixed locations on each side".** It is two counts, not a fraction. Executed: `_graining_check([...one bare path...])` -> `{'bare_path_count': 1, 'qualname_count': 0}`. Harmless in itself, but it is the *one* limitation a reader consults to decide whether two files were authored at the same granularity, and "fraction" invites comparing two sides of different sizes directly. | `tools/touch_set_score.py:KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[2]` |
| **TSS4** | **`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]` cites a report that does not exist, with a draw count that does not match the real source.** It attributes the Monte Carlo result to the *"TOO-45 adversarial report, 20,000 paired draws"*. `find . -iname '*adversarial*'` returns nothing; `touch-set-adversarial-report.md` is not in `reports/` or anywhere in the tree. The real source is `reports/micro-canary-protocol.md:185`, which says **"3000 draws per cell across n=1..12 and p=1.0..0.4"** and confirms the substantive figures (64.7% at p=0.8, 3 locations vs 8, one leak held equal; and 90.9% at p=0.5, which the string omits). `grep -rn "20,000"` finds the number **only** inside `touch_set_score.py` itself -- the docstring copy and the string -- so the draw count is unsupported by any document in the repo. The rate figures `0.333 vs 0.125` are just 1/3 and 1/8 and are fine. The docstring copy of the whole argument was cut (it duplicated the string the tool already prints); the string's dead citation remains. | `tools/touch_set_score.py:KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]`; real source `reports/micro-canary-protocol.md:185` |
| **TSS5** | **`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[7]` over-promises what its cross-module pointer delivers** -- the brief's finding 1, confirmed. It sends the reader to `tools/touch_set_inventory.py`'s `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS` for *"exactly what subset of gitignore syntax is and is not supported"*. That target is accurate but is a list of four **unsupported features** (nested cascading, negation, global/repo-config excludes, `git` subprocess); it says nothing about anchored patterns losing globbing, which is a **matching-semantics** fact and lives in `_is_gitignored`'s docstring instead. "Exactly what ... is and is not supported" claims a completeness the target does not have. Fix when TSI1 is fixed: either close the gap in the inventory's own list (making this pointer true) or retarget this string at `_is_gitignored`. See TSI1 for the executed matcher evidence. | `tools/touch_set_score.py:KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[7]`; target `tools/touch_set_inventory.py:KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]` |
| **TSS6** | **`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[5]` attributes NFKC and whitespace-collapse to the whole location.** It reads *"exact string equality on (path, qualname) AFTER normalisation (NFKC, internal-whitespace collapse around '.', backslash-to-forward-slash)"*. Both NFKC and the whitespace collapse are applied by `normalize_qualname_text` only; the path half goes through `normalize_path_text`, which does neither. Executed: `normalize_location("ａ.py")` -> `'ａ.py'` (fullwidth U+FF41 **survives** in a path), while `normalize_location("m.py::ａ")` -> `'m.py::a'`; and `normalize_location("a b.py")` -> `'a b.py'` versus `normalize_location("m.py::Outer. inner")` -> `'m.py::Outer.inner'`. Real consequence: two actuals files spelling the same directory with different Unicode normal forms would not match, and the published limitation says they would. The module docstring made the same claim more strongly (*"Every location string is NFKC-normalised"*) and has been corrected. | `tools/touch_set_score.py:KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[5]`, `normalize_path_text` |
| **TSS7** | **Two dead functions.** `detect_actuals_mode` (returns `MODE_DUAL_JUDGE`/`MODE_SINGLE_JUDGE`) and `_entry_dict` are each defined once and called nowhere -- not in the module, not in `test/unit/test_touch_set_score.py`. `grep -c` over module + test file: `detect_actuals_mode` 1 occurrence (its own `def`), `_entry_dict` 1. `main` inlines the two mode constants directly instead. Neither has a docstring, so neither was touched by the sweep. | `tools/touch_set_score.py:detect_actuals_mode`, `_entry_dict` |
| **TSS8** | **`build_report` takes two parameters it never reads.** `raw_prediction_count` and `raw_actual_count` appear in the signature and nowhere in the body (checked against `inspect.getsource` with the docstring stripped: both `False`). `main` computes `raw_actual_count = len(j1_entries) + len(j2_entries)` in one branch and `len(actuals)` in the other purely to feed them. The pre-sweep docstring's claim that *"every numeric field is either a plain input count or a `len()`"* was the only thing that made the "input count" parameters look used. Either report the raw (pre-dedupe) counts -- which would be genuinely informative next to the unique counts -- or drop the parameters. | `tools/touch_set_score.py:build_report`, `main` |
| **TSS9** | **The test file carries two of the same errors (frozen: `test/` is out of scope for this sweep).** `test_report_has_no_rate_or_ratio_key_except_the_documented_one`'s Given/When/Then calls `predicted_to_actual_ratio` *"the plain, **undivided** display string ... (not a numeric score)"* -- it is produced by a division (TSS1); "not a numeric score" is fair, "undivided" is not. And the test module docstring cites the same nonexistent `reports/touch-set-adversarial-report.md` as TSS4, as the source of the D4/D6/D8/D9/D10 hazard suite. | `test/unit/test_touch_set_score.py:1-10, ~336` |

### TSS: false claims found and cut (all pre-existing; the two worst were completeness headlines)

- **Module docstring: "This tool computes NO rate, ratio, score, or cross-tree comparison of any kind ... nothing derived by division appears anywhere in this tool's output."** False -- see TSS1 for the executed output. This is the sweep's canonical shape: a *section headline* ("Evidence, not scoring") whose supporting sentence over-reaches into a universal, sitting above prose that is otherwise true. Replaced with what the code does: the lists it produces, plus the one quotient named explicitly and the reason it is there.
- **Module docstring: "Never silently drop anything -- ... every ambiguous (duplicate-location) entry ... is counted AND listed explicitly -- never folded into another bucket."** False in two-judge mode (TSS2). The same headline shape as `touch_set_inventory`'s *"Never silently skip anything"* (TSI2), in the sibling module, found independently. The section was cut rather than reworded: three of its five clauses were already stated in `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS`, and the one genuinely new fact -- that dual-judge mode drops the ambiguity map -- belongs at the line that drops it, where it now is.
- **Module docstring: "Every location string is NFKC-normalised ... has internal whitespace around `.` collapsed, and has backslashes in the path half converted to forward slashes."** The first two are qualname-half only; only the third was correctly scoped. Executed evidence in TSS6. Rewritten as two sentences, one per half.
- **Module docstring: "Matching is exact string equality ... on this pair"**, under the heading *"The prediction unit is (location, kind)"*. Kind takes no part in matching. Executed: predicted `a.py`/`decide` against actual `a.py`/`test` -> `predicted_and_changed` 1, `kind_mismatches` 1, `predicted_but_not_changed` 0. The nearest antecedent for "this pair" is `(location, kind)`, so the sentence reads as claiming a kind mismatch is a non-match -- the opposite of what the code does, and the opposite of the design in `reports/micro-canary-protocol.md:94`, which wants kind mismatches *visible*, not discarded. Now says matching is on the location and the kinds are compared afterwards.
- **`build_report`: "No field here is a rate, ratio, or score -- every numeric field is either a plain input count or a `len()` of a list printed alongside that same list."** False on both halves: the ratio (TSS1), and `ambiguous_*` items carry an `index`, which is a source position rather than a count. Replaced with the single accurate statement -- which field is the divided one and why -- and no universal about the rest. (I twice wrote a replacement universal here -- *"every other numeric field is a count of entries"* -- and twice had to delete it; the `index` field is what breaks it. Recording the near-miss because the standard predicts exactly this move.)
- **`_graining_check`: "D2: a best-effort signal ... that a file's authors graded locations at a module level".** "graded" for "grained", and the `D2` label points nowhere. See also TSS3 for the "fraction" claim in the string it describes.
- **`_RawObject`: "(see the module docstring's D6 reference)".** A pointer to nothing -- `"D6" in module.__doc__` is `False`; the label lived only in a section-banner comment, which is not the module docstring. Cut with the rest of the D-labels.

### TSS: cut without replacement

- **The 26-line Monte Carlo argument in the module docstring.** Near-verbatim duplicate of `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]`, which is *in this file* and which the tool **prints in every report** -- the "one fact, one home" case with the second home being the tool's own output. It additionally carried the dead report citation and the unsupported "20,000 paired draws" (TSS4), and the past-tense narrative *"An earlier version of this tool promoted the count and demoted the rate ... which replaces an earlier, now-retracted claim"*. Reduced to a two-line pointer at `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS` #1 naming the conclusion. Nothing was relocated: the argument is intact in the constant and in `reports/micro-canary-protocol.md:180-191`.
- **The `pyproject.toml` "NOT shipped" cross-reference** (*"see `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages` list, which does not include this directory"*). True -- `packages = ["toolguard"]`, `pyproject.toml:97` -- and cut anyway under rule 0: it reaches into another file to prove something "Dev-only instrument" already says.
- **Design history, five sites.** *"This is a deliberate reversal of an earlier design, not a stylistic choice"*; *"TWO JUDGES is **now** expressed as ... not a single file with paired `kind_1`/`kind_2` fields, which an earlier version of this tool used"*; `LocationSetDisagreement`'s *"the disagreement a single-file kind_1/kind_2 format could never express"*; `is_abstention`'s *"an earlier version of this tool folded an abstention into 'the predictor was wrong'"*; and `_RawEntry`'s *"successfully-parsed"* framing. **Unverifiable in either direction**: the file has exactly one commit in its entire history (`git log --all --follow` -> `e46900b` only), so every pre-commit revision is gone and no "earlier version" claim here can be checked. Cut rather than kept-with-hedge; the `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[0]` retraction survives as the one record of a reversal.
- **`D2` / `D4` / `D6` / `D9` increment labels** (six sites, including two section banners). The test classes keep theirs, which is where a label belongs.
- **`kind_2: str | None = None  # set only by reconcile_two_judges`** -- a call-site fact, and already in `LocationEntry`'s own docstring.

### TSS: verdicts on the two findings put to this file

- **Finding 1 (the `:224` gitignore pointer over-promises): CONFIRMED, recorded as TSS5, string left alone.** The pointer's target is real and accurate; the phrase *"exactly what subset of gitignore syntax is and is not supported"* is what fails, because the target enumerates unsupported *features* and not *matching semantics*, and the anchored-pattern gap is the latter. `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS[7]` is a string, so it could not be edited here.
- **Finding 2 (the `change_role_classifier` rationale): the claim was NEVER in this file. Nothing restored, nothing invented, gap left open.** `tools/touch_set_score.py` has one commit in its history (`e46900b`); `change_role_classifier`, `anti-correlated` and `role label` return **zero** hits in that commit's version of the file, in the working tree, and in every version in between (there are none). So `touch_set_inventory.py`'s pointer did not aim at text that was later removed -- there was never any text to aim at. Corroborating: the nearest real statement, `reports/surprise-factor-protocol.md:104`, is about *"this ticket's abandoned scorers"* generally, and `reports/micro-canary-protocol.md:71` still has M2 using `change_role_classifier` for its role labels, so the outward claim is contradicted as well as unsourced. This file now says nothing about `change_role_classifier`, which is the accurate state. **The open question -- whether M2's protocol really does still depend on that classifier, and if so whether that was intended -- belongs to the ticket, not to either module's comments.**

### TSS: rejected from the brief, with evidence

- **"If it claims ASCII output"** -- no such claim exists in this file, so there was nothing to correct. Unlike `rule_apply.py:467` and `mining.py:331`, neither the docstrings nor the frozen strings assert an ASCII property; the module handles non-ASCII deliberately (NFKC on the qualname half, `café` used as the worked example) and `_safe_read_text` has no counterpart here because entry files are read as plain UTF-8.
- **Adding a docstring to `detect_actuals_mode`** -- considered, rejected. It is a one-line ternary over two named constants and the standard forbids explaining short simple code; and it is dead (TSS7), so the honest fix is deletion, not documentation.

### TSS: refactoring candidate

| # | function | signal | shape of the fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TSS-R1** | `touch_set_score.main` | ~100 lines that branch on `two_judge` **five separate times** -- argument validation, loading, error printing, reconciliation, and the raw-count computation -- so the single-judge and dual-judge pipelines are interleaved rather than adjacent. That interleaving is what hides TSS2: the ambiguity map is discarded in one branch and preserved in the other, and nothing in either branch is near enough to the other to make the divergence visible. It is also the only place in the file that needed an inline comment during this sweep, and the comment exists solely to say what the *other* branch does. | Two named loaders -- `_load_single_judge_actuals(path)` and `_load_two_judge_actuals(p1, p2)` -- each returning the same `(actuals, location_set_disagreements, ambiguous, warnings, errors, description)` shape, with `main` reduced to validate-args, pick one, report, build. Each branch's behaviour then reads in one place, and the discarded ambiguity map becomes a visible omission in a return tuple rather than an `_` in a tuple unpack. |

## CB — from the comment sweep of `tools/corpus_build.py` (2026-08-12)

Editor pass on the builder of the verdict-equivalence corpus -- the corpus every TOO-45 refactor conclusion is checked against. 1064 -> 1007 lines, zero code-shape drift on this file, `ruff format`/`check` clean, full suite OK. Every claim below was executed against the live `logs/` tree and the committed goldens, not read off the module.

### CB: code-level defects (comments-only sweep could not fix these)

The paragraph most likely predates the sub-verdict extension that added the field and was never revisited. **This is the load-bearing kind**: it tells a future engineer that a mutation test for override detection *must* use the slow end-to-end corpus, when the fast one already pins it as a hard invariant. Narrowed to "Writes no corpus file", which is the property that actually matters and is true.

## CRC: comment sweep of `tools/change_role_classifier.py` (2026-08-12)

Editor pass on the role classifier, the largest remaining unswept file. 2628 -> 2485 lines, zero code-shape drift on this file, `ruff format`/`check` clean, suite 2733 OK. `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS` (a module-level list of strings spanning 197 lines) is **code** under the comments-only rule and was not touched; part of the evidence below concerns text inside it. Every claim below was executed, not read off the module.

### CRC: code-level defects (comments-only sweep could not fix these)

| # | defect | evidence | where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CRC1** | **A `yield` produces one opaque-hop record per enclosing scope AND per enclosing control-flow statement.** The second opaque-hop source scans for `Yield`/`YieldFrom` with `ast.walk(stmt)` over `_flatten_scope_statements`' output, which lists a statement and its nested bodies, and the scan also runs at module scope where the whole `def` is one statement. Executed on `def gen(meta):` / `if meta:` / `yield SUBJ` -- **3 opaque hops for one yield**, at lines 2 (the `def`), 3 (the `if`) and 4 (the `yield`). Control: the same nesting with `return SUBJ` gives **1**, because returns are matched by `isinstance(stmt, ast.Return)` on the flat list rather than by `ast.walk`. This inflates the number the tool prints as its fairness check on DECISION comparisons, in proportion to nesting depth, and only for generators. | `tools/change_role_classifier.py:_find_opaque_hops_in_scope` (the `for sub in ast.walk(stmt)` yield scan), `_analyze_tree`'s `for scope_node in idx.scope_nodes` loop |
| **CRC2** | **A file at HEAD whose bytes cannot be decoded is dropped from closure discovery with no entry in any bucket.** `_git_show` returns `None` for both "not found" and "undecodable", and the closure-fetch loop `continue`s on `None` without recording anything -- two lines above the `else:` branch that carefully records a *parse* failure for exactly this reason. Executed: a throwaway repo with `undecodable.py` (a `# -*- coding: no-such-codec-at-all -*-` cookie) committed in the base commit and untouched at head -- `_git_ls_tree_python_files` lists it, `_git_show` returns `None`, and it is named in **none** of `parse_failures`, `closure.parse_failures`, `removed_files`, `test_files` or `production_files`. Closure growth is silently incomplete. A comment now records the measurement at the `continue`; the fix is to distinguish "absent" from "undecodable". | `tools/change_role_classifier.py:run_git_diff` closure loop; `_git_show` |
| **CRC3** | **The same undecodable file, when it IS part of the diff, is reported as removed.** Same probe with the file added in the head commit: `new_source = _git_show(...)` returns `None` and the code appends to `removed_files` with the comment "deleted in head after all". Executed: `removed_files: ['undecodable.py']` for a file that exists at head. Wrong bucket rather than a silent drop, but it makes `removed_files` untrustworthy for exactly the encoding cases `_decode_python_bytes` exists to survive. | `tools/change_role_classifier.py:run_git_diff` |
| **CRC4** | **`_git_diff_entries` drops any two-path status that is not `R`.** `status = fields[0][0]`; the first branch requires `status == "R" and len(fields) == 3`, the second requires `len(fields) == 2`, and a line like `C100\tsrc/a.py\tsrc/b.py` matches neither and is discarded. Executed over the parse loop: `'C100\ta.py\tb.py'` -> DROPPED, `'T\ta.py'` -> kept, `'R090\ta.py\tb.py'` -> kept. Not reachable today because the command passes `-M` without `--find-copies`, so git emits no `C`; the field comment claiming any other status is "treated like `M`" was corrected to say what the parser actually does. | `tools/change_role_classifier.py:_git_diff_entries` |
| **CRC5** | **`CLOSURE_RULE_OWN_NAME` is dead code.** `consider()` returns early for any name already in `subject_set`, and the own-name rule only ever calls it with `d.name in subject_set`, so it can never admit a member. Executed on a tree containing `def wibble()` with subject `wibble`: rules admitted `['seed']`, own-name never appears. `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS` entry N8 already says this (frozen string, correct); the module docstring's rule enumeration used to list it as one of three growth rules and now lists the three that can actually fire. **Follow-on nobody has recorded: with own-name dead, every `ClassDef` entry in `_collect_whole_tree_facts`' `defs` table is inert.** Classes are appended with `returned_names=frozenset()`, so the returns-tracked rule skips them, and own-name was their only other route. Deleting the rule should therefore also drop the ClassDef collection, or the collection should be given a purpose. | `tools/change_role_classifier.py:_closure_growth_candidates`, `_collect_whole_tree_facts` |
| **CRC6** | **Frozen string cites a symbol that has never existed in `toolguard/`.** The `def-name` occurrence note says the body's real value may be read "through a differently-named constant or parameter (as `rule_entry.py`'s `allow_in_auto_mode` property does via `ALLOW_IN_AUTO_MODE_KEY`)". `grep -rn 'allow_in_auto_mode\|ALLOW_IN_AUTO_MODE' toolguard/` -> no match, and `git log -S allow_in_auto_mode -- toolguard/` -> **no commits at all**. The symbol lives only in the two throwaway canary trees and in test fixtures. Corroborates the existing finding at queue line 977. The `--tree ... --subject allow_in_auto_mode` usage example in the module docstring is fine -- it names a subject, not a symbol in this repo. | `tools/change_role_classifier.py:_analyze_tree`, the `def-name` `record(...)` note |

### CRC: false claims found and cut (all pre-existing)

- **Module docstring: "Never silently skip anything"** (CRC-F1) -- the same completeness headline found false in `touch_set_inventory.py` (TSI2) and `touch_set_score.py` (TSS), now a third time, in a third module, for a third reason. False here at minimum twice over: discovery drops every `EXCLUDED_DIR_NAMES` directory with no record, and CRC2 above is an executed case of a file vanishing from every bucket at once. Retitled **"Parse failures and unrecognised constructs"** and reduced to the two mechanisms that are actually true: a parse failure is named in `parse_failures` and excluded from the counts, and an unrecognised AST construct becomes an explicit UNCLASSIFIED occurrence.
- **Module docstring: "every member and every excluded-by-hop-limit candidate is printed with its hop, its rule, and why"** (CRC-F2) -- **false for the default text output on both halves.** Executed on a four-member closure: the text report prints `[seed]  wibble` with no hop, no rule and no reason, and prints non-seed members as `[hop 2] ALIAS -- assigned from tracked symbol 'THE_KEY' ...` with **no rule field**. Checked each rule name against the report text: `'string-literal-binding'`, `'name-alias'`, `'own-name'`, `'returns-tracked'` all absent (`'seed'` matches only the `[seed]` label). The `rule` field exists solely in `--json`. Replaced with "both the members and the candidates cut off by the limit are listed", which holds in both output modes.
- **Module docstring: the symbol-closure rule enumeration was wrong in both directions** (CRC-F3) -- it listed three rules: a name bound to the subject string, a `def`/`class` whose own name equals the subject, and a function returning a tracked symbol. It **omitted** `CLOSURE_RULE_NAME_ALIAS`, which demonstrably fires (executed: `ALIAS = KEY` joins at hop 2 with `rule='name-alias'`), and **included** `CLOSURE_RULE_OWN_NAME`, which cannot fire at all (CRC5). Now lists the three rules that can add a member. `compute_symbol_closure`'s "see `_closure_growth_candidates`'s three rules" was wrong under either reading -- four are implemented, three are live -- and is now a plain pointer with no count.
- **`compute_symbol_closure`: "Runs exactly *hop_limit* growth rounds"** (CRC-F4) -- false; the loop `break`s as soon as a round adds nothing. Now "at most *hop_limit* growth rounds, stopping early once a round adds nothing".
- **`discover_python_files`: "`rglob` with `recurse_symlinks=True` guards against symlink cycles internally (CPython's own glob implementation tracks visited directories), so no separate cycle guard is added here"** (CRC-F5) -- **a false hazard note, the most costly kind, and it is prescriptive**: it tells the next reader not to add a guard. Executed on Python 3.14.5 with `pkg/loop -> pkg`: `discover_python_files` returned **41 paths for one file**, `pkg/loop/loop/.../mod.py` at depths 1..40. It terminates, so something bounds the recursion, but nothing tracks visited directories and nothing deduplicates. The justification was deleted rather than replaced, and one measured clause kept in its place: nothing here deduplicates, so a looping directory symlink makes one file discoverable under many paths. Note that `KNOWN_LIMITATIONS`' last entry (frozen) contrasts the file-level symlink double-count with "the now-traversed symlinked DIRECTORY case", which reads as though the directory case is handled; it is traversed, not handled.
- **`Occurrence.kind`'s enumeration dropped one of the twelve kinds** (CRC-F6) -- the comment listed `name | attribute | arg | import-alias | keyword-name | except-name | match-as | match-star | match-mapping-rest | global | nonlocal`, eleven values. Executed against a snippet exercising every branch: the code produces twelve, the extra being **`def-name`** (recorded for a `def`/`class` whose own name equals the subject -- one of the more interesting kinds, since it carries WRITE and its own note). Added.
- **Module docstring: "verified exact against an independent AST oracle over 394 real occurrences across five subjects"** (CRC-F7) -- the 394 and the oracle are sourced (`reports/micro-canary-protocol.md:197`, `proposed-tickets/06-measurement-tools-keep-or-remove.md:24`, both phrased "394 occurrences against an AST oracle"). **"across five subjects" is sourced nowhere**: `grep -rn 'five subject\|5 subjects' toolguard-memories/` returns nothing, and neither source mentions a subject count. The unsourceable qualifier was cut and the sourced part kept, once, in the module docstring; the duplicate copy in `build_report`'s docstring was cut entirely.
- **`ast.Set()`'s inline comment cited a shape it got right and a reason it did not need** -- "found live via validation on real data (`rule_entry.py`'s `KNOWN_ENRICHMENT_KEYS = frozenset({...})`), not invented". The citation **resolves** (`toolguard/rule_entry.py:45`, `KNOWN_ENRICHMENT_KEYS = frozenset({ADDITIONAL_CONTEXT_KEY})`, exactly that shape) but is provenance rather than content; cut, keeping the mechanical reason a Set literal cannot be folded into the List/Tuple case.
