- **`test_compound_resolve_seam.py`** — the 30-line module docstring, reduced to one line. It was TDD provenance (*"Written BEFORE any production code ... was touched (Plan B step 0)"*), a per-class inventory, and **a claim that is now false**: *"The golden verdict corpus ... does NOT track `RuntimeVerdict.sub_matches` or `.overrides` content/order (see `test/verdict_corpus/fixture_loader.py`), so those two fields have no other pinning coverage across this refactor."* `fixture_loader.py` now carries `unit_verdict_to_dict`/`override_to_dict`, emits `"sub_matches"` and `"overrides"` into the golden schema, and defines `BREAKDOWN_FIELDS = ("sub_matches", "overrides")` as **HARD** rather than TRACKED — the corpus sub-verdict extension landed after the docstring was written. Also removed: **a citation to a function that no longer exists** — `TestAskFloorFallbackMatrix`'s docstring pointed at `:func:`~toolguard.compound._resolve_leaf_detailed``, deleted by this very refactor (`compound-cycle-implementation.md`: *"`_resolve_leaf_detailed` is deleted"*); five more class docstrings carrying judgment/plan-step numbers (*"Judgment R3"*, *"Judgment R5"*, *"Plan B step 0"*), reduced to the one-line claim each makes; `_make_config`'s "mirrors test_resolve.py's own helper" paragraph and Args/Returns, keeping the load-bearing sentence (index 0 is the most specific level); five Given/Then tails of ticket archaeology (*"TOO-45 R1e"*, *"TOO-45 R5"*, *"TOO-45 risk R1/R2"*) and cross-references to sibling test classes; and one inline comment.
- **`test_hook_eval.py`** — the module docstring's two-property inventory; the **9-line `# TOO-19:` block** above `_log_tmp_dir`, which explained why the module needs log-dir isolation and then pointed at `.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md`, where the same explanation lives (compressed to two lines rather than deleted, because the non-obvious fact — that this module also drives `main()` *without* `--eval` — is what makes the isolation necessary and is not inferable from the code); four class docstrings reduced to one line; six Given/Then tails of ticket provenance and rationale, the longest being the seven-line *"TOO-45 punch-list #04 fix pass M2: this used to print the deny decision to stderr ... this test previously pinned exactly that defect"*; and one inline comment.
- **`test_tools_log_harvest.py`** — the 12-line module docstring (a 5-item test inventory plus *"All tests use stdlib unittest with BDD Given/When/Then docstrings"*, a restatement of `.claude/rules/testing.md`) reduced to one line; a `# Minimal log content snippets for use in tests` banner over eight obviously-named fixtures; and **five trailing inline comments, every one a restatement of the assertion or the Then beside it** — `# should be skipped`, `# should be ignored` (x2), `# very old`, `# today only`, `# Should be in chronological order (oldest first)`.

### CS: rejected from the brief, with evidence

- **"Check whether this seam file pins extraction at all, or only resolution" — the answer is "yes, but only where it works".** Rejecting the implied "resolution only": `test_unit_for_peg_splits_a_plain_leaf_into_multiple_parts`, `test_unit_for_maps_ask_floor_leaf_to_inline_code_with_untruncated_stub` and `test_decompose_splits_undecidable_and_plain_units` call `_unit_for`/`decompose` directly and are the tests that die first under an extraction mutation (**constructed**: mapping an `ask_floor` leaf to `'plain'` fails six of the file's tests; using the full leaf text instead of the outer stub fails one). What is absent is any test of the three *bypasses* — recorded above as a coverage gap, not as a false claim in the file.
- **The brief's `test_hook_eval.py` lead about `hook.py` writing `status="refused"` for every toolguard deny.** No test in this file reaches `_log_non_allow_decision` or inspects a `LogRecord`: `--eval` never logs at all, and `TestEvalMatchesLiveHookUnderFallback._run_live` patches `toolguard.hook.log_command` out entirely and asserts only on `permissionDecision`. Nothing here to correct or flag; the defect stands on its own.
- **The brief's `EL1` lead (`log_crash` before `_emit_decision` in `main()`'s three `except` clauses).** Same finding: this file never drives `main()` into any of those handlers on the live path — `TestEvalModeMain`'s malformed-stdin case goes through `_run_eval_mode`'s own `except`, which calls neither `log_crash` nor `_report_crash_fault`. EL1 keeps its own row.
- **The brief's `_LOG_NAME_RE` lead.** Verified and **already covered**: `test_harvest_ignores_non_log_files` is the one test in the batch that a regex mutation kills — **constructed** by broadening `_LOG_NAME_RE` to `^toolguard-.*?(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})\.md$`, which fails exactly that test and nothing else. The `-conflict-` and `*.jsonl` cases the brief names are not in the fixture, but the `-error-`/`-warning-` pair pins the same branch. No defect.
- **The brief's `LH2` lead (multi-rule `Violated Rules` parsing into one mangled string).** Confirmed unexercised — `_SIMPLE_REFUSED` is the file's only refused fixture and carries a single rule — but this is the same gap `LH2` already records, so it is not re-filed here.
- **The brief's note about a citation to `test/unit/CLAUDE.md`.** Not present in any of these three files (`grep`ed all three), as the brief predicted. FL's nine sites remain the full list.
- **Live TDD instructions / RED-PHASE notes.** None in these three files. The nearest is the seam module docstring's *"Written BEFORE any production code ... was touched"*, which is TDD *provenance*, not an instruction, and is deleted above.
- **"Nothing substantive" as a per-file verdict.** Not claimed for any of the three. `test_hook_eval.py` came closest — its two headline findings are notes rather than table rows — but its prose carried the most ticket archaeology of the batch.
- **Missing docstrings, flagged not added.** `test_compound_resolve_seam.py:TestAskFloorFallbackMatrix._leaf` has none, which `~/.claude/rules/python.md` requires. Not added, per the no-added-prose constraint.

## MB — from the comment sweep of the ninth `test/unit/` batch (3 files, 2026-08-12)

Editor pass over `test_install_provenance.py`, `test_sandbox.py`, `test_multiline_bash.py`. 1,646 -> 1,549 lines (524->507, 553->516, 569->526). Comments and docstrings only; `--compare-against HEAD` reports zero drift on all three (still `toolguard/__init__.py` and `tools/architecture_fitness.py`, the known non-sweep changes), `ruff format`/`check` clean, suite **2733 OK** (unchanged). Every "constructed" below is a real whole-suite mutation run inside an `rsync` copy of the repo in the scratchpad — **the working tree was never mutated**; it was checked back with `git diff` and a `MUTANT` grep after each round. The copied tree carries 4 environment-artifact failures in its unmutated baseline (`test_architecture_fitness.TestSmokeAgainstRealTree` x2, `test_hook_error_reporter.TestOrdinaryInvocationStderr` x2); "detected by NONE" below means *no failure beyond those four*.

### MB: vacuous or self-satisfying tests (code changes, NOT made here)

| # | test | why the assertion cannot fail |
|---|---|---|
| **MB1** | `test_sandbox.py:TestSandboxHookEndToEnd.test_run_hook_returns_a_permission_decision` | **The observable is the whole JSON dump, and the failure text contains the asserted word** (a new variant of "the asserted value is what every alternative produces"). It asserts `assertIn("allow", json.dumps(result))` over the entire hook payload, `permissionDecisionReason` included. **Constructed**: ran the same `run_hook` under the same `ALLOW_LS` config with the command `curl example.com`, which the config does not allow. The payload is `{"permissionDecision": "ask", "permissionDecisionReason": "Compound command contains sub-command requiring approval: curl example.com (Command does not match any allow patterns; awaiting a decision (no_match_fallback=ask))"}` — `"allow" in payload` is **True**, and `_returncode` is **0**, so both of the test's assertions pass on a non-allow verdict. The rule not taking effect at all is exactly the failure this test exists to catch, and it cannot see it. **Fix shape**: `self.assertEqual(result["hookSpecificOutput"]["permissionDecision"], "allow")`. |
| **MB2** | `test_install_provenance.py:TestPythonpathShadowEntries.test_defaults_to_os_environ_when_env_not_given` | **The fixture cannot produce the distinguishing outcome**: the environ is patched empty (`clear=True`), so `()` is what *every* possible default returns. **Constructed**: replaced `env = os.environ` with `env = {}` in the throwaway copy and ran the **whole 2733-test suite** — **detected by NONE**. The `os.environ` default therefore has no coverage anywhere in the suite, and this test's Then (*"Then it reads PYTHONPATH from os.environ itself"*) names a mechanism nothing checks. **Fix shape**: `patch.dict(os.environ, {"PYTHONPATH": shadow_dir}, clear=True)` around a temp dir holding `toolguard/__init__.py`, then assert `pythonpath_shadow_entries() == (shadow_dir,)` with no argument passed. |
| **MB3** | `test_multiline_bash.py:TestMultilineRegressionGuards.test_hash_inside_argument_is_not_a_comment` | **The asserted value is what both branches produce.** With `allow=["echo:*"]` and no deny, `echo http://x#frag` resolves to `allow` whether or not the `#` is treated as a comment — stripping it leaves the leaf `echo http://x`, which still matches `echo:*`. **Constructed**: replaced `_strip_comments`'s word-boundary condition (`if i == 0 or text[i - 1] in " \t\n":`) with `if True:` and ran the whole suite — this test **passes**; the only extra failure anywhere is the golden-corpus test `test_verdict_corpus.test_no_sub_command_breakdown_changed`. **Fix shape**: make the fragment load-bearing — `_resolve("echo http://x#frag", ["echo:*"], ["[regex]#frag"])` should be `"deny"` (it becomes `"allow"` under the mutation), or assert the leaf text directly with the file's own `_leaf_texts` helper. |
| **MB4** | `test_sandbox.py:TestSandboxEvaluation.test_rewriting_the_config_changes_the_next_verdict` | **The second half of the Then is unpinned: the fixture defeats the cache without help from the mechanism.** The Then is *"Then the new verdict reflects the NEW config, not a cached parse"*, and the deleted rationale claimed the `lru_cache` "would silently return the stale verdict" without invalidation. `_parse_config_file_cached` is keyed on (mtime, size), and the two configs differ in size, so the second parse is a cache miss regardless. **Constructed**: replaced `Sandbox._invalidate_config_cache`'s body (`toolguard_config._parse_config_file_cached.cache_clear()`) with `pass`, disabling it at all three call sites, and ran the whole suite — **detected by NONE**. **Fix shape**: rewrite to a config of the *same byte length* inside one mtime tick (e.g. `allow = ["Bash(ls *)"]` -> `deny  = ["Bash(ls *)"]`, padded to equal length) so the key collides, or spy on `cache_clear` and assert it was called. |

### MB: mechanisms mutated that the whole suite failed to detect

- **`install_provenance.pythonpath_shadow_entries`'s `env = os.environ` default** — zero detection across 2,733 tests (MB2).
- **`sandbox.Sandbox._invalidate_config_cache`** — zero detection across 2,733 tests, with all three call sites (`write_config`, `write_rules_file`, `load_configuration`) neutralised at once (MB4).
- **`multiline._strip_comments`'s word-boundary guard** — detected only by the golden verdict corpus, not by the dedicated test that names it (MB3).
- **`multiline._find_heredocs_in_line`'s quote-parity skip** (`if before.count("'") % 2 == 1: continue`) — disabled it entirely; detected only by the golden verdict corpus. No dedicated test in `test_multiline_bash.py` or anywhere else in `test/unit/` covers it, in either direction: neither that it suppresses a genuinely quoted `<<`, nor the filed bypass where an escaped `'` hides every later heredoc.
- **Detected, for contrast**: `install_provenance._hash_py_files`'s `sorted(...)` — dropping it fails `test_identical_content_hashes_equal_regardless_of_creation_order`, which is doing exactly what its name says.

### MB: coverage gaps found while checking claims against the modules

- **None of the three filed `multiline.py` bypasses is pinned by any test in `test/unit/`.** Verified by grep over `test/unit/*.py`: no fixture combines `&&` with `<<` (the bash-family-token-steals-a-foreign-sink case, `multiline.py:186-190`), none has two heredocs on one line (`_process_heredocs`'s "rightmost delimiter claims the leftmost body", `:352-359`), and none puts an escaped `'` before a heredoc (`_find_heredocs_in_line`, `:145-151`). All three are documented as defects in the module's own docstrings and pinned nowhere — the same conclusion CS reached from the seam file, now confirmed from the other side. **Fix shape**: three characterization tests asserting the *current* leaf list for those inputs, marked as pinning a known bypass.
- **`_Tripwire._check`'s two defensive branches are untested here** — the `except TypeError: return` path (a non-path target, e.g. an open file descriptor) and the `bytes` path decode. `test_sandbox.py` is the only test module for `sandbox.py`, so these are uncovered outright.
- **`test_install_provenance.py` never states the constraint its four `TestGitSubtreeIsClean` tests depend on.** They patch `install_provenance.subprocess.run`, but the call now happens in `toolguard._git.run_git`; the patch works only because `install_provenance.subprocess` *is* the one global module object. Dropping the `import subprocess` from `install_provenance.py` — which nothing but a PEP 526 local annotation keeps ruff from flagging — breaks all four with `AttributeError` at patch time. The module says so (`install_provenance.py:34-38`); the tests do not, and per the no-added-prose constraint nothing was added.

### MB: left uncorrected and flagged, per the settled precedent

- **`test_sandbox.py:test_guard_logic_rejects_the_real_rules_directories_without_any_io`** — Given/When/Then says *"When it is asked about paths in the two REAL rules directories / Then it raises for both"*. The loop has **four** victims: the two rules directories plus `~/.claude/toolguard_hook.toml` and `~/.claude/settings.json`. "Both" is wrong and the subTest loop is what actually runs. Left verbatim.
- **`test_sandbox.py:TestSandboxIsolation`'s class docstring** — *"Isolation of the four config-discovery anchors"*, where `sandbox.py`'s own module docstring says *"Three config-discovery anchors are redirected inward ... and a tripwire catches whatever gets past them"*, and `.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md` counts three plus a fourth (the log dir) that this class does not test. Three counts, three documents. Left as found.
- **`test_sandbox.py:test_run_hook_returns_a_permission_decision`'s Then** — *"Then it returns a hook JSON payload carrying an allow decision"*. True of the payload, unverifiable by the assertion (MB1). Left.
- **`test_install_provenance.py:test_defaults_to_os_environ_when_env_not_given`'s Then** — *"Then it reads PYTHONPATH from os.environ itself"*. Nothing in the body can observe that (MB2). Left.
- **`test_multiline_bash.py:test_hash_inside_argument_is_not_a_comment`'s Then** — *"Then it is not treated as a comment and the command is ALLOWED"*. The second clause is asserted; the first is not, and is what the test is named for (MB3). Left.

### MB: near-vacuous, kept as notes

- **`test_sandbox.py:test_tripwire_allows_reading_outside_the_sandbox`** — `assertIn("test_tripwire_allows_reading_outside_the_sandbox", content)` where `content` is this file's own source, which contains that literal *because the assertion is written there*. Given the read returned at all, it cannot fail. The load-bearing assertion is the absence of a `SandboxEscapeError`, which is real; the `assertIn` only rules out an empty read.
- **`test_multiline_bash.py:TestForeignInterpreterVersionRobustness`'s first two tests** are the same path twice (`python3.14 -c` and `python3.99 -c`), and their docstrings claim different properties (*"a Python version not enumerated anywhere"* vs *"prefix recognition, not a maintained version list"*). Harmless redundancy, no fix proposed.
- **`test_multiline_bash.py:_resolve`** discards `reason` and `additional_context` into unused locals, and `_leaf` uses a bare `assert` in a repo whose testing rule bans pytest-style bare asserts. Code, not comments — untouched.

### MB: deleted (noise, no factual defect)

- **`test_multiline_bash.py`** — the 17-line module docstring, which was a **live RED-phase instruction** (*"These are the LOCKED fixtures (F1-F24) from the TOO-17 design, written TDD-first: most 'bypass' tests are expected to FAIL until the multi-line decomposition fix lands"*) plus a harness description and a design-principle paragraph, reduced to one line; **three class docstrings carrying the same stale RED-phase parenthetical** (*"(Expected to FAIL until the TOO-17 decomposition fix is implemented.)"*, *"(Bypass/ask tests expected to FAIL until implemented; body-not-parsed tests are guards.)"*, *"(Expected to FAIL until implemented.)"*) and a fourth beginning *"Already-passing guards:"*, all reduced to their one-line claim; **four Then tails asserting the pre-fix behaviour as if it were current** — *"(Currently denies because the mangled continuation matches no allow and fails closed.)"*, *"(Currently allowed because `bash -c:*` matches the whole opaque string.)"*, *"(Currently allowed as one opaque `diff ...` command -- a latent fail-open.)"*, *"(Currently denied because the comment+command blob matches no allow.)"* — every one of them false since the fix landed; `TestHeredocSentinelShape`'s *"(was untested)"* and its IR-refactor paragraph; and the second paragraphs of `_leaf_texts` and `_resolve`.
- **`test_sandbox.py`** — the module docstring's first paragraph (*"These tests are the proof that the sandbox's isolation is structural rather than incidental"*), keeping the second, which records why this module deliberately does not use `ConfigIsolationMixin` — `.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md` requires that exception to be stated in the file; the `#:` comment on `ALLOW_LS_ENRICHED`, which restated the literal and carried a phase number; **six trailing rationale paragraphs under Thens**, including *"toolguard caches parsed config files in an unbounded lru_cache; without an explicit invalidation this would silently return the stale verdict"* (MB4 shows the test does not demonstrate this), *"This is the question that was originally answered by editing live config (TOO-19, 2026-07-25). It is now one safe call."*, *"os.replace is the final step of toolguard's own atomic config write"*, *"This is the ~/.toolguard wipe class of failure (TOO-15, four occurrences)"*, and two *"Named explicitly rather than relying on Path.home() patching ... (TOO-19)"* variants; and `_fail_if_created`'s Args block.
- **`test_install_provenance.py`** — the 12-line module docstring (a coverage inventory, a "follows the established pattern in test_update_check.py" cross-reference, and a restatement of TOO-19's silence-on-uncertainty requirement) reduced to one line, and `_write_fake_package`'s four-line docstring reduced to one. Nothing else: **every test docstring in this file is already pure Given/When/Then** — the closest this batch comes to a "nothing substantive" verdict on prose, though it still holds MB2.

### MB: rejected from the brief, with evidence

- **My own hypothesis that `test_rules_file_in_toolguard_rules_dir_is_discovered` and its XDG twin are satisfied by the no-match fallback — rejected.** Measured in a sandbox: with `ALLOW_LS` and **no** rules file, `evaluate("Bash", "rsync a b").decision` is **`ask`**; with the rules file it is `deny`. The fallback does not supply the asserted outcome, so both tests can fail. Sound as written.
- **The brief's `test_sandbox.py` lead — that the module's claims propagate into project documentation (the CLAUDE.md "comment-only marker behaved identically to no marker" measurement).** Checked and **nothing to file**: that measurement was made *through* the sandbox CLI, and no test in this file asserts anything about comment handling or marker matching. The propagation risk is real but lands on `multiline._strip_comments` (MB3), not on the sandbox tests.
- **The brief's `test_install_provenance.py` lead, half-rejected.** The `subprocess` import is load-bearing and the tests would break without it — but not for the stated reason that they reach `install_provenance`'s own reference: `patch.object(install_provenance.subprocess, "run", ...)` mutates the single global `subprocess` module object, which is why it still works after the call moved into `toolguard._git.run_git`. The tests are therefore *correct* and *portable*; what is fragile is only the import's survival against an "unused import" cleanup. Recorded as a coverage gap above rather than as a test defect.
- **The brief's note about a citation to `test/unit/CLAUDE.md`.** Not present in any of these three files (`grep`ed all three). FL's nine sites remain the full list.
- **"Nothing substantive" as a per-file verdict.** Not claimed. `test_install_provenance.py` is the only candidate on prose grounds, and it carries MB2.

## DA — from the comment sweep of the tenth `test/unit/` batch (2 files, 2026-08-12)

Files: `test/unit/test_tools_danger.py` (605 -> 532), `test/unit/test_permissions.py` (639 -> 596). Method: `rsync` copy of the repo to a scratch directory outside it, one mutation at a time to the SOURCE under test, full 2,733-test suite per mutation, revert. The copy has a 4-failure environmental floor (`test_architecture_fitness`'s two real-repo smoke tests, `test_hook_error_reporter`'s two stderr tests); "undetected" below means zero failures above that floor.

### DA: mechanisms mutated that the whole suite failed to detect (9)

- **DA1 — `danger()`'s `findings.sort(key=lambda f: (-f.severity.value, f.tool, f.pattern))`, deleted outright.** Zero detection across 2,733 tests. The module's public contract ("sorted by descending severity, then tool, then pattern") is unpinned; `test_findings_sorted_critical_first` is the test that names it and cannot fail (see DA-vacuous below).
- **DA2 — `_audit_tool`'s `neutralized_by_takeover` guard in the BLANKET loop, neutralised.** Zero detection. This is the brief's known-vacuous `test_native_blanket_allow_not_flagged_when_takeover_on`, now confirmed by mutation rather than by reading: `config.permission_layers` strips `Bash(*)` from native layers upstream, so the guard never fires on any path a test constructs.
- **DA3 — the same guard in the CONTENT loop, neutralised.** Zero detection. `danger.py:517-519` already documents this as dead on every path in the repo; nothing tests it, so nothing would notice it becoming live-but-wrong either.
- **DA4 — `danger()` never reading takeover state from `config`** (replaced `config.takeover_mode()` with a hardcoded disabled `TakeoverConfig`). Zero detection. `test_takeover_read_from_config_when_not_provided` exists solely to assert this and does not.
- **DA5 — `_is_blanket_allow`'s GLOB branch (`ptype == GLOB and body in ("*", "**")`), deleted.** Zero detection. No test anywhere constructs a `[glob]*` or `[glob]**` allow. (Controls: deleting the DEFAULT `*` branch fails `test_live_blanket_allow_is_critical`; deleting the REGEX catch-all branch fails `test_blanket_pattern_not_double_reported`. So two of the three branches are pinned and the third is not.)
- **DA6 — `match_pattern`'s NATIVE semantics rebound to GLOB.** Zero detection across the whole suite — not by `test_permissions.py`'s two native tests, and (independently confirming the sibling batch's finding on `test_patterns.py`) not by anything else in 2,733 tests. The entire NATIVE pattern type is behaviourally indistinguishable from GLOB as far as this suite is concerned.
- **DA7 — `match_command`'s `**/<component>/**` special case extended to GLOB and REGEX** (i.e. deliberately breaking the "DEFAULT-only" property). Zero detection. Measured: `match_command("cat .env", ["[glob]**/.env/**"])` is `False` today and becomes `True` under the mutation, and no test observes it.
- **DA8 — `_is_secrets_exposure`'s REGEX branch forced to `return False`.** Zero detection. No test passes a `[regex]` body containing `.env`/`.ssh`/`id_rsa`/`.pem` to the secrets detector.
- **DA9 — `_is_destructive`'s REGEX branch forced to `return False`.** Zero detection. Same shape: the destructive detector's regex path has no test. (Control: the *arbitrary-exec* REGEX branch, forced to `False`, fails `test_regex_anchored_python_flagged` and `test_regex_anchored_node_ruby_perl_flagged` — so of the three predicates with a REGEX branch, exactly one is covered, and `test_tools_danger.py` has a whole class of anchored-regex exec tests while the two sibling predicates have none.)

### DA: tests whose assertions cannot fail

- **`test_tools_danger.py:test_takeover_read_from_config_when_not_provided`.** Then: *"Then danger() reads the takeover state from config automatically"*. The only assertion is `self.assertIsInstance(findings, list)`, and `danger()` is annotated `-> List[DangerFinding]` and returns a list on every path. **Constructed failure**: none exists short of an exception — DA4 severed the config read entirely and the test stayed green. **Fix shape**: build one config whose takeover state changes the verdict (a *toolguard-layer* `Bash(*)` plus `ignored_allow_patterns = ["Bash(*)"]`, which survives `permission_layers` because it is not native), call `danger(config)` with no `takeover=`, and assert the blanket finding is absent — then assert it is present when the same config has takeover disabled.
- **`test_tools_danger.py:test_native_blanket_allow_not_flagged_when_takeover_on`.** Confirmed vacuous by DA2. The fixture cannot produce the absence it asserts: `Bash(*)` is stripped from the native layer before `danger()` sees it, so `blanket_findings == []` for a reason unrelated to the `neutralized_by_takeover` guard the test is named for. The `self.assertTrue(takeover.enabled)` line is the only assertion in the test that can fail, and it tests `config.takeover_mode()`, not `danger()`. **Fix shape**: as above — put the blanket allow in a toolguard layer so it reaches the detector, and assert the guard suppresses it.
- **`test_tools_danger.py:test_findings_sorted_critical_first`.** Two independent defects. (a) Every assertion is inside `if len(findings) >= 2:` — the guard-clause shape; a change that reduced the finding count to one would make the test assert nothing and still pass. (b) The assertion is `assertGreaterEqual(findings[0].severity.value, findings[-1].severity.value)` on a 2-element list built from `allow=["uv run python:*", "rm -rf:*"]` — **insertion order already satisfies it**, because the CRITICAL pattern is listed first. DA1 deleted the sort and the test stayed green. **Fix shape**: reverse the fixture order (`["rm -rf:*", "uv run python:*"]`) so insertion order and sorted order differ, drop the guard, and assert the full severity sequence is non-increasing.
- **`test_permissions.py:test_env_special_handling_only_for_default`.** Then: *"Then the `**/.env/**` path-component special handling applies ONLY to DEFAULT patterns, while regex and glob must match the full command literally"*. All **four** assertions are `assertTrue(matched)`; there is no negative case anywhere in the body, so the test cannot distinguish "special handling is DEFAULT-only" from "special handling applies to every pattern type". DA7 proved it: extending the special case to GLOB and REGEX leaves it green. **Fix shape**: add `self.assertFalse(match_command("cat .env", ["[glob]**/.env/**"])[0])` and the REGEX equivalent — the only assertions that would carry the word "only".
- **`test_permissions.py:test_native_pattern_uses_native_semantics`.** Then: *"Then word-level NATIVE segment matching is used"*. DA6 shows every one of the three cases is byte-identical under GLOB: `match_pattern(NATIVE, "git * main", c) == match_pattern(GLOB, "git * main", c)` for `"git checkout main"` (True), `"git merge main"` (True) and `"git checkout develop"` (False). The test pins "some wildcard engine", not NATIVE. **Fix shape**: use a fixture where the two engines diverge — NATIVE's `*` crosses `/` and GLOB's does not, e.g. `[native]cat */x` against `cat a/b/x`.

### DA: near-vacuous, kept as a note

- **`test_tools_danger.py:test_finding_has_all_required_fields`** is seven `assertIsInstance` calls against a frozen dataclass whose every field is populated from a literal or an already-typed source in `_audit_tool`. It is a type-shape restatement of `DangerFinding`'s annotations; it can fail only if someone passes a wrongly-typed constructor argument, which is not a failure mode this code has. Left as found.
- **`test_tools_danger.py:test_safe_python_specific_script_not_flagged` is a strict duplicate of `test_uv_run_python_wildcard_flagged`** — both bodies reach `_ARBITRARY_EXEC_PREFIXES`'s `"uv run python"` entry, and no mutation can fail one without failing the other. Its name also asserts the opposite of what it tests (see below).

### DA: coverage gaps found while checking the brief's tickets against the modules

- **Ticket 18 (the shipped over-match) is not pinned by `test_permissions.py` in either direction.** Measured live: `match_command("git logfoo", ["git log:*"])` -> `(True, 'git log:*')`, and `git commit:*` matches `git commit-tree abc` and `git commitfoo -x`. Then I applied the **fix** (boundary-guard the pattern's last token: match `stem` or `stem + " *"` instead of gluing a bare `*`) and ran the whole suite: **20 tests fail, none of them in `test_permissions.py` or `test_patterns.py`** — they are all in the maintenance/consolidation/corpus tier (`test_tools_redundancy`, `test_tools_edit_proposal`, `test_tools_maintenance`, `test_hard_deny`, `test_verdict_corpus` and neighbours), i.e. the over-match is depended on incidentally by the rule-consolidation machinery, and the matcher's own unit tests say nothing about it. Worth knowing before ticket 18 is scheduled: the fix is not a localised change.
- **The one existing false-positive test only exercises the guarded shape.** `test_relative_path_no_false_positive` uses a **one-token** pattern (`./bin/precommit_checks.sh:*`), and one-token patterns *are* boundary-guarded by `matched_base` — measured: `match_command("bin/precommit_checks.shX", ["./bin/precommit_checks.sh:*"])` -> `(False, None)`. The asymmetry is on token N of an N>=2-token pattern, which no test in the file constructs.
- **Ticket 17 (NATIVE under-match) is untested here.** Measured: `[native]git * main` matches `"git checkout main"` but **not** `"git checkout main --force"`. `test_native_pattern_uses_native_semantics` uses only commands where the final segment lands at the end, so the under-match is invisible to it.
- **The GLOB/NATIVE bypass of the DEFAULT newline guard is untested in this file.** Measured on the leaf `"git status\nrm -rf /"`: DEFAULT `git *` -> `False` (guard fires), `[glob]git*` -> `True`, `[native]git *` -> `True`. `test_permissions.py` has no multi-line fixture at all, in either direction.
- **The "any colon triggers the `cmd:args` split" behaviour is untested here.** Measured: `match_command("curl http://ex.com/x", ["curl http://ex.com/*"])` -> `False`, the case `permissions.py:136-138` documents in its own comment. Nothing asserts it.
- **`_ids` in `test_tools_danger.py` is dead code** — defined at module level with a docstring, called nowhere in the file. Code, not comments; left in place.

The Then read *"Then... it may be flagged because it starts with 'uv run python'"* — a Then that hedges with "may" is not a Then. Deleted the trailing `NOTE:` paragraph and the three inline comments that argued for the behaviour; left the name and the hedged Then verbatim, not rewritten.

## EC — from the comment sweep of the eleventh `test/unit/` batch (2 files, 2026-08-12)

Files: `test/unit/test_env_config.py` (654 -> 640), `test/unit/test_toml_config.py` (718 -> 667). Suite unchanged at 2733 OK; zero code-shape drift.

### EC: mechanisms with NO detection anywhere in the suite (mutated in an out-of-repo copy, whole suite re-run)

- **`toolguard/path_utils.py::iter_dirs_upward` — the home-directory stop is undetected by all 2733 tests.** Mutation: `if current == home or current == current.parent:` -> `if current == current.parent:` (the walk now runs to `/`). Whole-suite result: identical to baseline (only the two `test_architecture_fitness.TestSmokeAgainstRealTree` errors that come from the copy not being a git repo). The test that exists precisely for this — `test_env_config.py::TestFindProjectRoot::test_stops_at_home_directory` — passes with the stop deleted, because its fixture has no project marker anywhere above it either, so `None` is what *both* the stopping and the non-stopping walk return. **Fix shape**: put a marker (e.g. `pyproject.toml`) in a directory ABOVE the mocked home and assert `None`; without the stop that marker would be found and the assertion would fail. Note the Then — *"Then the search stops at home and returns None"* — names a mechanism the assertion cannot observe.
- **`toolguard/env_config.py::load_env_file` — the whole-line `#` comment skip is undetected by all 2733 tests.** Mutation: `if not line or line.startswith("#"):` -> `if not line:`. Whole-suite result: identical to baseline. `test_load_handles_comments` survives because both comment lines in its fixture (`# Comment`, `# Another comment`) contain no `=` and are dropped one line later by the `if "=" not in line: continue` guard — the comment branch is never load-bearing for this fixture. Its `self.assertNotIn("# Comment", result)` is therefore an absence asserted against a fixture that cannot produce it. **Fix shape**: use a comment that WOULD parse as a pair — `# KEY=commented_out` — and assert both that `"# KEY"` is not a key and that `KEY` is absent.

### EC: tests whose assertions cannot fail (or cannot fail for the stated reason)

- **`test_env_config.py::TestFindProjectRoot::test_stops_at_home_directory`** — see above. Constructed: delete the home stop; test still passes.
- **`test_env_config.py::TestFindProjectRoot::test_prefers_git_over_pyproject`** — the name and the (now deleted) inline comment *"# Should find project_dir (with .git taking precedence)"* claim a precedence that **does not exist on this code path and cannot be observed by this fixture**. `env_config.find_project_root` calls `resolve_project_root(strict=True)`, whose docstring says "the nearest marker of any kind in `indicators`, **all equally trusted**"; `find_nearest_marker` returns the *directory*, never the marker. Both markers sit in the same directory, so every marker ordering yields `project_dir`. Constructed: reordered `CONFIG_ROOT_INDICATORS` to `("pyproject.toml",) + STRONG_PROJECT_ANCHORS` in the copy — `TestFindProjectRoot` still 9/9 OK. **Fix shape**: either delete the test as a duplicate of `test_finds_git_directory`, or make it a real precedence test against the *tiered* (`strict=False`) resolver with `.git` in a parent and `pyproject.toml` in a child — which is the only place a git-over-manifest preference actually lives.
- **`test_env_config.py::TestLoadEnvFile::test_load_handles_comments`** — the `assertNotIn` half; see above. The `assertEqual(result.get("KEY"), "value")` half is real.
- **`test_toml_config.py::TestErrorLog::test_warning_log_file_created`** — two of the four assertions are tautologies of the filter that produced the value:
  ```python
  warning_files = list(log_dir.glob("toolguard-warning-*.md"))
  self.assertEqual(len(warning_files), 1)
  self.assertTrue(warning_files[0].name.startswith("toolguard-warning-"))   # guaranteed by the glob
  self.assertTrue(warning_files[0].name.endswith(".md"))                    # guaranteed by the glob
  ```
  No implementation can make either fail once the `assertEqual` above has passed. **Fix shape**: drop both, or replace with an assertion on the *variable* part of the name (that the stem carries today's date, which is what the naming scheme actually promises).
- **`test_toml_config.py::TestTomlConfigLoader::test_load_invalid_toml_raises_error`** — `with self.assertRaises(Exception)`. Any exception at all passes, including one raised before parsing is reached. **Fix shape**: `assertRaises(tomllib.TOMLDecodeError)` — the docstring already says that is what is meant ("(TOML decode error)"), and the module is importable.
- **`test_toml_config.py::TestTomlConfigLoader::test_load_toml_with_missing_optional_sections`** — *lower confidence.* `assertNotIn("permissions", config)` against a fixture with no `[permissions]` table: `tomllib` cannot invent a key, so the only mutation that fails it is one that adds default-section synthesis to `load_config_file`, which today is a thin memoized `tomllib.load`. Real as a "no post-processing" contract check, vacuous as a parse test. Left as a judgement call for the owner.
- **`test_toml_config.py::TestValidatePermissions::test_empty_config_no_warnings`** — *lower confidence.* `validate_permissions({})` returns `()` under every implementation that does not crash: `governed_tools` defaults, `permissions` defaults to `{}`, the entry loop runs zero times. It is a crash smoke test wearing an assertion. Worth keeping only if labelled as such.

### EC: the cache-invalidation guarantee is narrower than the test class claims (code-level)

`TestLoadConfigFileCacheInvalidation`'s class docstring said a same-mtime-tick rewrite "**must still invalidate the cache**", generally. It does not. The cache key is `(path_str, file_format, st_mtime_ns, st_size)`, and the test's own fixture changes the file from 26 bytes to 79 — so it passes on **size**, not on anything mtime-related. Constructed the missing case (probe, out-of-repo): write `governed_tools = ["Bash"]\n`, read, rewrite to `governed_tools = ["Read"]\n` (identical 26-byte length), force the original `st_mtime_ns` back with `os.utime`, read again -> **the second read returns `['Bash']`: STALE**. The read-modify-write callers the docstring names (installer seed-self-perms, `migrate_permissions`) are exactly the ones that can produce a same-length rewrite (a pattern swapped for another of equal length). **Fix shape**: either add `st_ctime_ns`/a content hash to the key, or narrow the claim to "a size-changing rewrite" and add the same-size test as an expected-stale characterization. The Given (*"while its st_mtime_ns happens to be unchanged"*) names the wrong discriminator and should say so.

### EC: stale or overstated Given/When/Then, flagged not laundered

- `test_env_config.py::test_stops_at_home_directory` — *"Then the search stops at home and returns None"*. The stop is unobservable here (above).
- `test_env_config.py::test_prefers_git_over_pyproject` — the Then (*"Then that directory is returned as the root"*) is honest; the **test name** is the false claim, and the deleted inline comment made it explicit. Renaming is a code change, so left alone.
- `test_env_config.py::test_load_handles_comments` — *"Then the key is returned and comment lines are excluded"*. The second clause is not verified by any assertion that could fail.
- `test_toml_config.py::TestParseSourceTomlDiagnostics` (both tests) — *"Then it returns None (fail-open, unchanged)"*. **Accurate for this function specifically**, and worth recording because it reads as contradicting the established project fact that a parse failure fails *safe*: `_parse_source` delegates to `_parse_source_recording_failures` with a throwaway accumulator, so its failures never reach `Configuration.parse_failures` and never trigger the ASK-floor clamp; only the `_parse_source_recording_failures` call sites inside `load_configuration` fail safe. The parenthetical is true but reads as a general statement about toolguard, which it is not. Not edited.
- `test_toml_config.py::test_rewrite_within_same_mtime_tick_is_not_served_stale` — Given/Then name mtime as the thing being defeated; size is what actually defeats it (above).
