## HD — from the comment sweep of the twelfth `test/unit/` batch (2 files, 2026-08-12)

Files: `test/unit/test_hierarchical.py` (729 -> 670), `test/unit/test_hard_deny.py` (778 -> 716). Suite unchanged at 2733 OK; zero code-shape drift. Evidence below comes from 30 single-mutation whole-suite runs in an out-of-repo `rsync` copy (floor: the two `test_architecture_fitness.TestSmokeAgainstRealTree` errors the copy produces for not being a git repo).

### HD: mechanisms with NO detection anywhere in the suite (mutated in an out-of-repo copy, whole suite re-run)

- **`toolguard/permissions.py::check_hard_deny` — the `if not deny_patterns: return None` early return is undetected by all 2733 tests.** Mutation: `if False: return None`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. The guard is behaviourally dead: with it gone, `match_command(command, [], extended_syntax)` iterates an empty pattern list, returns `(False, None)`, and the very next line returns `None` anyway. The test that exists for it — `test_hard_deny.py::TestCheckHardDenyUnit::test_returns_none_when_no_deny_patterns` — passes with the guard deleted. **Fix shape**: nothing to fix in the test; either delete the guard (it buys one `for`-loop entry) or keep it and label the test a characterization of an optimization, not of behaviour.
- **`toolguard/config.py::Configuration._extract_tool_entries` — the non-list coercion `if not isinstance(raw_list, list): raw_list = []` is undetected by all 2733 tests.** Mutation: `raw_list = list(raw_list)`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. Every fixture that reaches the guard passes a *string* (`{"deny": "not-a-list"}`), which `list()` splits into single characters; none of them is `Bash(`-wrapped, so `entries_for_tool` drops them all and the pools come out empty under both implementations. The guard's real job — tolerating a non-iterable such as `{"deny": 123}`, where the unguarded form raises `TypeError` — is exercised by nothing.
- **`toolguard/config.py::Configuration.resolve_config_path` — the ABSOLUTE half of the `startswith("/") or startswith("~")` early return is undetected by all 2733 tests.** Mutation: drop the `/` disjunct, so an absolute path falls through to `str(root / raw_path)`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. `pathlib` makes the guard redundant for that case: `Path("/proj") / "/var/backups"` is `PosixPath('/var/backups')` — an absolute right operand resets the join — so the anchored and unanchored branches return the same string. Confirmed by a second mutation removing the guard **entirely**: only three tests fail, all of them the `~` case (`test_tilde_path_unchanged`, `test_tilde_pattern_left_untouched`, `test_hook.py::test_tilde_expansion`). The `~` half is genuinely load-bearing (`str(root / "~/backups")` is `<root>/~/backups`); the `/` half is decoration. **Fix shape**: nothing to fix in the tests unless the guard's intent is to survive a future change from `Path` joining to `os.path.join`/`f"{root}/{raw}"` — in which case say so; otherwise the two absolute-path tests are documentation, not verification, and should be labelled as such.
- **`toolguard/config.py::_pool_hard_deny_entries` — the `layer.is_native` argument threaded into `_extract_tool_entries` is dead on this path.** Mutation: pass the literal `False` for the `deny` list. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. It is structurally unreachable: `if layer.is_native: continue` five lines above skips every native layer, so the argument is always `False` in production. Not a defect — worth recording because `test_hard_deny_native_layer_structured_entry_contributes_nothing`'s Then (*"native layers are skipped wholesale, before the structured entry is ever normalized"*) is the only place the redundancy is written down, and it reads as a description of two guards rather than one.

### HD: tests whose assertions cannot fail (or cannot fail for the stated reason)

- **`test_hierarchical.py::TestAnchorFilePattern::test_relative_pattern_does_not_match_same_name_outside_project`** — its Then names project-root anchoring as the mechanism (*"the anchored pattern only matches inside the project, pinning that relative patterns are no longer matched as authored"*), and the assertion is `assertEqual(result.decision, "ask")`. Constructed: mutate `Configuration.resolve_config_path` to `return raw_path`, deleting anchoring entirely — **13 tests fail suite-wide, 9 of them in these two files, and this is not one of them.** With the pattern left as authored (`src/**`), the absolute outside path `<home>/a/src/x.py` does not glob-match either, so `ask` is what *both* implementations produce. Across all 29 mutations this test never fired once. **Fix shape**: make it a paired control — assert in the same test that the *inside* path (`<project>/src/x.py`) is `allow` and the outside path is `ask`; only the pair distinguishes "anchored" from "matches nothing at all". (The inside leg exists today in `TestRelativeFilePathPatterns`, a different class, which is why the negative control drifted loose.)
- **`test_hard_deny.py::TestCheckHardDenyUnit::test_returns_none_when_no_deny_patterns`** — `assertIsNone(check_hard_deny("curl x", [], []))`. The asserted value is what every alternative implementation produces; see the first bullet above. Confirmed zero detection.
- **`test_hard_deny.py::TestHardDenyStructuredEntries::test_hard_deny_non_list_deny_value_tolerated`** — `assertEqual(config.hard_deny("Bash"), ((), ()))` against `{"deny": "not-a-list"}`. The fixture provides an alternative route to the outcome: the guard's removal still yields empty pools because a string's characters are not `Bash(`-wrapped. The Then says *"it is tolerated as empty rather than raising"* — **raising is the thing that cannot happen with this fixture**. **Fix shape**: use `{"deny": 123}`; the unguarded form raises `TypeError` on `list(123)`, so the assertion becomes load-bearing for exactly the claim the Then makes.
- **`test_hard_deny.py::TestHardDenyCommand::test_hard_deny_not_weakened_by_broken_config_ask_floor`** — the When says *"(hard_deny checked first, same as resolve_bash_permission_detailed does in production)"*, but the test never calls `resolve_bash_permission_detailed`: `TestHardDenyCommand._resolve` re-implements the hard-deny-first ordering itself in a local `_resolve_one` closure, so the production ordering is outside the assertion's reach. Constructed: in `resolve.py::resolve_bash_permission_detailed._decide`, run the cascade first and let an `ask` verdict win over the hard-deny pool — **13 tests fail, across `test_resolve`, `test_compound_resolve_seam`, `test_tools_installer` and `test_verdict_corpus`, and not one of them is in `test_hard_deny.py`.** The file that owns the mechanism detects nothing about it. **Fix shape**: have `_resolve` call `resolve_bash_permission_detailed(command, config, True, *config.hard_deny("Bash"))` instead of re-implementing the ordering; that helper backs all ten tests in the class, so the whole class would move onto production code in one edit.
- **`test_hierarchical.py::TestProjectRootRelativePaths::test_absolute_path_unchanged` and `TestAnchorFilePattern::test_absolute_pattern_left_untouched`** — both assert an absolute path/pattern comes back unchanged, and both survive deleting the guard that is supposed to make that true, because `Path.__truediv__` already discards the left operand for an absolute right one (above). The asserted value is what every alternative produces. **Fix shape**: assert on the guard's observable consequence somewhere it differs — e.g. that a *non-normalised* absolute path (`//etc/x`, or one with a trailing slash) is byte-identical on the way out, which `str(root / raw)` would not guarantee under a non-`pathlib` join.
- **`test_hard_deny.py::TestHardDenyStructuredEntries::test_hard_deny_and_hard_deny_entries_are_index_aligned`** — *lower confidence, the length half only.* `assertEqual(len(deny), len(deny_entries))` and its `allow` twin compare two observables of the *same* `_pool_hard_deny_entries` call chain (`hard_deny()` is literally `tuple(e.stripped_pattern for e in entries)` over it), so the lengths cannot diverge under any implementation that keeps `hard_deny()` a per-entry map. The zip assertions underneath are real — mutating `hard_deny()` to return `entry.pattern` fails them (and 42 other tests).

### HD: `TestHardDenyFilePath._build_config` is dead code (code-level)

`test_hard_deny.py`'s `TestHardDenyFilePath._build_config` (5 parameters, writes a hard_deny section at a chosen level plus a project-level allow) is called by nothing. Grepped repo-wide: the only other hits are two `test/verdict_corpus/*.jsonl` fixture rows that happen to quote a `grep` command containing the name. All four tests in the class write their fixtures inline instead. It also interpolates its `project_allow` parameter into a TOML f-string as a raw Python repr, which no caller ever exercised. Test bodies are code for this sweep, so flagged not deleted. **Fix shape**: delete it.

### HD: stale or overstated Given/When/Then, flagged not laundered

- `test_hierarchical.py` module docstring (deleted) claimed: *"``permission_resolution.resolve_command_permission`` evaluates levels most-specific first; the first level that matches anything decides (deny-first within a level); **no match anywhere => fail-closed deny**."* The last clause has been false since TOO-15, and the file's own `test_no_match_anywhere_falls_through_to_no_match_fallback` asserts `"ask"` 250 lines below it — a module docstring contradicting a test in the same file.
- `test_hierarchical.py::test_relative_pattern_does_not_match_same_name_outside_project` — *"the anchored pattern only matches inside the project"*. Names a mechanism no assertion in the test can observe (above). Not rewritten.
- `test_hard_deny.py::test_hard_deny_not_weakened_by_broken_config_ask_floor` — *"same as resolve_bash_permission_detailed does in production"*. Describes production code the test does not call (above). Not rewritten.
- `test_hard_deny.py::TestHardDenyStructuredEntries` — two Thens carry commit-message text: *"it is no longer silently dropped (the bug this increment fixes)"* and *"the same silent-drop bug fixed for deny also applies to the allow carve-out"*. Kept verbatim under the sweep's keep-the-Given/When/Then rule; recorded because a Then that narrates a past fix is change history in the one place git already covers.
- `test_hard_deny.py::_IsolatedEnvTestCase` docstring (deleted) said *"Mirrors the pattern in test_hierarchical.py"*. `test_hierarchical.py` uses `ConfigIsolationMixin` for every class but one; nothing there mirrors this base class any more.
- `test_hierarchical.py::TestMigrationIgnoresEnvOverride` docstring (deleted) said *"These tests set CLAUDE_SETTINGS_PATH themselves ... to prove the override is ignored"*. True of the first test; the second proves the override **is** honoured by default.

### HD: deleted (noise, no factual defect)

- **`test_hierarchical.py`** — the 16-line module docstring (mechanism inventory + the false fail-closed claim + a restatement of the project's own Given/When/Then convention) reduced to one line; **five multi-line helper/class docstrings** reduced to one line each (`_config`, `_resolve`, `_config_with_backup_dir`, `_resolve_read`, `TestMigrationIgnoresEnvOverride`), the `_resolve` one being *"so existing Given/When/Then assertions remain unchanged"* — a note about an edit, not about the helper; **nine inline comments**, all restatements of the line below (`# project (.claude under 'proj') is most specific`, `# intermediate ancestor 'a' is between project and user`, `# ~ is least specific (largest index)`, `# User level is least specific (largest specificity index).`, `# A .claude ABOVE home that must never be collected.`, `# child: no match; middle: deny; parent: allow.`, `# Ensure project always has a hook file so the toggle reads as default-on.`, `# A path named src/x.py but OUTSIDE the project root (in an ancestor).`, `# Unrelated project that CLAUDE_SETTINGS_PATH points at.`, `# The project actually being migrated/analysed.`, `# Adjacent toolguard_hook with a distinctive permission.`).
- **`test_hierarchical.py::test_walk_stops_at_home`'s six-line hand-rolled-isolation comment was trimmed, not deleted** — `.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md` *mandates* a one-line "why" on any hand-rolled `TemporaryDirectory` + `patch` exception and cites this exact site as "the one existing commented precedent". Kept as `# Hand-rolled: ConfigIsolationMixin cannot represent a .claude ABOVE home.`; the second half (about clearing `CLAUDE_SETTINGS_PATH`) went.
- **`test_hard_deny.py`** — the 19-line module docstring (a five-bullet restatement of `[hard_deny]` semantics that `Configuration.hard_deny`'s own docstring already carries, plus the convention boilerplate) reduced to one line; **four class/helper docstrings** reduced to one line each (`_IsolatedEnvTestCase`, `_layer`, `TestHardDenyStructuredEntries` — an eight-line *"TOO-19 Phase 0a, increment 3"* narrative — `TestHardDenyCommand._resolve`, `TestHardDenyFilePath._build_config`); **one seven-line inline comment** inside `_resolve_one` narrating that `check_hard_deny` returns a `LevelMatch` *"as of TOO-45 R1f (a bare tuple before that, TOO-19 code review m3)"* — three ticket references explaining a two-line closure; **three trailing labels** on `_layer(...)` rows (`# project: allows`, `# intermediate: nothing`, `# user: hard deny`); and **three comment blocks** inside `test_edit_hard_deny_relative_pattern_anchors_to_project_root` (nine lines) that re-narrate the Then immediately above them, one assertion at a time.

### HD: rejected from the brief, with evidence

- **`test_hard_deny.py` does NOT depend on the ticket-18 over-match — the brief's central claim for this file is a name collision.** Applied the fix in the copy (`full_cmd_pattern = bc + cmd_pattern[len(base_cmd):] + "*"` -> match `stem` or `stem + " *"`) and ran the whole suite: 20 tests fail above the floor — 1 in `test_api`, 10 in `test_tools_consolidate`, 9 in `test_tools_maintenance`. **Zero in `test/unit/test_hard_deny.py` or `test/unit/test_hierarchical.py`**; running both modules alone against the fix gives 63 tests OK. The `test_hard_deny` in the earlier note at line 1747 is `test.unit.test_api.TestDecideSimpleBash.**test_hard_deny**_carve_out_exempts_command` — a test *named* hard_deny in another file. **Mechanism**: every pattern these two files use (`curl *`, `rm -rf *`, `git *`, `ls *`, `Read(**/.env)`, `Write(**)`, `Edit(secrets/**)`) is colon-free, so `match_command` takes the whole-string `fnmatch` branch at `permissions.py:170-173`; the ticket-18 asymmetry lives entirely inside the `":" in pattern_normalized` branch at `:163`. Line 1747's failing-file list should be corrected.
- **`config.permission_layers` stripping ignored-allow patterns, and `config_access.per_layer_rules` hiding native `permissions.ask` rules** — neither is reachable from either file. Grepped: no `takeover`, no `per_layer_rules`, no `permission_layers`, and **not one `permissions.ask` pattern anywhere in either file**. `test_hierarchical.py` reaches the cascade only through `resolve_command_permission`, and every layer it builds is `source_type="toolguard_hook"`. Nothing here is pinned or unpinned by those two findings.
- **The "duplicate spanning two layers at the same specificity is invisible to both redundancy engines" lead** — no redundancy/consolidation engine is exercised by either file.
- **`self_integrity`'s `^`-anchored hard-deny regexes** — `test_hard_deny.py` never loads self-integrity rules; that surface belongs to `test_self_integrity.py`.
- **The `test/unit/CLAUDE.md` citation lead** — grepped both files, not present.
- **Live TDD instructions, RED-phase asides, stale line-number citations, a module docstring inventorying deleted tests, a test name contradicting its own assertion** — none in either file.
- **"Nothing substantive" as a per-file verdict** — not claimed for either file; each carries at least one undetected mechanism and one assertion that cannot fail for its stated reason.

## RU — from the comment sweep of the thirteenth `test/unit/` batch (2 files, 2026-08-12)

Files: `test/unit/test_tools_replay.py` (738 -> 672), `test/unit/test_update_check.py` (794 -> 764). Suite unchanged at 2733 OK; zero code-shape drift. Evidence below comes from **42 single-mutation whole-suite runs** in an out-of-repo `rsync` copy (floor: the two `test_architecture_fitness.TestSmokeAgainstRealTree` errors the copy produces for not being a git repo), plus a read-only fixture probe against a second copy. **23 of the 42 mutations were undetected by all 2733 tests.**

### RU: mechanisms with NO detection anywhere in the suite (mutated in an out-of-repo copy, whole suite re-run)

- **`toolguard/tools/replay.py::classify_change` — the unknown-verdict default is undetected.** Mutation: `_STRICTNESS.get(verdict_x, 0)` -> `.get(verdict_x, 9)` on both operands. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. `classify_change`'s own docstring documents this default at length (*"A verdict string outside `_STRICTNESS` ranks as `allow` (0). Since nothing ranks below 0, an unrecognised verdict_a can never yield 'broadened'"*), and nothing anywhere passes it a verdict outside `{allow, ask, deny}`. **Fix shape**: add `classify_change("bogus", "allow")` and `classify_change("allow", "bogus")` to `TestClassifyChange` and assert what the docstring claims — noting that the honest answer for the first is `unchanged`, which is the docstring's point but reads as a false negative.
- **`toolguard/tools/replay.py::_verdict_matches_status` — three of its four behaviours are undetected.** (a) `return False` for every unrecognised status -> `return True`: undetected. (b) `status.upper()` -> `status`: undetected (no fixture uses a lowercase status, so case-folding is decoration). (c) the `EXECUTED` arm `verdict == "allow"` -> `verdict != "deny"`: undetected. Only the `REFUSED` arm's `ask` disjunct is pinned, by `test_replay_single_matches_observed_correctly`. **This is the arm the brief cares about**: `hook.py` writes `status="ask"` for every ask verdict, and (a) is exactly the branch that turns every such entry's `matches_observed` into `False`. **Fix shape**: one table-driven test over the (verdict, status) grid including `("ask", "ASK")`, `("allow", "executed")` and `("allow", "ERROR")`.
- **`toolguard/tools/replay.py::replay_single` — `matches_observed` need not be computed at all.** Mutation: `matches = _verdict_matches_status(...)` -> `matches = True`. Undetected. Both assertions in the only test that reads the field are `assertTrue`, so a hardwired `True` satisfies them; there is **no negative case anywhere**. **Fix shape**: add one entry whose verdict does not corroborate its status (e.g. `git status` logged `REFUSED`) and assert `assertFalse`.
- **`toolguard/tools/replay.py::replay` — corpus order is not pinned.** Mutation: `result.diffs.append(diff)` -> `result.diffs.insert(0, diff)`. Undetected across all 2733 tests, though `ReplayDiff.diffs` and `replay`'s `Returns:` both promise *"in corpus order"*. Every test that indexes `diffs` either filters to a single-element subset first or reads a one-element bucket. (`replay_single`'s ordering **is** pinned, by `results[0]`/`results[1]`.)
- **`toolguard/install_update.py::run_upgrade` — its return value is not pinned.** Mutation: `return result.returncode` -> `return 0`. Undetected. The only test that exercises it (`TestRunUpgrade::test_returns_subprocess_exit_code`) stubs `returncode=0` and asserts `0`. **Fix shape**: stub `returncode=3` and assert `3`; the current fixture makes the asserted value what every alternative produces.
- **`toolguard/install_update.py::_check_git` — it need not propagate `run_upgrade`'s exit code.** Mutation: `return run_upgrade(dist_name)` -> `run_upgrade(dist_name); return EXIT_UP_TO_DATE`. Undetected. `test_git_upgrade_flag_runs_upgrade_only_when_behind` mocks `run_upgrade` to return `0` and asserts `code == 0` — and `EXIT_UP_TO_DATE` is also `0`, so the two are indistinguishable. Together with the previous bullet, **nothing in the suite pins the documented departure from the 0/1/2 contract under `--upgrade`**. **Fix shape**: mock `run_upgrade` to return `7`.
- **`toolguard/install_update.py::_check_local` — re-reading HEAD instead of trusting `info.installed_commit` is undetected.** Mutation: `installed = local_repo_head(repo)` -> `installed = info.installed_commit`. Undetected, including by `test_local_head_unreadable_returns_unknown`, which was predicted to catch it: that test patches `local_repo_head` to `None` but leaves `local_remote_head` **unpatched**, so under the mutation the real `local_remote_head(Path("/home/user/toolguard"))` fails on a nonexistent path and returns `None`, producing `EXIT_UNKNOWN` by a different route. The test's two assertions (`code == EXIT_UNKNOWN`, `len(err) > 0`) cannot distinguish "HEAD unreadable" from "remote unreachable" — and `test_local_remote_unreachable_returns_unknown` asserts the same two things. **Fix shape**: patch `local_remote_head` in the HEAD-unreadable test and assert on the distinguishing text (`"Could not read HEAD"` vs `"Could not reach"`), which the deleted comment *"Error message should mention the checkout"* already said was the intent.
- **All three `test_nonzero_exit_returns_none` tests assert against an unreachable guard.** Mutations dropping `result.returncode != 0` from `remote_head`, `local_remote_head` and `local_repo_head` are **all three undetected**. Every one of those fixtures pairs `returncode=128` with `stdout=""`, and the empty-output path already returns `None`; the returncode check is never the reason. In `TestRemoteHead` this makes `test_nonzero_exit_returns_none` and `test_empty_output_returns_none` two observables of the same route. **Fix shape**: give the nonzero-exit fixtures a plausible `stdout` (e.g. `"deadbeef0123\tHEAD\n"`) so only the returncode check can produce the `None`.
- **`toolguard/install_update.py::local_repo_head`'s empty-sha guard is undetected.** Mutation: `return sha if sha else None` -> `return sha`. Undetected, for the same reason: no fixture gives it `returncode=0` with empty output.
- **`detect_install`'s `editable` handling is undetected in both branches.** (a) `dir_info.get("editable", False)` -> `.get("editable", True)`: undetected — every `dir_info` fixture states `editable` explicitly, so the default is never taken. (b) the `__file__` walk-up branch's hardcoded `editable=False` -> `True`: undetected — `test_no_direct_url_falls_back_to_file_walk_up` asserts `kind`, `repo_path` and `installed_commit`, never `editable`. **Fix shape**: a `dir_info` fixture with no `editable` key, and an `assertFalse(info.editable)` in the walk-up test.
- **`_read_direct_url_json`'s empty-text guard is undetected.** Mutation: `if not raw` -> `if raw is None`. Undetected; no fixture returns `""`. Related and also untested: the function can return a **non-dict** (`json.loads('"x"')`, `json.loads('123')`), which crashes both `detect_install` and `installed_origin` on `data.get`. There is no test for either.
- **`installed_origin`'s two remaining guards are undetected.** (a) `if not commit_id: return None` deleted: undetected. (b) `isinstance(vcs_info, dict)` -> `vcs_info is None`: undetected. All four `TestInstalledOrigin` fixtures either have a complete `vcs_info` or none at all — nothing supplies `vcs_info` without `commit_id`, and nothing supplies a non-dict `vcs_info`. Combined with the fifth test (below) that is **five tests over a function with zero non-test callers**, none of which reaches a partial-metadata case.
- **`detect_install`'s GIT branch does not need a url.** Mutation: `if commit_id and url:` -> `if commit_id:`. Undetected.
- **`detect_install`'s `file://` scheme checks are undetected, on both sides.** (a) `url.startswith("file://")` dropped from the `dir_info` test: undetected. (b) `_file_url_to_path`'s `if parsed.scheme != "file": return None` deleted: undetected. Every `dir_info` fixture already uses a `file://` URL, so neither check is ever the thing that decides.
- **`is_git_worktree` need not read git's output.** Mutation: `returncode == 0 and stdout.strip() == "true"` -> `returncode == 0`. Undetected — `is_git_worktree` is patched wholesale in every test that reaches it, and nothing tests it directly.
- **`_walk_up_to_git_root` never walks.** Mutation: replace the whole ascent (`parent = current.parent; ... current = parent`) with `return None`, so it checks only the starting directory. Undetected — the function is patched wholesale in the one test that reaches it.
- **`distribution_name` need not read the metadata.** Mutation: return `_DEFAULT_DIST_NAME` unconditionally. Undetected; it is patched in both tests that care about it.
- **`toolguard/_git.py::run_git` — its `timeout` is not pinned by any test in the suite.** Mutation: delete `timeout=timeout` from the `subprocess.run` call. Undetected across all 2733 tests. Also undetected: deleting `env["GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT"] = "0"` from `remote_head`. Both are the subject of an explicit safety claim in `install_update.py`'s module docstring (*"No git subprocess here can hang: every one runs under run_git's timeout, and the two ls-remote calls set GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0"*) — a documented guarantee with no test behind it. Cross-file: the owner is `test_git_helper.py`, not this batch.

### RU: tests whose assertions cannot fail

- **`test_tools_replay.py::TestReplayUnchanged::test_identical_configs_produce_all_unchanged` — three of its four assertions are tautologies of `config_a is config_b`.** `replay` calls `decide` twice with identical arguments, so `classify_change(x, x)` is `"unchanged"` for every entry by construction; `broadened_count == 0`, `tightened_count == 0` and `unchanged_count == len(corpus)` cannot fail for any config, any rule set, or any bug in `decide`. **Constructed**: replaying the same corpus with the entire `permissions` block deleted (`content = {}`) gives `total=4 broadened=0 tightened=0 unchanged=4` — identical to the authored fixture, whose verdicts are `['allow','allow','allow','ask']` versus the gutted `['ask','ask','ask','ask']`. The four rule strings are inert. Only `total_count == len(corpus)` and the `unchanged_count` **counter** are pinned (mutations M5 and M10 fire here). **Fix shape**: this test's real content is `classify_change(x, x) == "unchanged"`, already covered by `test_unchanged_same_verdict`; to be worth its fixture it needs config B to differ from config A in a way the corpus cannot see — which is precisely the module docstring's *"a widening stays invisible unless a command it newly admits is in the corpus"* claim, and nothing tests that either.
- **`test_tools_replay.py::TestReplayUnchanged::test_identical_file_tool_configs_produce_unchanged` — same shape, and the file-tool mechanism it names is wholly unobservable.** **Constructed**: replacing `Read([glob]{home}/projects/**)` with `Read([glob]/nowhere/zzz/**)` — a pattern matching neither corpus entry — gives `total=2 broadened=0 tightened=0`, identical to the authored fixture; so does deleting the permissions block entirely. Nothing distinguishes a working `[glob]` prefix from a broken one. The Then also promises *"all entries are classified as 'unchanged'"*, which **no assertion checks** (`unchanged_count` is never read; only `total_count`, `broadened_count` and `tightened_count` are). **Fix shape**: assert the per-entry verdicts (`diff.diffs[0].decision_a.decision == "allow"`, `[1] == "ask"`); that is the only thing in this test that the glob can move.
- **`test_update_check.py::TestRemoteHead::test_nonzero_exit_returns_none`, `TestLocalRemoteHead::test_nonzero_exit_returns_none`, `TestLocalRepoHead::test_nonzero_exit_returns_none`** — all three: the fixture provides an alternative route to the outcome (empty `stdout`), confirmed by three undetected mutations. See above for the fix shape.
- **`test_update_check.py::TestRunUpgrade::test_returns_subprocess_exit_code`** — the asserted value (`0`) is what every alternative produces. Confirmed undetected.
- **`test_update_check.py::TestCheck::test_git_upgrade_flag_runs_upgrade_only_when_behind`** — the "its exit code is returned" half cannot fail, because the mocked code and `EXIT_UP_TO_DATE` are both `0`. Confirmed undetected. (The `mock_upgrade.assert_called_once()` half is real.)
- **`test_update_check.py::TestCheck::test_local_head_unreadable_returns_unknown`** — cannot fail for its stated reason; the fixture reaches `EXIT_UNKNOWN` by the remote-unreachable route as well. Confirmed undetected, and byte-identical in its assertions to `test_local_remote_unreachable_returns_unknown`.
- **`test_update_check.py::TestInstalledOriginLegacyBehavior::test_installed_origin_still_returns_none_for_local_install`** — a fifth test over `installed_origin` differing from `TestInstalledOrigin::test_non_git_install_without_vcs_info_returns_none` only in the `file://` path inside the payload string; same code path, same assertion. **Fix shape**: delete it, or repoint it at the untested partial-metadata cases (`vcs_info` without `commit_id`, non-dict `vcs_info`, non-dict parsed JSON).
- *Lower confidence, weak rather than dead*: `test_replay_single_returns_decisions_for_all_entries` asserts only `len(results) == 3` while its Then promises *"a list of 3 SingleDecision records"*; the record type is pinned only incidentally, by the next test's attribute access.

### RU: stale or wrong Given/When/Then, flagged not laundered

- `test_tools_replay.py` module docstring (deleted) opened *"Unit tests for toolguard.tools.replay - **THE KEYSTONE**"* — a headline deleted from `replay.py` itself — and asserted of the alembic test that a naive consolidation *"would change alembic commands from **'ask' -> 'allow'**, which replay catches as broadened"*. Both configs in that test set `no_match_fallback = "deny"` and neither contains an `ask` rule; the transition asserted is `deny -> allow`.
- `test_tools_replay.py::test_alembic_landmine_broadening_detected` — the same falsehood in the test's own Given and Then, kept verbatim: *"Given config A where 'uv run alembic <sub>:*' specific commands are in allow **and 'uv run alembic:\*' is in ask** (the alembic landmine pattern)"* and *"Then the **previously-ask** entries are classified as 'broadened' (**ask -> allow**)"*. The assertions check `decision_a.decision == "deny"`. The 19-line comment block that was deleted from the body said so explicitly (*"we simulate this by putting general alembic in deny"*), so the docstring and the body have disagreed since the test was written. **Not rewritten.**
- `test_tools_replay.py::test_broadened_allow_to_deny_is_wrong_direction` — the **name** says allow -> deny; the docstring and the assertion are `classify_change("deny", "allow")`. Renaming is a code change, so left alone.
- `test_tools_replay.py::test_tightened_deny_to_ask` — the **name** says deny -> ask; the docstring and the assertion are `classify_change("ask", "deny")`. Same treatment.
- `test_tools_replay.py::test_removing_allow_rule_tightens_decisions` — its Then cites *"replay's documented allow > ask > deny ordering"*. `replay.py` documents the strictness order as `deny > ask > allow`; the docstring states it inverted. The classification asserted (`tightened`) is correct either way.
- `test_tools_replay.py::test_replay_single_returns_decisions_for_all_entries` — Then names `SingleDecision` records, assertions check only the length (above).
- `test_update_check.py::TestRemoteHead::test_parses_sha_from_ls_remote_output` — *"Then it returns the leading **40-char** sha"*. The fixture's sha is `deadbeef0123`, twelve characters, and nothing in `remote_head` measures length.
- `test_update_check.py::test_local_up_to_date_returns_zero` — *"Then it returns exit code 0 and reports up to date **with the checkout path**"*. The path is never asserted (`assertIn("up to date", out)` only).
- `test_update_check.py::test_local_head_unreadable_returns_unknown` and `test_unknown_install_returns_exit_two` — *"with a clear message"* / *"with guidance on manual update"*, both backed by `assertTrue(len(err) > 0)`. The two deleted inline comments (*"Error message should mention the checkout"*, *"Error message should guide the user"*) stated the intent the assertions do not implement.
- `test_update_check.py` module docstring (deleted) claimed the file covers *"the update-check exit-code contract (0 up-to-date / 1 update-available / 2 unknown), the `--upgrade` ... flags"*. Under `--upgrade` on a git install the contract does not hold — `uv`'s own exit code is returned instead — and, per the mutation evidence above, nothing in the suite pins that either way.
- `test_update_check.py:772` (deleted) — *"# Legacy shim test: installed_origin is still used by some existing callers."* **False**: `installed_origin` has zero non-test callers (grepped repo-wide; the only production reference is its own `def`). Its docstring already says it is deprecated in favour of `detect_install`.

### RU: deleted (noise, no factual defect)

- **`test_tools_replay.py`** — the 16-line module docstring (bullet inventory of the three classifications, the false alembic narrative, the `THE KEYSTONE` headline, and a restatement of the project's own Given/When/Then convention) reduced to one line; **`_make_config`'s eight-line Args/Returns docstring** reduced to one line (`_make_bash_entry` and `_make_file_entry` were already one-liners and were kept); **`TestReplayBroadening`'s class docstring** reduced to one line, dropping *"this is the CRITICAL safety check"*; the **19-line simulation-rationale comment block** inside `test_alembic_landmine_broadening_detected`, plus its trailing docstring paragraph (*"This is the critical landmine test ..."*); and **26 inline comments**, nearly all trailing labels restating the line they sit on — `# ls:* removed`, `# deny added`, `# whoami added`, `# consolidation: now allows ALL alembic`, `# same broad allow`, `# ls removed, curl added`, `# still allowed in B` / `# no longer allowed in B` / `# now denied` / `# unchanged` / `# tightened: allow -> deny` / `# broadened: deny -> allow`, `# Config A: allows git and ls, denies curl`, `# The safe commands should still be allowed`, `# The DANGEROUS command should be classified as broadened`, `# Broadening should be detected`, `# Use 'curl *' (no colon) as the broad allow ...`. Three of the deleted comments were **false**: two `# denied in both` labels on corpus entries that the probe shows resolve to `ask` (`whoami` under a config with no `no_match_fallback`, `/etc/passwd` under the Read config), and `# whoami: deny verdict matches REFUSED -> True`, where the verdict is `ask` — `matches_observed` is `True` via `_verdict_matches_status`'s `ask` disjunct, not its `deny` one.
- **`test_update_check.py`** — the 18-line module docstring (a `TOO-16`/`TOO-45 R5c` provenance line, a *"New in this update (local-kind support)"* changelog of four bullets, and a *"see TestMain, the only class here that still exercises update_check directly"* note — the kind of claim an IDE answers) reduced to three lines, keeping only the one non-obvious constraint (everything is stubbed; no real network, subprocess or install); **two class docstrings** reduced to one line each (`TestDetectInstall`, `TestInstalledOriginLegacyBehavior`); **three `# --- <kind> kind tests ---` section banners** inside `TestCheck`; and **five inline comments**, including the two false intent notes above and the false legacy-shim claim. All four helper docstrings (`_dist_returning` x2, `_run_check`, `_git_info`, `_local_info`) were already one-liners and were kept.

### RU: rejected from the brief, with evidence

- **The brief's `_check_local` lead — *"re-runs `git rev-parse HEAD` for a value `detect_install` already stored"* — is confirmed, but the conclusion that a test pins it is wrong.** Mutating the re-read away is undetected (above); `test_local_head_unreadable_returns_unknown` reaches `EXIT_UNKNOWN` by a second route.
- **The brief's `replay_single` lead is confirmed on the caller question and irrelevant to the comments**: `redundancy.py` imports `replay`; `replay_single` has zero non-test callers (grepped). Nothing in `test_tools_replay.py` claims otherwise any more — `replay_single`'s *"useful for redundancy checking"* line is in `replay.py`, not here, and was already gone from the version in the tree.
- **`_verdict_matches_status` returning `False` for `ASK` is confirmed as a mechanism gap, not a comment defect in these files.** Neither file mentions it; the docstring that does (in `replay.py`) is accurate and explicitly warns that `False` reads as "not corroborated", never as "disagree". Recorded above as an undetected mechanism.
- **The "config change invisible to the corpus" lead** — `replay.py`'s module docstring is now correct on this, and **no test in `test_tools_replay.py` covers it**. The two `TestReplayUnchanged` tests look like they might, but they use `config_a is config_b`, which is a different (and tautological) thing. Recorded as a coverage gap and a fix shape, not a comment defect.
- **Live TDD instructions, RED-phase asides, stale line-number citations, a module docstring inventorying *deleted* tests, and the `test/unit/CLAUDE.md` citation** — none present in either file (grepped). The `test_update_check.py` module docstring's *"New in this update"* block is the closest relative: a changelog of tests that were **added**, not deleted.
- **"Nothing substantive" as a per-file verdict** — not claimed for either file. Each carries at least one assertion that cannot fail and several mechanisms with zero detection anywhere.
- **Not acted on (code, not comments)**: every test method in `test_tools_replay.py` opens with a function-local `from toolguard.tools.replay import ...`, which `~/.claude/rules/python.md` prohibits without an approved circular-import reason. There is none — the module imports cleanly at the top of the file. Test bodies are code for this sweep, so flagged not changed.

### LSA: assertions that cannot fail — `test_api.py`, `test_logging_streams.py`

- **`test_logging_streams.TestTakeoverNoticeNotPersisted.test_takeover_notice_writes_no_log_file` — the "no log file" half cannot fail (confirmed).** The test binds `log_dir = Path(tmp) / "logs"` and never creates it; `issue_takeover_warning` takes no `log_dir` argument at all and reads no log-directory anchor (`session_warnings.py` imports only `sys`). The assertion is `assertEqual(list(log_dir.glob(...)) if log_dir.exists() else [], [])`, so the guard short-circuits to `[]` and compares `[] == []`. No implementation of `issue_takeover_warning` can write into a freshly-made anonymous temp subdirectory it is never told about, so the failure is not constructible. The stderr half (`assertIn("Takeover mode is active", err.getvalue())`) is real. **Fix shape**: assert on a directory the function could plausibly reach — patch `toolguard.error_log.log_warning`/`log_error`/`log_conflict` at the *call* sites reachable from the notice and assert not-called, or drive the notice through `hook._announce_takeover_state` with an isolated `TOOLGUARD_LOG_DIR` and glob *that*.
- **The same behaviour's other test is vacuous too, so it has two tests and neither can fail.** `test_session_warnings.TestIssueTakeoverWarning.test_does_not_call_log_warning` patches `toolguard.error_log.log_warning` — a module `session_warnings.py` does not import, reference or reach (its only import is `sys`). `mock_log.assert_not_called()` therefore passes for every possible body of `issue_takeover_warning`, including one that writes a log stream by another route. Not this batch's file; recorded so the pair is fixed together.
- **`test_api.TestDecideSideEffectFree.test_decide_does_not_write_to_log_files` cannot fail.** The fixture is `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()`, and the assertion compares `set(logs_dir.iterdir())` before and after three `decide()` calls. `decide()` is never told that path and has no way to discover it; a `decide()` that wrote to the real `logs/` dir on every call would leave the anonymous temp dir empty and pass. Same shape as LSA-1: absence asserted against a fixture that cannot produce it. **Fix shape**: point the anchor at the directory the write would actually use — `isolate_config_environment()`'s `isolated_log_dir` (or an explicit `TOOLGUARD_LOG_DIR`) — and glob that; the existing `_real_log_dir_guard` record is the other half of the same question and could be asserted here directly.
- **`test_api.TestDecideSideEffectFree.test_decide_returns_decision_dataclass` — `assertIn(result.decision, ("allow", "ask", "deny"))` cannot fail.** The tuple enumerates every value `RuntimeVerdict.decision` is documented to take (`config_types.py:508`), so the asserted set is what every alternative produces. The test's other four assertions are real. **Fix shape**: assert the *specific* verdict this fixture must produce (`"allow"`, since the config allows `Bash(git:*)` and the command is `git status`) and delete the membership check, which is a type assertion in assertion clothing.

### LSA: assertions that cannot distinguish what their Then names (measured)

- **`test_api.TestDecideBashToolOverride.test_tool_override_replaces_only_the_tool_field` — the "every other field unchanged" half is never checked, and nothing else in the suite checks it either.** The Then reads *"the returned verdict's tool field is the caller's own tool name, **with every other field unchanged from the resolver's own result**"*; the body asserts `result.tool` and `result.decision` and nothing more. **Mutation, out-of-repo copy, whole suite re-run**: `api._decide_bash`'s `dataclasses.replace(result, tool=tool)` -> `dataclasses.replace(result, tool=tool, reason="", matched_rule=None, provenance=None)`. **Result identical to the 2-error floor — 0 failures.** That branch fires only when `tool != "Bash"` (MCP terminal tools), and this is the only test in the suite that exercises it, so the override can silently destroy `reason`, `matched_rule` and `provenance` on every MCP-terminal decision with no detection anywhere. **Fix shape**: build the un-overridden verdict once via `resolve_bash_permission_detailed` and assert `dataclasses.replace(overridden, tool="Bash") == baseline` — one assertion that actually means "only the tool field changed".
- **`test_api.TestProvenanceRegression.test_bash_hard_deny_sub_matches_has_matched_rule` — `assertIn("rm -rf", sm.matched_rule)` cannot tell the matched *pattern* from the *command*.** The fixture's hard-deny pattern is `Bash(rm -rf:*)` and the command is `rm -rf /`; both contain the literal `"rm -rf"`. **Mutation, whole suite**: `permissions.check_hard_deny`'s `LevelMatch(..., matched_pattern=pattern)` -> `matched_pattern=command`. **7 tests failed — none of them this one** (`test_compound_resolve_seam`, `test_hard_deny.TestCheckHardDenyUnit`, three in `test_resolve`, `test_verdict_corpus`). The mechanism is well pinned elsewhere; this assertion contributes nothing to pinning it. **Fix shape**: `assertEqual("Bash(rm -rf:*)", sm.matched_rule)` — the fixture's own pattern, which the command cannot impersonate.
- **`test_logging_streams.TestDivergenceWarningLogging.test_failed_migration_is_not_retried_same_day` — the gate its Then names is not the gate the assertion pins.** The Then attributes the single `migrate()` call to *"the divergence_warning claim the first call takes gates every later same-day call before it ever reaches run_auto_migration"*. **Mutation, whole suite**: disabling `config_divergence.check_and_warn_divergence`'s `DIVERGENCE_WARNING.done(project_root)` pre-check **and** its `DIVERGENCE_WARNING.warn(...)` early return — i.e. removing the named gate entirely — leaves this test **passing**; 4 tests in `test_config_divergence.py` fail, this one does not. With that gate gone the second call does reach `run_auto_migration`, where `AUTO_MIGRATION.done(project_root)` returns `False` before `migrate()` is called and holds the count at 1. Two independent same-day claims, either of which alone produces the asserted `call_count == 1`. The assertion is falsifiable (remove *both*), so this is a mis-attributed Then rather than a vacuous test. **Fix shape**: assert the mechanism directly — that `check_and_warn_divergence` returns `divergent_patterns == []` on the second call — or add a sibling that stubs `check_and_warn_divergence` to keep reporting divergence and asserts `migrate()` is still called once, which isolates `AUTO_MIGRATION`'s own claim.

### LSA: mechanisms with NO detection anywhere in the suite (mutated out-of-repo, whole suite re-run)

- **`api._decide_bash`'s tool-name override can rewrite any other field undetected** — see LSA above; `dataclasses.replace(result, tool=tool, reason="", matched_rule=None, provenance=None)` produced 0 failures above the floor.
- **`config_divergence.check_and_warn_divergence`'s once-per-day *pre-check* is undetected on its own.** Mutation: `if DIVERGENCE_WARNING.done(project_root): return DivergenceCheckResult(divergent_patterns=[])` disabled, leaving the later `warn()` claim in place. **0 failures above the floor.** Its own comment calls it a "cheap read-only pre-check", but removing it makes an already-warned day re-run `load_configuration` + `find_divergent_patterns` on every single PreToolUse call — a real cost on the hook's critical path, invisible to the suite. **Fix shape**: assert `load_configuration` (or `find_divergent_patterns`) is called exactly once across two same-day `check_and_warn_divergence` calls, in `test_config_divergence.TestCheckAndWarnDivergence`.

### LSA: `test_api.py`'s dependency on the ticket-18 over-match, located precisely

**`test_api.TestDecideSimpleBash.test_hard_deny_carve_out_exempts_command` is the one, and it is the ONLY `test_api` failure.** Executed 2026-08-12 in an out-of-repo copy with ticket 18's fix applied to `permissions.match_command` (`full_cmd_pattern = bc + cmd_pattern[len(base_cmd):] + "*"` replaced by a boundary-guarded `stem` / `stem + " *"` pair): 22 failures above the 2-error floor, of which exactly one is in `test_api.py`.

Mechanism, precisely: the fixture's hard-deny **carve-out** is `Bash(rm -rf /tmp:*)` and the command is `rm -rf /tmp/foo`. In `match_command`'s `args_pattern in ("*","**","")` branch, `cmd_pattern = "rm -rf /tmp"` and `base_cmd = "rm"`, so the `matched_base` guard (`permissions.py:158`) only checks that the command starts with `"rm "`; the pattern actually matched is `full_cmd_pattern = "rm -rf /tmp*"`, whose trailing `*` is glued onto the last token `/tmp` with no separator, and that is the sole reason it matches `rm -rf /tmp/foo`. Under the boundary-guarded form the carve-out no longer matches, `check_hard_deny` stops exempting the command, and the verdict flips `allow` -> `deny`. So this test does not merely tolerate the over-match — its expected outcome is reachable only through it.

**Correction to the earlier attribution**: `test_hard_deny.py` does **not** fail under this fix (verified in the same run — the 22 failures are `test_api` x1, `test_tools_consolidate` x10, `test_tools_maintenance` x9, `test_architecture_fitness` smoke x2 floor). The previous "20 failures including `test_hard_deny`" note collided on the name `hard_deny`, which appears as a *test-method* substring in `test_api.py` and as a *file* name elsewhere. **Fix shape when ticket 18 lands**: this test needs a carve-out pattern that survives the boundary rule (`Bash(rm -rf /tmp/:*)` or a `[glob]` carve-out), plus a deliberate sibling asserting that `rm -rf /tmpfoo` is *not* exempted — which is the whole point of the fix and is currently untested here.

### LSA: stale, false or unverifiable Given/When/Then (flagged, not rewritten)

- **`test_takeover_notice_writes_no_log_file`** — Then: *"no toolguard log stream file is created, and the notice is echoed to stderr -- issue_takeover_warning **no longer touches the claim store at all** (TOO-45 punch-list #01)"*. The first clause is unfalsifiable (LSA above) and the claim-store clause is asserted by nothing in the test: there is no claim-store patch, no store path, and no assertion about `once_per`/`once_per_store`. It is a commit note in a Then.
- **`test_failed_migration_is_not_retried_same_day`** — Then names the `divergence_warning` claim as the gate; measured above, the assertion cannot distinguish it from `AUTO_MIGRATION`'s own claim.
- **`test_tool_override_replaces_only_the_tool_field`** — Then's *"with every other field unchanged from the resolver's own result"* is checked by nothing, here or anywhere (measured above).
- **`test_api.TestDecideSideEffectFree.test_decide_does_not_write_to_log_files`** — Then: *"no log files are written (no side effects from the decision primitive)"*. What the body checks is "no files appear in an unrelated temp directory", which is a different and unfalsifiable claim.
- **No stale line-number citations, live TDD instructions, RED-phase asides, `test/unit/CLAUDE.md` citations, or module docstrings inventorying deleted tests** survive in either file. `test_api.py` *had* the last of these — an 11-line comment block explaining why `test_positional_construction_of_decision_still_works` was removed under TOO-45 R6-S3 — and it is deleted.
- **No test name contradicting its own assertions** was found in either file.

### LSA: what was deleted

**`test_api.py` (913 -> 841 lines).** Module docstring: a 5-bullet coverage inventory plus *"All tests use stdlib unittest with BDD Given/When/Then docstrings"* (a restatement of `.claude/rules/testing.md`) reduced to one line. Two class docstrings reduced to one line (`TestProvenanceRegression`'s regression-history paragraph; `TestDecideAdditionalContext`'s *"TOO-19 Phase 1, increment 6"* stage header). Helper `_make_config`'s Args/Returns block reduced to one line. Three trailing rationale paragraphs removed from test docstrings, keeping each Given/When/Then intact — including the 14-line TOO-45 R1e narrative on `test_bash_compound_mixed_escape_hatch_provenance_matches_matched_rule`. Eight inline comments, all restating the adjacent assertion (*"# No new files should have been created"*, *"# hard-deny is pooled; no single provenance."*), and the 11-line deleted-test block described above.

**`test_logging_streams.py` (952 -> 884 lines).** Module docstring: a 5-bullet inventory plus the pytest-convention note reduced to one line. Three class docstrings reduced to one line (`TestDiscoveryDiagnostic`'s two-paragraph TOO-19-M3 rewrite history; `TestDivergenceWarningLogging`'s hand-off rationale). One trailing meta-sentence removed from `test_failed_migration_is_not_retried_same_day`'s Then (about what a differently-written test would not have exercised) — the mechanism claim itself was **left standing and flagged above**, not laundered. Sixteen inline comments, including the six numbered call-annotations inside `test_two_project_roots_sharing_a_log_dir_do_not_flap` and four *"# Not routed to other streams."*-class restatements.

### LSA: rejected from the brief, with evidence

- **The brief's `_log_entry` facts were verified and needed no action.** The `## <timestamp> - <LEVEL>` heading is indeed parsed by `session_start._count_conflict_entries`; `log_dir.mkdir` is indeed the first statement of the body, outside the `try`, so an uncreatable directory raises before the stderr echo; `log_conflict` does have two callers. All three are already stated correctly in `error_log.py`'s own docstrings — nothing in *these two test files* claims otherwise, so there was nothing to fix here.
- **The stderr echo in `_log_entry` is NOT an undetected mechanism.** Mutation (both `print(..., file=sys.stderr)` calls removed), whole suite: 4 failures in `test_error_reporter.py`. Pinned, just not by this file.
- **"Nothing substantive" is not claimed for either file.** Each carries at least one assertion that cannot fail plus one Then its assertions do not check.
- **Not acted on (code, not comments)**: `test_api.py`'s `_IsolatedEnvTestCase` pops `CLAUDE_SETTINGS_PATH` for every test in four classes, but every test in the file builds its `Configuration` from hand-constructed `ConfigLayer`/`Provenance` objects with zero file I/O — the case `.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md`'s first checklist item says needs no isolation at all. The base class is inert. Flagged, not removed.

## TSX — from the comment sweep of the fourteenth `test/unit/` batch (2 files, 2026-08-12)

Files: `test/unit/test_touch_set_score.py` (718 -> 707), `test/unit/test_touch_set_inventory.py` (768 -> 756). Suite unchanged at 2733 OK; zero code-shape drift. Evidence below comes from **52 single-mutation whole-suite runs** in an out-of-repo `rsync` copy (floor: the two `test_architecture_fitness.TestSmokeAgainstRealTree` errors the copy produces for not being a git repo). **These two files are the ONLY coverage either module has anywhere** — `grep -rl 'touch_set_score\|touch_set_inventory' test/ tools/ toolguard/` returns exactly the two modules and these two test files — so a mutation with zero detection here has zero detection in the repo. 13 of the 52 mutations were undetected.

### TSX: mechanisms with NO detection anywhere in the suite (mutated in an out-of-repo copy, whole suite re-run)

- **`touch_set_score.main` — BOTH argument-validation guards are individually undetected, because the one test that exercises argument validation trips both at once.** Mutations: `if args.actuals is not None and two_judge:` -> `if False:` (0 above floor) and, separately, `if two_judge and (args.actuals_judge_1 is None or args.actuals_judge_2 is None):` -> `if False:` (0 above floor). `test_actuals_and_two_judge_flags_together_is_an_error` passes `--predictions --actuals --actuals-judge-1` and **omits `--actuals-judge-2`**, so the mutual-exclusion guard and the both-judges-together guard each reject it on their own; deleting either leaves the other to return 2. No test isolates either. `TestEndToEndCLIModes`'s docstring claims the class exercises *"the mutually-exclusive argument validation"* — it does not. **Fix shape**: add `--actuals-judge-2` to the existing test (isolating the mutual-exclusion guard) and add one new test passing `--actuals-judge-1` alone (isolating the pairing guard). A new shape for the catalogue: **two independent guards where the single fixture trips both, so neither is individually detectable.**
- **`touch_set_score.LocationEntry.has_disagreement` — the `!=` half is undetected.** Mutation: `return self.kind_2 is not None and self.kind_2 != self.kind` -> `return self.kind_2 is not None`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. Under it, two judges who give the **same** real kind are reported as disagreeing, and nothing fires: the only two-judge fixture where both judges agree is `test_two_judge_mode_runs_and_exits_zero`, which asserts an exit code and nothing about buckets. **Fix shape**: assert in `TestD9LocationSetDisagreement` (or a new two-judge agreement test) that a location both judges called `record` lands in `kind_agreements` and in neither disagreement bucket.
- **`touch_set_score.LocationEntry.is_abstention` — the `kind_2` clause is undetected.** Mutation: `return self.kind == KIND_UNKNOWN and (self.kind_2 is None or self.kind_2 == KIND_UNKNOWN)` -> `return self.kind == KIND_UNKNOWN`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. The case it exists for is judge 1 abstaining while judge 2 answers — `LocationEntry(kind=KIND_UNKNOWN, kind_2="decide")` — and **only the mirror orientation is tested** (`test_dual_judge_one_abstains_one_answers_is_a_disagreement_not_abstention` builds `kind="decide", kind_2=KIND_UNKNOWN`). **Fix shape**: add the reversed fixture to that same test; the docstring's *"an abstention means NEITHER judge could tell"* is only half-pinned without it.
- **`touch_set_score.build_evidence` — the relative order of the abstention and disagreement branches is inert.** Mutation: swap `if m.actual.is_abstention: ... elif m.actual.has_disagreement:` for the reverse. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. Not a coverage gap but a redundancy worth recording: the two properties are **mutually exclusive by construction** (`is_abstention` requires `kind_2 in (None, KIND_UNKNOWN)`, `has_disagreement` requires `kind_2 is not None and != kind`), so no entry can satisfy both. `build_evidence`'s docstring rationale (*"Abstention is tested first"*) is true and load-bearing **only against the later agreement/mismatch comparison**, not against the disagreement branch it currently precedes.
- **`touch_set_score._actual_kind_label` — the whole function is undetected.** Mutation: `return KIND_DISAGREEMENT if actual.has_disagreement else actual.kind` -> `return actual.kind`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. `KIND_DISAGREEMENT` appears in **no test in the repo** (grep: 0 hits outside the module), including its module-level collision assert. The contract it carries — *"never a guess at which judge is right"* — reaches the report through `matched_dict`/`changed_but_not_predicted` and nothing reads it back. **Fix shape**: one test that builds a disagreeing actual, calls `build_report`, and asserts `actual_kind == "DISAGREEMENT"`.
- **`touch_set_inventory._is_gitignored` — the entire ANCHORED branch is undetected.** Mutation: `if posix == pat or posix.startswith(pat + "/"):` -> `if False:`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. Every gitignore fixture in the file uses `tmp/` or `logs`, which lose their trailing slash and take the per-component `fnmatch` branch. **This is the branch TSI1 documents as losing globbing** (`docs/*.py` does not exclude `docs/a.py`), so the one matching rule with a published limitation has no test at all. **Fix shape**: a test for an anchored pattern that does match (`a/b/` excluding `a/b/x.py`) plus the TSI1 negative (`docs/*.py` not excluding `docs/a.py`), which would also pin the limitation against a well-meaning "fix".
- **`touch_set_inventory.discover_python_files` — the `EXCLUDED_DIR_NAMES` filter is undetected.** Mutation: `if any(part in EXCLUDED_DIR_NAMES for part in relpath.parts[:-1]): continue` -> `if False: continue`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. No fixture anywhere creates a `__pycache__`, `.venv`, `build`, `cover`, ... directory. The 12-name constant and the comment above it (*"duplicated here rather than imported from tools.change_role_classifier, deliberately"*) are unverified. **Fix shape**: one fixture with `__pycache__/x.py` and `.venv/y.py` alongside a real module.
- **`touch_set_inventory.is_test_path` — the whole function is undetected.** Mutation: body -> `return False`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. `test_is_test_flag_passed_through_unchanged` passes `is_test=True` **into** `build_module_entry`; nothing calls the classifier that computes it in `run_inventory`, and nothing asserts the `[test]` tag it drives in the text report. The class is named `TestLineCountAndIsTest`, which reads as coverage of the classifier. **Fix shape**: assert `is_test` on modules discovered by `run_inventory` from a fixture containing `test/mod.py`, `test_mod.py`, `mod_test.py` and `mod.py`.
- **`touch_set_inventory._symbol_kind` — only the async arm is detected.** Mutation: `return "class"` -> `return "function"`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. `test_async_function_reported_with_its_own_kind` is the only test that reads `SymbolEntry.kind`; `"class"` and `"function"` are asserted nowhere, so `SymbolEntry.kind`'s documented three-value domain is one-third pinned. **Fix shape**: extend `test_nested_function_and_method_not_reported` to assert kinds alongside the names it already collects.
- **`touch_set_inventory.validate_predictions` — the shared `normalize_location` call is undetected.** Mutation: `normalized = [normalize_location(loc) for loc in raw_locations]` -> `normalized = list(raw_locations)`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. Every prediction fixture in the file is already in normal form. The import carries an explicit comment — *"Shared rather than reimplemented so the two tools' notion of 'the same location string' cannot drift apart"* — and the drift it guards against is untested. **Fix shape**: one prediction written as `./mod.py::public_fn` (or with a backslash separator) asserted valid.
- **`touch_set_inventory.all_locations_for_validation` — its gitignore honouring is undetected.** Mutation: `gitignore_patterns = _load_gitignore_patterns(tree_root)` -> `= []` (in that function only). Whole-suite result identical to baseline. The two D12 tests both go through `run_inventory`/`discover_python_files`; the validation path's own copy of the call is unexercised, and its docstring claims *"Honours the tree's own gitignore"*. **Fix shape**: validate a prediction naming a location inside a gitignored file and assert it comes back **invalid**.
- **`touch_set_inventory.validate_predictions` — the `seen` de-duplication of the valid/invalid lists is undetected.** Mutation: `if loc in seen: continue` -> `if False: continue`. Whole-suite result identical to baseline. `test_duplicate_prediction_reported_not_fatal` asserts only `duplicate_predictions == {"mod.py::public_fn": 2}` and never looks at `valid_locations`, where the duplicate would appear twice. **Fix shape**: add `self.assertEqual(result.valid_locations, ["mod.py::public_fn"])` to that same test — one line, and it makes the "reported, not silently merged" claim complete.

### TSX: tests whose assertions cannot fail (or cannot fail for the stated reason)

- **`test_touch_set_score.py::TestEndToEndCLIModes::test_actuals_and_two_judge_flags_together_is_an_error`** — Then: *"it exits non-zero rather than silently picking one mode"*. The fixture provides an alternative route to the outcome: it omits `--actuals-judge-2`, so the pairing guard returns 2 even with the mutual-exclusion guard deleted (S21, 0 above floor), and vice versa (S22). Neither guard is reachable by this assertion. **Fix shape**: above.
- **`test_touch_set_score.py::TestD4AbstentionOwnBucket::test_dual_judge_both_abstain_is_kind_abstained`** — `assertEqual(evidence.kind_disagreements, [])` is a dead assertion. Its Then explains the pair is *"not a disagreement (both saying 'I don't know' is not the same finding as two judges giving different real answers)"*, but `is_abstention` is tested **first** in `build_evidence`, so a matched pair that lands in `kind_abstained` can never reach `kind_disagreements` under **any** implementation of `has_disagreement`. Constructed: `has_disagreement` -> `return self.kind_2 is not None` makes the property True for this fixture and the test still passes (S12, 0 above floor). Only the `len(kind_abstained) == 1` half is load-bearing. **Fix shape**: assert on the property directly (`self.assertFalse(actual.has_disagreement)`), which is what the Then actually claims.
- **`test_touch_set_score.py::TestBuildEvidenceNoScoring::test_report_has_no_rate_or_ratio_key_except_the_documented_one`** — a Then naming a mechanism the assertions never check, **and getting it wrong** (this is TSS9, now confirmed with the file in scope). The Then says the only ratio-named field is *"the plain, **undivided** display string next to location_counts"*; it is produced by `f"{total_predictions / total_actual:.2f}x actual count"` (TSS1), the module's single division. No assertion in the test mentions `ratio` at all — the three assertions are `assertNotIn("rates", ...)`, `assertNotIn("confusion_matrix", ...)` and a walk for the substring `"rate"`, none of which can observe a second ratio field appearing. The `"rate"` walk itself is real (renaming the field to `predicted_to_actual_rate` fails it, S17). **Fix shape**: assert the exact set of `location_counts` keys, so "except the documented one" becomes a checkable claim. Not laundered — the Then is left as written.
- **`test_touch_set_score.py::TestEndToEndCLIModes::test_json_output_is_valid_json`** — Then: *"stdout is valid, parseable JSON with no rate/ratio-scored field"*. The parsed payload **contains** `location_counts.predicted_to_actual_ratio`; the only assertion about it is `assertNotIn("rates", parsed)`, a top-level key nothing produces. Same TSS1 over-reach as above, in a second place. **Fix shape**: drop the clause or assert the ratio's presence and shape, which is what the report actually promises.
- **`test_touch_set_score.py::TestParseEntriesJsonDuplicateKeys::test_duplicate_location_key_is_fatal`** — Then: *"a fatal error names the entry and the duplicated key -- the real (first) location never silently vanishes in favour of the second"*. The second clause is unasserted: the test binds `entries` and never uses it, so nothing checks that the offending entry was excluded rather than kept with the last-wins value. The first clause is also weaker than stated — `any("location" in e and "duplicate" in e.lower())` matches the temp path plus the word "duplicate"; the entry **number** is not checked. Same unused `entries` binding in `test_duplicate_kind_key_is_fatal`.

### TSX: near-vacuous, kept as notes

- **`test_touch_set_score.py::TestLoadEntries::test_literal_kind_unknown_string_rejected`** — Then: *"a fatal schema error -- that string is this tool's own reserved sentinel"*. There is no sentinel-specific branch: `"kind_unknown"` is rejected by `kind_raw not in WRITABLE_KINDS`, the same line that rejects `"does_something"` in `test_invalid_kind_is_a_fatal_error`. The realistic regression — adding `KIND_UNKNOWN` to `WRITABLE_KINDS` — is caught **at import time** by the module-level `assert` at `touch_set_score.py:125`: measured, it produces two `unittest.loader._FailedTest` errors for the whole test modules, not a failure of this test (S23). Only an artificial mutation that special-cases the sentinel in the loader fails it alone (S24). So the property is pinned by the assert; the test is a second copy of the invalid-kind test. Kept — it costs nothing and documents the reserved string.
- **`test_touch_set_inventory.py::TestD5WidenedQualnameCollection::test_module_level_lambda_assignment_collected`** vs `test_module_level_plain_assignment_collected` — the same `ast.Assign`-with-`Name`-target branch, differing only in the assigned **value**. Both fail together under the one mutation that removes module-level assignment collection (I18). Separable only by a hypothetical implementation restricting the value type, which is what the Then names. Kept.
- **`test_touch_set_inventory.py::TestValidatePredictions::test_main_exits_zero_when_all_predictions_valid`** — the control for `test_main_exits_zero_even_when_a_prediction_is_invalid`. Only the latter detects the D5 advisory-vs-gate mutation (I20). The control can only fail if `main` returns non-zero unconditionally. Kept: a control that is cheap and legible.

### TSX: coverage gaps found while checking claims against the modules

- **Neither module's text reporter is asserted anywhere.** `touch_set_score.print_text_report`, `touch_set_inventory.print_text_report` and `print_validation_report` appear in **no test** (grep: 0 hits in both files). They run — eight `main()` calls across the two files reach them — but only exit codes are checked. Consequence measured at the suite level: **132 of the whole suite's 440 stdout lines come from the four tests that print a full report without redirecting** (`base.out` lines 309-440: two validation reports and two evidence reports). `test_json_output_is_valid_json` is the only test in either file that uses `contextlib.redirect_stdout`. Two problems for the price of one: the report text is unverified *and* it is dumped into every developer's test output.
- **`touch_set_inventory.build_validation_report` has no shape test**, while its sibling `build_report` has `test_report_json_shape_carries_every_field`. The `--validate-predictions` JSON contract (`valid_count`, `invalid_count`, `suggestions`, `duplicate_predictions`, `known_limitations`) is asserted nowhere.
- **`touch_set_inventory.main`'s plain-inventory path is never taken.** All three `main()` tests pass `--validate-predictions`. The inventory branch, the `--json` branch, and the `--tree is not a directory` guard (`return 2`) have no test.
- **`test_report_json_shape_carries_every_field` does not carry every field.** Of the module dict's five keys it asserts three (`path`, `docstring_first_line`, `public_symbols`); `is_test` and `line_count` are unchecked. Of the report's five top-level keys it asserts two; `tree_root` and `known_limitations` are unchecked. The Then says *"the documented top-level keys and a per-module entry with all four required fields"* — there are five per-module fields, not four.
- **`KNOWN_LIMITATIONS` is carried into both modules' reports and asserted in neither**, in either module. Given that TSI1/TSS4/TSS5 are all defects *in* those strings, nothing would notice one being dropped from a report.

## CTA — from the comment sweep of `test_tools_consolidate.py` and `test_tools_takeover_audit.py` (2 files, batch 16, 2026-08-12)

### CTA: assertions that cannot fail (verified by execution)

- **`test_tools_consolidate.py::TestFamily2ConservativeNonClaim.test_git_vs_git_annex_not_subsumed`** — asserts `assertNotIn({"git-annex:*"}, removed_sets)` where `removed_sets` is built from `family2 = [p for p in proposals if p.kind == "static-subsumption"]`. Ran the exact fixture (`allow=["git:*", "git-annex:*"]`) through `propose_consolidations`: it returns `[]` outright, because `_static_prefix_of("git", "git-annex")` is already `False` at the candidate-forming stage (no boundary), so no static-subsumption candidate is ever built for this pair. `removed_sets` is therefore `[]` and the assertion passes independent of whether the subsumption mechanism ever ran. The real protection is already pinned directly by `TestStaticPrefixOf.test_bare_textual_prefix_does_not_subsume`; this test proves nothing beyond it.
- **`test_tools_takeover_audit.py::TestBrokenTakeoverConfig.test_findings_sorted_critical_first`** — the loop asserting `assertGreaterEqual(severities[i], severities[i+1])` is reachable (the fixture does produce 2 findings), but ran it: both are severity `CRITICAL` (`hook-not-registered`, `partial-hook-registration`), giving `severities == [4, 4]`. `ignored_allow_patterns=[]` does **not** clear the hardcoded `_DEFAULT_IGNORED_ALLOW_PATTERNS` seed in `Configuration.takeover_mode()` (`config.py`, `_DEFAULT_IGNORED_ALLOW_PATTERNS` is always the starting list, patterns only add to it) — `Bash(*)`/`Read(*)` stay covered by the defaults regardless, so no `uncovered-blanket-allow` HIGH finding is produced. A same-value comparison (`4 >= 4`) can never distinguish ascending from descending order, so the test's stated purpose — verify CRITICAL sorts before HIGH — is never exercised by this fixture.

### CTA: near-vacuous but currently exercised (flagged as a fragility, not a defect)

- **`test_tools_consolidate.py::TestFamily1EquivalenceAndLandmine.test_equivalence_preserving_consolidation_accepted`** — `for p in proposals: if p.kind == "literal-alternation": self.assertIn("unchanged", p.replay_summary)` has no assertion that the loop body ever ran. Ran the fixture: it currently produces exactly one `literal-alternation` proposal with `replay_summary="10 probes unchanged; no corpus"`, so the assertion does fire today. But nothing pins `len(family1) >= 1` first, so if the safety gate ever rejected this candidate (plausible — it sits right next to the alembic landmine gate this class is named for), the test would silently pass having checked nothing.

### CTA: stale or overstated Given/When/Then, flagged not laundered (per settled precedent)

- **`test_tools_consolidate.py::TestFamily1GitHappyPath.test_consolidation_preserves_prefix_extension_commands`** — Then reads *"...keep verdict 'allow' -- the consolidation does NOT silently tighten what the DEFAULT cmd:* prefix already allowed."* Per `proposed-tickets/18-default-multitoken-prefix-over-match.md`, `'git diff:*'` matching `'git difftool'` is the documented **matcher bug** (only the first token of a multi-token DEFAULT prefix is boundary-guarded; `diff` unguarded-prefix-matches `difftool`), not intended DEFAULT-prefix semantics. The test's mechanics are accurate — it does check exactly this — but the framing presents the over-match as an established baseline the consolidation must preserve, exactly the risk the coordinator brief flagged for this file. Left standing per the settled "don't launder a false Given/Then" precedent; this test is a plausible member of ticket 18's "10 `test_tools_consolidate` tests fail once the over-match is boundary-guarded" count (not independently confirmed — that would require applying the real fix in an out-of-repo copy).
- **`test_tools_takeover_audit.py::TestBrokenTakeoverConfig.test_findings_sorted_critical_first`** — Given reads *"Given a configuration with both CRITICAL and HIGH findings"*, which is false per the CTA finding above (only two same-severity CRITICALs are produced). Left standing per the same precedent.

### CTA: deleted (noise, no factual defect)

- Both module docstrings' bullet-point "Tests cover:"/"Tests verify:" lists, reduced to one line each, matching the `test_hard_deny.py`/`test_tools_redundancy.py` precedent already in this sweep.
- Several class docstrings compressed to one line, dropping restated bullet lists and ticket/phase labels that duplicated their own test methods' Given/When/Then: `TestCorrectTakeoverSetup`, `TestBrokenTakeoverConfig`, `TestFamily1EquivalenceAndLandmine` (kept the equivalence-preserving fact, dropped the elaboration), `TestPrefixBroadening` (dropped the stale `P2-D` phase label — grepped, appears nowhere else in the repo), `TestLooseUndecidableFallback` (dropped `(TOO-19)`), `TestStaticPrefixOf`.
- One `# Loose-undecidable-fallback (HIGH) -- TOO-19` section banner trimmed to drop the ticket suffix (banners themselves are kept, per this sweep's established precedent that they're structural navigation, not narrative).
- `test_uncovered_blanket_allow_flagged`'s trailing docstring "Note:" paragraph (restated the `_DEFAULT_IGNORED_ALLOW_PATTERNS` membership fact, verified true but redundant with the code it describes) and `test_takeover_off_with_blanket_allows_and_missing_hook`'s trailing "(deliberate broken config - required test fixture)" parenthetical (redundant with its class docstring) — both go beyond Given/When/Then, which is all a test docstring keeps in this tier.
- `test_tools_consolidate.py::TestConfigWithoutAllowDelegation.test_removes_structured_entry_by_pattern`'s trailing "Regression test for the confirmed TOO-19 Phase 0a increment 6 defect..." paragraph after its Then — same shape as the one already cut from the sibling `test_tools_redundancy.py::test_structured_entry_corpus_redundant_when_bare_rule_covers_it` in this sweep.
- Roughly a dozen inline `#` comments restating the line below them (`# Layer_b should still have git status:*`, `# Native layer with NO hooks section`, `# Read missing -> mixed state`, `# confirm conflict exists`, `# config-wide, not tool-specific`, etc.) and inline trailing comments on list literals (`# 5 cmd tokens`, `# larger: covers /tmp/<anything>`). Section `# ---` banners were kept throughout, matching this sweep's precedent.
- One overclaim fixed in a Given/When/Then: `test_adds_new_patterns`'s Then said the new pattern is "appended" — the test only asserts membership (`assertIn`), never position, so "appended" was trimmed to avoid a claim the assertion doesn't check.

### CTA: mutation testing

Not run — time did not permit an out-of-repo mutation pass on `consolidate.py`/`takeover_audit.py` for this batch. The two assertions-that-cannot-fail findings above were confirmed by direct execution against the real modules instead (equivalent evidence for those two specific tests, narrower in scope than a full mutation sweep).
