## AMD — from the comment sweep of `test_auto_migrate.py` and `test_config_divergence.py` (batch 17, 2026-08-12)

### AMD: mis-attributed Then, confirmed (Lead A from the brief)

- **`test_auto_migrate.py::TestRunAutoMigration.test_run_auto_migration_migrate_failure`** — Then reads *"it returns False and the day's claim stays held -- the only production caller only ever reaches this function once a day (gated by `check_and_warn_divergence`'s own daily claim), so there is no same-day retry to release for."* This test calls `run_auto_migration` directly, never through `check_and_warn_divergence` — the `divergence_warning` gate the Then credits is never exercised in this test at all. The "claim stays held" observation the test actually asserts is produced entirely by `AUTO_MIGRATION`'s own claim, taken inside `run_auto_migration`'s own call to `OncePer.run()` (`toolguard/once_per.py`), independent of `divergence_warning`. Confirmed by reading both `auto_migrate.py` (the only claim taken in this call path is `AUTO_MIGRATION`'s) and `hook.py:_run_divergence_check` (which does gate the production call site on `check_and_warn_divergence`'s result -- so the *production* fact stated is true, but it explains nothing about what this unit test itself observes). Left standing per the settled "don't launder a false Given/Then" precedent — the mechanics the test runs are correct, only the explanation for why the second claim attempt reports HELD is wrong.

### AMD: undetected mechanism, confirmed by mutation (Lead B from the brief)

- **`config_divergence.check_and_warn_divergence`'s once-per-day pre-check** (`if DIVERGENCE_WARNING.done(project_root): return DivergenceCheckResult(divergent_patterns=[])`, `config_divergence.py:180-181`) was deleted in an out-of-repo copy (`rsync`'d to scratch, mutated, cleaned up after). Full suite: **2733 tests, 2 errors** — exactly the documented environmental floor (`TestSmokeAgainstRealTree`, needs a git repo), i.e. **zero detection**. Mechanism: `test_deduplication`'s second call still comes back empty because `.warn()`'s own `_claim()` returns `HELD_BY_SOMEONE_ELSE` and short-circuits before printing — the pre-check only saves the cost of re-running `load_configuration`/`find_divergent_patterns` on an already-warned day, a performance property no test checks. No Given/Then in the file claims the pre-check exists or skips analysis (checked both `test_deduplication` and `test_no_divergence_releases_claim_so_later_scan_same_day_still_works` — neither mentions skipping work, only the dedup outcome), so there was no false claim to flag here, just a queue-worthy coverage gap.

### AMD: assertions that don't verify what their Then claims

- **`test_auto_migrate.py::TestRunAutoMigration.test_run_auto_migration_takeover_mode_ignored_patterns`** — Given/Then: *"Given takeover mode ignores Bash(*) and divergence detection filters it out ... Then no divergence remains."* The test mocks `find_divergent_patterns` itself and hardcodes its return value to `{"allow": [], "deny": [], "ask": []}` (inline comment previously read `# Bash(*) was ignored`, now deleted as it asserted the same false premise) — the mock returns this **regardless of what arguments it receives**, so the test never checks that `run_auto_migration`'s `ignored_patterns` computation (`auto_migrate.py:84-89`, folding `ignored_allow_patterns` + `additional_ignored_patterns` under `takeover_config["enabled"]`) was ever built or passed. Deleting that computation entirely would not fail this test. The real, non-vacuous coverage of ignored-pattern filtering is `test_config_divergence.py::TestFindDivergentPatterns.test_ignore_patterns_in_takeover_mode`, which calls the real `find_divergent_patterns`. This test is effectively a duplicate of `test_run_auto_migration_no_divergence` (same mechanism: `find_divergent_patterns` returns empty, `run_auto_migration` returns False) wearing a takeover-mode label.
- **`test_config_divergence.py::TestDivergenceCheckCreatesNoStorageWhenNothingToReport.test_no_divergence_creates_no_legacy_logs_dir_or_db`** — Then reads *"...a healthy, non-diverging project must not gain a logs/ directory **or claim database** it never asked for"* (method name also says `..._or_db`), but the body only asserts `self.assertFalse(logs_dir.exists())` — no assertion anywhere checks for a claim-database file. Verified the claim mechanically holds today (`once_per_store._resolve_store_path()` in `toolguard/once_per_store.py` always resolves to `~/.toolguard/once_per.db`, never under the project, so there is no project-local DB file this test even *could* check for), but that makes the "or claim database" clause untestable-as-written rather than tested. Flagged, not rewritten.

### AMD: claim reaching outside the file, likely false

- **`test_auto_migrate.py::TestRunAutoMigration.test_skips_migration_when_sqlite_unavailable`** — Then's rationale clause: *"...unlike a warning, migrate() writes permission config, so concurrent, uncoordinated processes could otherwise both run it at once with no mutual exclusion and last-writer-wins could silently discard one process's changes (TOO-45 punch-list #15: once-per-day throttling being unavailable now fails CLOSED for this action, not open)."* Checked `toolguard/permission_migration.py:1242-1262`: `migrate()` takes its own OS-level exclusive file lock (`file_lock.exclusive(lock_path, ...)`) spanning the whole real-run call, and declines with `MigrationOutcome.DECLINED_LOCKED` rather than racing when contended — added by the very commit this Then cites (`caa83e7`, "TOO-45 Item 15 - migrate() serialises itself with an OS file lock"). So "no mutual exclusion" is false as stated: migrate() invocations cannot race each other regardless of the once-per-day throttle's state. The `Repeat.UNSAFE` choice for this action may still be right for a different reason (repeated *serial* migrations on every hook call, not corrupted concurrent writes), but the stated justification is not it. Left standing per the settled precedent.

- A subprocess-isolation hazard comment in `test_two_processes_only_one_warns` (explaining why the child script patches `once_per_store._STORE_PATH`, to avoid polluting the developer's real `~/.toolguard/once_per.db`) and a "Create project marker so discover_config_files works" comment repeated across four tests in `TestCheckAndWarnDivergence`. Both are genuinely informative and border on hazard notes; cut per this batch's explicit "delete essentially every other comment" instruction rather than the general standard's keep-a-hazard guidance. Flagging here in case the coordinator wants either restored — the STORE_PATH one in particular protects against a costly mistake (a future editor removing the patch and corrupting a real database) that a reader can't fully infer from the code alone.
- Roughly twenty inline `#` comments restating the assertion immediately below them (`# Should return defaults...`, `# Last file (file2) should win`, `# No divergence after filtering...`, `# Should find divergence`, `# Not a governed tool` x2, `# Trailing space makes it different`, `# Pattern-only de-dup...`, etc.) and a handful of trailing same-line comments (`# default` x2, `# Success`, `# Failure outcome`, `# must not raise`).
- `_config_from_layers`' docstring (`test_config_divergence.py`) trimmed from a 4-line block to one line — kept the `(source_type, content)` shape explanation (non-obvious from a bare `*layers` signature) since it's a private test helper, cut the "lets divergence tests exercise..." rationale sentence as derivable from reading its one call site.

Nothing was claimed in this call, so nothing is "released"; the day's slot was simply never touched. The test's own assertion (a fresh `claim()` reports `CLAIMED`) is consistent with either story, so it doesn't distinguish them, but "releases" specifically implies a claim-then-release sequence that didn't happen.

## OPC — from the comment sweep of `test_once_per_store.py` and `test_tools_config_access.py` (batch 18, 2026-08-12)

### OPC: assertions that cannot fail

None found. Went through both files against the 14-shape catalog method-by-method. The closest candidates that turned out to be real tests: `test_different_kind/scope/project_claims_independently` (`test_once_per_store.py`) look like they could be trivially true (a second `claim()` call with nothing pre-existing always succeeds), but each varies exactly one dimension of the `(project, kind, scope)` key while holding the others fixed to a value the FIRST call already used — a build that dropped isolation on that one dimension would collide and report `HELD_BY_SOMEONE_ELSE` instead of `CLAIMED`, so the assertion is live. Likewise `test_expired_claim_can_be_reclaimed` looks like it could pass under a build with no reclaim logic at all (an `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` variant) since nothing pre-exists before the first call either — but the *second* call in that same test only succeeds because of the `WHERE claims.expires_at < excluded.created_at` reclaim clause; a no-op-on-conflict build would report `HELD_BY_SOMEONE_ELSE` there and fail. No defect.

### OPC: Lead A (decoy-row masking in `test_once_per_store.py`) — checked, not found

Went through every test asserting a row's absence (`is_claimed` returning `False`, `reap()`'s row-count checks, post-`release()` checks) and traced which single mechanism each depends on:

- Expiry-only tests (`test_false_for_an_expired_claim`, the two `TestIsClaimedMalformedTimestamp` tests) never call `reap()` or `release()` in the same test — they isolate `is_claimed`'s own `datetime` comparison.
- Deletion-only tests (`test_false_after_release`, `test_release_allows_reclaim`) use `ttl=timedelta(days=1)` (not expired) so expiry cannot be the cause of the observed absence — isolates `release()`'s `DELETE`.
- `reap()`'s tests query the `claims` table directly via `sqlite3.connect(...).execute("SELECT COUNT(*)...")` rather than through `is_claimed()`, so a `reap()` bug and an `is_claimed()` bug can't be confused with each other by the same assertion.

No test in this file creates a row that is simultaneously a candidate for two absence-causing mechanisms (e.g., a row both released AND naturally expired by the time it's checked). The "decoy row expired-on-write" shape from elsewhere in the sweep does not recur here.

### OPC: mutation testing (`test_once_per_store.py`)

Three mutations, each in an out-of-repo `rsync` copy under the scratchpad, one at a time, full suite run, cleaned up after each:

1. **`reap()`'s expiry filter removed** (`DELETE FROM claims` unconditionally, dropping `WHERE expires_at < ?`): 11 failures + the 2 environmental-floor errors. Caught directly by `test_reap_keeps_live_claims` in the target file, plus five tests across `test_once_per.py`, `test_auto_migrate.py`, `test_config_divergence.py`, `test_logging_streams.py` that all depend on same-day claims surviving a `reap()` call. No masking — heavily over-determined.
2. **`is_claimed()`'s expiry comparison replaced with `return True`** (never treats anything as expired): 3 failures + the 2 floor errors, all three inside `test_once_per_store.py` itself (`test_false_for_an_expired_claim`, both `TestIsClaimedMalformedTimestamp` tests). Clean, no masking.
3. **`release()`'s `DELETE` replaced with a no-op**: 2 failures + the 2 floor errors (`test_release_allows_reclaim`, `test_false_after_release`). Clean, no masking.

No masking pair found between expiry, deletion, and reap in this file — Lead A's premise (that this file was a prime candidate) didn't pan out on the mechanisms tried, though only three of the module's several mechanisms were mutated; the `claim()` reclaim `WHERE` clause and the schema-versioning repair path were not separately mutated (time budget), so absence of evidence there is weaker than for the three above.

### OPC: Lead B (`verified_write_config` in `test_tools_config_access.py`) — rejected, doesn't apply

`verified_write_config` lives in `toolguard/config_write_guard.py`, not `toolguard/tools/config_access.py`. `test_tools_config_access.py` imports only from `toolguard.tools.config_access` (`RuleComment`, `audit_context`, `config_summary`, `discover_tools`, `effective_takeover`, `load_config`, `neutralized_by_takeover`, `nosecurity_reason_for`, `per_layer_rules`, `rule_comments_for_tool`) and never references `verified_write_config` or a `present` variable at all (`grep` confirms). The brief's Lead B is about a different test file; noted as a coordinator error per the "reject with evidence" instruction.

### Assertions that cannot fail

- `test_serializes_findings_with_expanded_provenance` (`TestReportToDict`): `self.assertEqual(payload["total_findings"], report.total_findings)`. `toolguard/tools/maintenance.py:388`, `report_to_dict`, does `"total_findings": report.total_findings` — a direct attribute copy, no independent computation on either side. The comparison is `x == x` under a different name and cannot fail for any report. (The adjacent `assertTrue(payload["has_any_findings"])` on the same test is a real check — truthiness of the actual report content, not a copy comparison — and was left alone.)

Minor — the other four assertions in the same test (broadened/tightened counts and commands) are real, this one line is not.

Left in place (not this tier's job to rewrite assertions), noting for whoever next touches this test.

None of these were introduced by this pass — the code and test bodies were unchanged by the sweep; they're pre-existing and surfaced by reading `report_to_dict`/`replay_diff_to_dict` against the assertions while verifying GWT accuracy.

## LWT -- test/unit/test_log_writer.py (TOO-45 punch-list #07)

Line count: 1204 before, 1147 after (-57).

### Assertions that cannot fail

None found. Every assertion in this file checks a real fact about `log_writer.py`'s output (field presence, field order, exact byte content, or an omission) computed by the module under test, not restated from the test's own setup. No mock-with-hardcoded-return, no fixture that can't produce the asserted absence, no both-sides-compute-the-same-thing pattern, no bound-but-unasserted variable. The golden-file tests (`TestLogFormatGoldenFile`) are the strongest in the file -- exact byte comparison against a literal expected string, independent of the module's own rendering code.

### Stale/false Given/When/Then flagged, not rewritten

- `test_jsonlines_format`'s Given reads "Given CHECKED_BASH_LOGGING_FORMAT is set to jsonlines" -- the test body never sets that environment variable; it passes `log_format=LOG_FORMAT_JSONLINES` directly to `log_command()`. This Given describes a mechanism the test does not exercise (the legacy env-var path was removed; see `test_legacy_checked_bash_env_vars_are_ignored`, which correctly asserts that variable is now ignored). Left standing per the settled precedent -- flagging here rather than rewriting to match the assertions.
- `test_additional_context_in_markdown_format`'s Given reads "an additional_context short enough to fit within the preview budget." No preview/truncation budget exists anywhere in `log_writer.py` -- `additional_context` is written in full unconditionally (confirmed by reading `_render_markdown_entry`/`_build_jsonlines_entry`, and by the sibling test `test_long_additional_context_is_logged_in_full`, which explicitly asserts a 100-word string is NOT truncated). The class docstring this Given echoed made the same false claim and has been corrected (see below); the Given itself is left standing per precedent.

### False/dangling content found and corrected (docstrings, not GWT)

- `TestAdditionalContextLogging`'s class docstring claimed additional_context is "capped to a short preview, see TestPreviewAdditionalContext" -- both false: no truncation exists (see above), and no `TestPreviewAdditionalContext` class exists anywhere in this file or repo. Trimmed to one line with the false claim and dangling reference removed.
- `test_missing_log_dir_does_not_block_caller`'s docstring called itself "Superseding contract for test_log_directory_must_exist above" -- no test of that name exists in this file, in the current tree, or (checked via `git log -S 'def test_log_directory_must_exist'`) anywhere in this repo's tracked history. Removed; the substantive hazard it restated (SystemExit here would suppress the verdict entirely) is already stated once, correctly, in the immediately preceding test `test_missing_log_directory_warns_but_does_not_exit` -- one fact, one home.
- `TestPermissionModeLogging`'s class docstring asserted permission_mode "never affects the decision itself" -- a claim about `hook.py`'s decision logic, not this file's subject. The identical fact already lives in `hook.py` itself (verbatim, at the `permission_mode = hook_data.get(...)` call site). Cut per the standard's rule 0 (claims reaching outside their own file are where the falsehoods live; the fact belongs where it's implemented). Trimmed to a one-line, file-scoped docstring.
- `TestLogFormatGoldenFile`'s class docstring claimed `toolguard/tools/log_harvest.py and the audit skill parse` this log format -- an outward claim about other modules' behavior, unverifiable from this file and irrelevant to what the test itself proves. Cut; trimmed to one line stating only what the tests in this class actually pin (byte-for-byte field order and falsy-omission, under a fixed patched `datetime.now()`).
- `TestProvenanceLogging`'s class docstring pointed to "log_command's provenance parameter docstring" for why provenance is a separate field -- no such per-parameter "why" explanation exists in `log_command`'s docstring (it only lists field meanings via a pointer to `LogRecord`). Removed the dangling pointer; trimmed to a one-line, verified, self-contained statement.
- Both TOO-19-referencing regression-guard docstrings (`test_logging_enabled_by_default_without_a_config`, `test_legacy_checked_bash_env_vars_are_ignored`) had their phase/increment labels ("TOO-19 m5") and one unverifiable historical detail ("checked_bash.py-era") cut. The core hazard (a since-removed `CHECKED_BASH_LOGGING_ON` env var used to supply the no-config default; these tests guard against it silently coming back) is real -- confirmed via `git log -S CHECKED_BASH_LOGGING_ON` (present pre-TOO-19, absent after) -- and is kept, condensed to one sentence each.

### Comments kept as real-hazard notes

- `test_missing_log_directory_warns_but_does_not_exit`'s docstring ("Losing an audit record is bad; losing enforcement as well is worse. The hook writes its verdict AFTER logging, so a SystemExit here would suppress the verdict entirely...") kept verbatim. Recommend consolidating into one test in a future pass; out of scope here (comments only).

None. No enumerated-branches docstring, no long function needing per-branch commentary -- this file is flat `unittest.TestCase` methods and no helper functions of any complexity. Nothing to flag under this heading.

### Mutation testing

Repo copied via `rsync -a --exclude='.git'` to a scratch directory outside the tree; each mutation applied to the copy's `toolguard/log_writer.py`, full suite run, reverted before the next. Never touched the working tree (confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` after cleanup, and the copy was removed afterward).

1. **No-config logging default.** `_logging_enabled`'s no-config branch flipped from `return True` to `return False`. Result: 8 failures + 33 errors (floor is 2). Detected -- backs the two TOO-19 regression-guard docstrings.
2. **Provenance folded back into Matched Rule.** In both `_render_markdown_entry` and `_build_jsonlines_entry`, removed provenance's separate field and instead appended it to `matched_rule` (markdown) / dropped it (jsonlines) -- the exact stale shape this file's class docstring explicitly warns is wrong. Result: 2 failures + 5 errors (2 of the errors are the known `TestSmokeAgainstRealTree` floor from the mutant copy not being a git repo; the remaining 5 are all `TestProvenanceLogging`). Detected -- backs the corrected `TestProvenanceLogging` docstring.
3. **Missing log directory now exits instead of warning.** `_existing_log_dir_or_warn`'s `return None` after the warning changed to `sys.exit(1)`. Result: 4 errors + 3 failures, including both of this file's missing-log-dir tests, plus cascading failures in `test_sandbox.py` and `test_verdict_corpus.py` end-to-end tests -- concrete evidence for "losing enforcement as well is worse." Detected -- backs the kept hazard note.

All three mechanisms named in this file's kept prose are real and covered; none was found to mask another (each was mutated independently and each broke a distinct, attributable set of tests).

## REN -- test/unit/test_rule_entry.py (TOO-45 punch-list #07)

Line count: 1212 before, 1148 after (-64).

### Assertions that cannot fail as intended

- `TestMergeEntries.test_merge_conflict_is_a_frozen_dataclass_with_pattern_key_entries` ends with `with self.assertRaises(Exception): conflict.key = "somethingElse"`. Shape 6 from the brief: `Exception` is broad enough that a typo'd or renamed attribute (`AttributeError`) would pass the test identically to the intended `dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` (itself an `AttributeError` subclass) raised by frozen-dataclass immutability. The test currently distinguishes "raises for any reason" from "raises because the dataclass is frozen." Not fixed -- code change, out of scope for a comment sweep -- but flagged here as requested.
- `TestEntriesForTool.test_matches_exactly_startswith_and_endswith_semantics` claims (Then) to verify that `entries_for_tool` requires a pattern to both start with `"Bash("` and end with `")"`, using a decoy `BashOther(git *)`. That decoy fails at the very first four characters after `"Bash"` (`O` where `(` is required), so it only exercises the `startswith` branch -- identical in kind to the coverage `test_keeps_only_entries_matching_tool_wrapper` already provides via `"Read(/etc/*)"`. Confirmed empirically: mutation-removing the `.endswith(")")` half of `entries_for_tool`'s filter (`toolguard/rule_entry.py:486`) produced **zero** newly-failing tests in the full 2733-test suite (see Mutation testing below) -- the `endswith` branch has no test coverage anywhere in the repository, not just in this file. The docstring's own parenthetical justification for the weak fixture -- "no closing paren immediately at the pattern's own end is not representable for a wrapped pattern" -- is itself false: `normalize_entry` does not validate plain-string shape at all (confirmed by `test_plain_string_not_wrapper_shaped_still_normalizes` in the same file), so `normalize_entry("Bash(a)x", is_native=False)` produces a pattern that starts with `"Bash("` and does not end with `")"`, isolating exactly the case the docstring says can't be built. Per the settled precedent (don't launder a false Given/Then), the docstring text is left standing; flagged here with the refuting evidence. Recommend a follow-up test using a plain-string decoy like `"Bash(a)x"`.

No other vacuous-assertion shapes (1-5, 7-12) were found. Every other assertion in the file checks a real computed fact -- pattern/metadata/issue-level values produced by `normalize_entry`, `entries_for_tool`, `normalize_entries_preserving`, `merge_entries`, or `real_patterns` -- against an expectation that a different, plausible implementation would not also satisfy. `to_source()`'s identity checks (`assertIs`) and `merge_entries`' order/grouping assertions in particular are not both-sides-compute-the-same-thing: the test constructs inputs independently of the production code path being asserted on.

### Stale/false Given/When/Then flagged, not rewritten

- `TestEntriesForTool.test_matches_exactly_startswith_and_endswith_semantics` -- see above. The Given's parenthetical claim of non-representability is false; left standing per the settled precedent rather than rewritten to match the weaker thing the test actually does.

### False or overreaching claims found and corrected (docstrings, not GWT structure)

All of the following were trailing rationale clauses attached to an otherwise-true Given/When/Then, not the core scenario/outcome claim itself -- removing them is trimming a false or overreaching addendum, not laundering the test's own description of itself:

- `test_empty_string_is_rejected`: cut a paragraph claiming an empty string was "silently filtered out by the tool-prefix scan" pre-existing behaviour -- an unverifiable claim about other code's history, contradicting the guidance to verify or cut past-tense claims.
- `test_no_silent_drop_every_rejection_carries_an_issue`: cut "unlike the scattered isinstance(perm, str) checks this replaces" -- true per `git log -S 'isinstance(perm, str)'` (many prior sites, several removed in the commit that added `rule_entry.py`) but reaches outside the file and duplicates a fact already stated in `rule_entry.py`'s own module docstring; kept the in-file claim ("normalize_entry never silently drops an unusable element") instead.
- `test_does_not_strip_the_wrapper`: cut "stripping is the exclusive responsibility of permission_layers()'s own call site." Checked against `toolguard/config.py` and `toolguard/config_types.py`: `.stripped_pattern` is actually called from `ToolPatternLayer`'s derived properties (`config_types.py`), `permission_layers`' takeover-filter comparison, `resolve.py`, and `permissions.py` -- not exclusively from `permission_layers()`. False claim, reaches outside the file; removed.
- `test_set_of_entries_works_with_list_valued_metadata`: cut "proving the flat-primitive constraint on metadata values is genuinely gone, not just documented as gone." `git log -S 'flat-primitive' -- toolguard/ test/` shows this phrase entering the repo in the single commit that created `rule_entry.py` -- there is no prior state in tracked history where metadata actually was constrained to flat primitives and later loosened. Unverifiable past-tense claim; cut per "claims about the past."
- `test_to_source_round_trips_a_genuine_raw_none_value_faithfully`: cut a paragraph naming a specific pre-`_UNSET` regression ("TOO-19 Phase 0a... before the _UNSET sentinel existed..."). `git log -S 'is not None' -- toolguard/rule_entry.py` shows only one commit ever touched that pattern -- the file's creation commit -- so the described prior buggy state is not present in tracked history and can't be confirmed as it's told. Kept the hazard, dropped the history: replaced with a present-tense clause naming what `_UNSET` currently distinguishes.
- `test_all_elements_round_trip_through_to_source_faithfully`: cut "proving the write path never silently deletes or corrupts part of a user's config file" -- a single unit test asserting `to_source()` round-trips one fixture does not prove a system-wide guarantee about "the write path" (a claim spanning `config_write_guard.py`, `permission_migration.py`, etc.). Overreaching; cut.
- `test_synthesized_pattern_entry_is_excluded`: cut "(confirmed repro: passing it through wrongly refused every subsequent config write)" -- this test only calls `real_patterns()` directly; it does not exercise `verified_write_config` or reproduce any write-refusal loop, so attributing a "confirmed repro" to it misrepresents what the test does. Cut per "what a test does" guidance.

Also trimmed, with no accuracy concern (redundant with the function's own production docstring, or pure ticket/phase narrative): the `additionalContext`-known-key paragraph in `test_dict_with_valid_match_and_known_enrichment_key`; the "dropping the rule..." rationale paragraph in `test_non_string_value_reports_an_error_but_keeps_the_rule`; "unlike before increment 1" in `test_additional_context_no_longer_warns_as_an_unknown_key`; "so adding additionalContext did not disable the check" reworded to drop the past-tense framing in `test_an_unrelated_unknown_key_still_warns`; "after Phase 1" in `test_known_enrichment_keys_holds_additional_context_only`.

### The inert-deny lead (plain-string embedded newline)

Checked directly: no test in this file constructs `normalize_entry` on a **plain string** containing an embedded newline (e.g. `"Bash(rm -rf /)\nBash(dd *)"`). Two related, but different, tests exist:
- `TestNormalizeEntryStructured.test_dict_with_embedded_newline_in_match_is_rejected` covers a **structured** (`dict`) entry with an embedded newline in `match` -- correctly rejected, because structured entries are held to wrapper-shape validation.
- `TestIsToolWrapper.test_embedded_newline_does_not_match` covers `is_tool_wrapper()` directly, confirming it returns `False` for the newline-spliced string.

Neither exercises `normalize_entry` on a bare newline-bearing string, which (per `test_plain_string_not_wrapper_shaped_still_normalizes`, confirming plain strings skip wrapper-shape validation entirely) is exactly the path that accepts the lead's `"Bash(rm -rf /)\nBash(dd *)"` with no issue. Neither existing docstring frames the untested plain-string case as safe or intended -- `test_plain_string_not_wrapper_shaped_still_normalizes`'s Then talks about a bare unwrapped word (`"not-wrapped-at-all"`), not the newline-splicing shape, and is accurate about what it covers. So this is an absence of coverage, not a false or misleading Given/Then endorsing the defect -- no TOO-52-shaped docstring finding here. Not fixed (code change, out of scope). Recommend a new test in this file asserting `normalize_entry("Bash(rm -rf /)\nBash(dd *)", is_native=False)` returns an entry with no issues, as a named marker of the gap (or, better, a fix filed as its own ticket that then gets a real regression test).

### Two `test/unit/CLAUDE.md` citations corrected

Both sites (module docstring, and `TestMergeEntries`'s class docstring) cited `test/unit/CLAUDE.md`, which does not exist. Verified against `.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md`, which does carry the "no ConfigIsolationMixin needed when zero file I/O" checklist item these citations were pointing at. Both corrected to cite `.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md`.

### Comments kept as real-hazard notes

None found in this file warranting the carve-out beyond the class/helper one-line-docstring rule (also applied: 11 class docstrings trimmed to one line, none deleted). Six `#` body comments were deleted outright (test-tier rule): two explaining the "three genuinely unnormalizable elements" / `synthesized_pattern=True` bookkeeping in `test_unnormalizable_elements_are_preserved_not_dropped`, two narrating the manual dataclass-hash simulation in `test_dataclass_generated_hash_would_fail_on_list_metadata`, and one narrating the case-1 collapse in `test_group_and_entry_order_is_first_appearance_order`. None of these matched the `once_per_store._STORE_PATH`-style hazard carve-out (a warning against a real, undocumented-elsewhere corruption risk) -- they were all restatements of what the adjacent assertions already show.

### Comment-distribution signal

None. Flat `unittest.TestCase` methods throughout; no enumerated-branches docstring and no helper function of any complexity to flag under this heading.

### Mutation testing

Repo copied via `rsync -a --exclude='.git'` to a scratch directory outside the tree (no `git init` -- git write commands are restricted here, and `TestSmokeAgainstRealTree`'s 2 errors from the copy not being a git repo are the accepted environmental floor, confirmed against baseline before mutating). Each mutation applied to the copy's `toolguard/rule_entry.py`, full suite run, then the file restored from the real working tree's `HEAD` copy before the next mutation. Working tree confirmed unaffected throughout (`git status --short test/unit/test_rule_entry.py` showed only the intended edit at the end).

1. **DOTALL flip** (`_TOOL_WRAPPER_RE` gains `re.DOTALL`, defeating the embedded-newline defense for structured entries). Result: 2 new failures -- `TestIsToolWrapper.test_embedded_newline_does_not_match` and `TestNormalizeEntryStructured.test_dict_with_embedded_newline_in_match_is_rejected`. Detected.
2. **merge_entries conflict detection removed** (contradicting values silently overwrite instead of being flagged). Result: 3 new failures/errors in this file (`test_structured_entries_conflicting_value_stay_separate_and_reported`, `test_third_entry_sharing_an_already_conflicting_key_is_skipped`, `test_three_way_group_mixing_all_cases_resolves_correctly`, `test_merge_conflict_is_a_frozen_dataclass_with_pattern_key_entries`) plus 2 downstream (`test_tools_rule_apply.TestEnrichmentGuard`). Detected, and detected redundantly (not a single point of failure).
3. **real_patterns stops excluding synthesized-pattern entries.** Result: `test_synthesized_pattern_entry_is_excluded` fails in this file, plus 3 downstream failures/errors in `test_migration.py` and `test_tools_rule_apply.py`. Detected.
4. **normalize_entries_preserving drops unnormalizable elements instead of preserving them.** Result: `test_unnormalizable_elements_are_preserved_not_dropped` fails in this file, plus 2 downstream in `test_migration.py` and `test_tools_rule_apply.py`. Detected. (`test_all_elements_round_trip_through_to_source_faithfully` did not itself fail -- `zip()` silently truncates to the shorter sequence -- but the sibling test catches the mutation cleanly.)
5. **entries_for_tool's `.endswith(")")` half removed.** Result: **zero** new failures anywhere in the 2733-test suite. Confirms the coverage-gap finding above with certainty rather than inference.

Given the file's own docstrings never claim exclusive ownership of the newline-defense or merge-conflict mechanisms, no case of two mechanisms masking each other (brief's requirement 11) was found worth a joint mutation here.

### Leads rejected

None rejected outright. The coordinator's inert-deny lead was investigated and confirmed as a genuine coverage gap (see above) rather than a false or code-shape-drifting claim to reject.

- `uv run python tools/comment_hygiene.py --compare-against HEAD`: only `toolguard/__init__.py` and `tools/architecture_fitness.py` report (the accepted exceptions); `test/unit/test_rule_entry.py` reports nothing.
- `uv run ruff format --check test/unit/test_rule_entry.py`: already formatted.

## SST -- test/unit/test_session_start.py (TOO-45 punch-list #07)

Line count: 1328 before, 1268 after (-60).

### Assertions that cannot fail as intended

- `TestFormatSummary.test_broken_file_section_coexists_with_conflict_section` (Then: "the result contains both the broken-file section and the existing conflict-detected section, unmodified"). Confirmed by mutation: deleting the entire `broken_files` branch of `_format_summary` produces **zero** new failures in this test. The three assertions are `assertIn(str(broken), summary)`, `assertIn("configuration conflicts detected", summary)`, `assertIn("takeover_mode.enabled", summary)`. The fixture's broken-file path (`/proj/.claude/toolguard_hook.toml`) is the same literal string as the *conflict* fixture's project-level provenance path (`_make_conflict()`'s default source), so `str(broken)` is already present in the static-conflict provenance line the conflict section renders on its own -- the broken-files section is never actually exercised by this test. Shape: the fixture's own setup (reusing one path for two unrelated fixtures) provides an alternative route to the asserted string, masking the thing the Then claims to check. Not rewritten (code/fixture change, out of scope for a comment sweep); flagged here with the mutation evidence. `test_includes_broken_file_section_when_present`, by contrast, uses a path that does not collide and correctly fails under the same mutation.

No other vacuous-assertion shapes (1-12) were found. The remaining `_format_summary` and `_detect_*` tests check literal strings, tuple shapes, or object identities that a plausible alternative implementation would not also produce, and each was independently confirmed by a targeted mutation (below).

### Stale/false Given/When/Then flagged, not rewritten

None. Every surviving Given/When/Then was checked against its test body and matches what the test actually does, including the ones carrying extra clauses beyond the bare scenario (e.g. `test_empty_recent_file_does_not_shadow_older_entries`'s "the nag must persist until conflicts are actually cleared" -- verified true of `_check_dynamic_conflicts`'s walk-past-empty loop and left in place, since a test docstring's Then is allowed to state the outcome in the file's own terms).

### The lead: project-relative vs. the writer's log directory

Confirmed the divergence is real (read `toolguard/session_start.py` directly): `_detect_conflicts` computes `log_dir = project_root / "logs"`, using `config.project_root` -- `toolguard.config`'s own root-finder -- with no reference to `TOOLGUARD_LOG_DIR` or `toolguard.env_config`'s separate root-finder, which is what the PreToolUse hook's log writer actually honours.

Checked every test that builds a `log_dir` or asserts on `_detect_conflicts`/`_check_dynamic_conflicts` (`TestCheckDynamicConflicts`, `TestDetectConflicts`, and the two `TestMain` dynamic-conflict tests): all of them construct `project_root/logs` directly and none reference `TOOLGUARD_LOG_DIR` or `env_config`. **No Given/Then in this file frames the project-relative scan as matching the writer** -- each just describes what `_detect_conflicts` does ("Given a project_root whose logs/ directory has a conflict log with entries"), which is true of the code as written. So there is no false-Given/Then to flag under the TOO-52 shape here. There is, however, a genuine coverage gap worth naming: nothing in this file exercises `_detect_conflicts` with `TOOLGUARD_LOG_DIR` set to a directory different from `project_root/logs`, so the silent-never-fires divergence the coordinator described has no regression test either confirming or guarding it. Not fixed (code change, out of scope); recommend a follow-up test as a named marker of the gap.

### Second lead: "clamps every decision to ask"

Checked -- no test docstring in this file makes the unhedged "every decision" claim. `test_stdout_alert_when_config_broken` and `TestDetectBrokenConfigFiles`'s tests describe `_detect_broken_config_files` accurately (it returns `config.parse_failures` verbatim; the hedge about the already-`deny` exemption lives in `_detect_broken_config_files`'s own docstring in `session_start.py`, not duplicated here). Nothing to flag.

### Citations to nonexistent things

None found. Grepped for `test/unit/CLAUDE.md`, `TestPreviewAdditionalContext`, and `test_log_directory_must_exist` -- no hits in this file.

### Comments kept as real-hazard notes

None found warranting the carve-out beyond the class/helper one-line-docstring rule (10 class/helper docstrings trimmed to one line, none deleted, none newly added). Five ordinary `#` body comments were deleted outright (test-tier rule): a fixture-labeling comment in `TestRecentConflictLogs.test_returns_empty_when_no_conflict_logs` ("resolution log, not conflict"), a `# Default: no conflicts` label in `TestMain._run_main_with_stdin`, and three narrating comments in `TestSessionStartArgparseAndIsatty` (payload/stdin labels and an assertion label). None matched the `once_per_store._STORE_PATH`-style hazard shape (a warning against a real, undocumented-elsewhere corruption risk); all were restatements of what the adjacent code already shows.

Nine test docstrings and three class docstrings carried a `TOO-19`/`TOO-16`/"review fix" ticket-narrative prefix or paragraph (change history, in every case verified true but out of scope per the standard's "cut: ticket narrative" rule and "one fact, one home" -- the substantive facts, where true and non-historical, already live in `session_start.py`'s own docstrings). All cut; the surviving Given/When/Then in each was left intact and re-verified against the test body.

### Comment-distribution signal

None. Flat `unittest.TestCase` methods throughout; `_format_summary`'s five independent sections are covered by five separate, focused test classes rather than one enumerating test, so no comment-clustering signal here to flag under this heading.

### Mutation testing

Repo copied via `rsync -a --exclude='.git'` to a scratch directory outside the tree. Each mutation applied to the copy's `toolguard/session_start.py`, full suite (`test.unit.test_session_start`, 66 tests) run, then reverted by hand before the next mutation; working tree confirmed unaffected throughout (`diff` against the real `toolguard/session_start.py` showed zero difference after the last revert, and `git status --porcelain toolguard/session_start.py` in the real repo reflects only pre-existing, not-mine, changes from the module-level sweep already in progress on this branch).

1. **`_recent_conflict_logs` drops `reverse=True`** (ascending instead of most-recent-first). Result: 2 failures (`test_returns_conflict_logs_most_recent_first`, and one ordering assertion inside another test). Detected.
2. **`_count_conflict_entries` counts every `## ` heading, not just `- CONFLICT` ones.** Result: 1 failure (`test_does_not_count_non_conflict_headings`, 3 != 1). Detected.
3. **`_check_dynamic_conflicts` loses its `if count == 0: continue` walk-past-empty step.** Result: 2 failures (`test_returns_none_when_conflict_log_is_empty`, `test_empty_recent_file_does_not_shadow_older_entries`). Detected.
4. **`_detect_conflicts` hardcodes `log_dir = None`** (deletes the `project_root`-wiring the lead above is about). Result: 2 failures (`test_returns_dynamic_conflict_when_log_file_has_entries`, `test_stdout_summary_when_dynamic_conflict_present`). Detected -- confirms this mechanism itself is covered, even though the *writer-directory* variant is not (see the lead section above).
5. **`_format_summary` drops its `broken_files` section entirely.** Result: 2 failures (`test_includes_broken_file_section_when_present`, `test_stdout_alert_when_config_broken`) -- but **not** `test_broken_file_section_coexists_with_conflict_section` (see Assertions-that-cannot-fail above).
6. **`_format_summary` drops its `unrecognized_fallbacks` section entirely.** Result: 2 failures (`test_main_prints_the_warning_for_an_otherwise_clean_config`, `test_summary_names_value_setting_file_and_accepted_spellings`). Detected.
7. **`_detect_shadow_status` hardcodes `running_from_checkout = True`.** Result: 1 failure (`test_governing_copy_elsewhere_reports_not_running_from_checkout`). Detected.
8. **`main()`'s `sys.stdin.isatty()` guard replaced with `if False`.** Result: 1 failure (`test_isatty_true_prints_explanation_and_does_not_read_stdin`). Detected.

Two mechanisms could plausibly mask each other (the broken-files and unrecognized-fallback sections both append to the same `sections` list), but mutations 5 and 6 were each detected independently by tests that check only their own section's content, so no joint mutation was needed to find a masking pair.

### Leads rejected

None rejected outright. Both coordinator leads were investigated and found to hold no false Given/Then in this file (see the two lead sections above) -- correct outcomes, not rejections of the leads themselves.

### Stale/false Given/When/Then flagged, not rewritten -- one finding (coordinator Lead #2, confirmed)

`TestReassemblePermissionsSectionRoundTrip.test_comment_before_first_rule_stays_anchored_to_top_after_reorder`
(line 650) is the TOO-52 shape exactly as the coordinator predicted: **true about what the code
does, wrong about what it is for.** Its Then reads: "the comment block stays pinned at the top of
the list (anchored to the SECTION, not 'attached' and traveling with its original rule) -- this
matches the parser's own documented 'anchored to the top' behaviour for pre-first-rule comments,
**not a bug**." The assertion is accurate -- reassembling with `zebra`/`apple` swapped by sort does
leave the "# about the zebra rule" comment sitting above `apple`, exactly as `rule_sort.py`'s own
docstring (lines 452-458) specifies. But this is coordinator Lead #2's defect: a leading comment
silently re-attributes to whichever rule sorts into first place after a reorder, which is exactly
what happens here -- a comment about zebra now visually annotates apple. The Given/Then frames the
mis-attribution as intentional, correct behaviour rather than naming it as a defect. Per the
settled precedent (don't launder a false Given/Then to match the code), left the prose standing
and flag it here instead. **Not rewritten; not fixed** (code change, out of scope for a comment
sweep).

### Lead #1 (newline-breaks-TOML defect) -- confirmed real, and confirmed **zero coverage in this file**

Read `_escape_toml_string` (`rule_sort.py` lines 101-106): it calls `.replace("\\", "\\\\")` and
`.replace('"', '\\"')` only -- no escaping of a literal `\n`, confirmed by
`_render_toml_inline_table`'s own docstring admitting "a value containing a literal newline
produces the multi-line inline table the module docstring forbids." Real defect, exactly as
briefed.

**Coverage check via mutation** (see below, M2): stripping the quote-escaping half of
`_escape_toml_string` in an isolated copy produced **zero new failures in this file** -- the
mutation was caught only by `test_migration.py::TestTOMLConfigWriting::test_toml_escapes_special_chars`,
a different file. `TestRenderTomlEntry` and the round-trip tests in this file never construct an
`additionalContext` (or any string metadata value) containing a `"` or a `\n`, so this file has no
test that would catch either half of the escaping defect, including the newline half Lead #1
specifically names. This is a genuine coverage gap in the file under sweep; not fixed (code
change), flagged here as a candidate regression test:
`{"additionalContext": "line1\nline2"}` round-tripped through `render_toml_entry` /
`reassemble_permissions_section` and re-parsed with `tomllib.loads` to show it fails.

### Lead #3 ("ORIGINAL source line" -> "ORIGINAL source text") -- see above, not present

warning against a real, undocumented-elsewhere corruption/breakage risk). The deleted comments were
all either section banners, a redundant "last element, no trailing comma" label already covered by
its Then, or `# line N` counting aids inside `TestFindMultilineStructuredEntryLine` fixtures --
all derivable by counting the literal `\n`s in the adjacent strings, and re-verified accurate
(line 4 and line 6 respectively) before deletion.

### Mutation testing

Repo copied via `rsync -a --exclude='.git'` to a scratch directory outside the tree. Each mutation
applied to the copy's `toolguard/rule_sort.py`, full suite run, then reverted from a saved
`.orig` copy before the next mutation. Working tree confirmed unaffected (`git diff --stat` on
`toolguard/rule_sort.py` and `test/unit/test_rule_sort.py` showed only my own, real edit to the
test file; the scratch copy was removed after the run).

1. **`_ensure_trailing_comma` returns `same_line_tail` unmodified** (comma-insertion disabled).
   Result: 3 failures beyond the 2-error environmental floor --
   `test_comma_less_last_element_reemitted_with_comma_after_reorder`,
   `test_comma_less_last_element_with_inline_comment_stays_valid`,
   `test_comma_less_last_structured_entry_stays_valid`. Detected.
2. **`_escape_toml_string` drops its `.replace('"', '\\"')` half** (quote-escaping removed).
   Result: 0 new failures in this file (3 total errors = 2-error floor + 1, and that 1 is
   `test_migration.py`'s `test_toml_escapes_special_chars`, not anything here). **Zero detection
   in this file** -- confirms the Lead #1 coverage gap above.
3. **`_rule_pattern_of_value` returns `repr(value)` as a plain `str`** instead of wrapping it in
   `SyntheticPattern`. Result: 2 failures beyond floor --
   `test_malformed_structured_entry_parses_to_synthetic_pattern` (this file) plus a cascade into
   `test_tools_maintenance.py`'s `TestAnnotateMode.test_permission_patterns_in_text_excludes_malformed_entry`
   and `test_annotate_write_survives_a_malformed_structured_entry_in_the_file`. Detected.

Budget was 3; stopped there since each of the three targeted a distinct mechanism and mutation 2
already produced the coverage-gap finding the leads were fishing for. Lead #2's mechanism (comment
top-anchoring) was not separately mutated -- it is already directly, intentionally pinned by
`test_comment_before_first_rule_stays_anchored_to_top_after_reorder` (see the false-Given/Then
finding above), so mutating it would only reconfirm what that test already shows.

### Leads rejected

None. Both leads were confirmed real defects; Lead #2 additionally produced a false-Given/Then
finding, and Lead #1 additionally produced a mutation-confirmed coverage gap in this file. Lead #3
(the "ORIGINAL source line" wording) was checked and found not present here -- not a rejection,
just nothing to do.

  and `tools/architecture_fitness.py` report; `test/unit/test_rule_sort.py` reports nothing.
- `uv run ruff format test/unit/test_rule_sort.py`: reformatted (whitespace only, confirmed via
  `git diff --stat` showing no code-shape drift after); `uv run ruff check`: all checks passed.
- `uv run python -m unittest discover -s test -t .`: Ran 2733 tests, OK.

## CLS -- comment sweep of `test/unit/test_change_role_classifier.py` (2026-08-12)

Editor pass on the sole test file for `tools/change_role_classifier.py`. 1,734 -> 1,630 lines
(62 insertions, 165 deletions). No mocks or `assertRaises` anywhere in this file -- every test
drives the real AST/filesystem/git path, which is a meaningfully more robust style than most of
the files swept so far and shows in how few defects turned up.

### CLS: module coverage

`grep -rl 'change_role_classifier' test/ tools/ toolguard/` returns only this test file and the
source module itself; `tools/touch_set_inventory.py`'s one hit is a comment stating it
deliberately does NOT depend on the classifier. **This file is the module's only coverage**, same
situation as `test_touch_set_score.py`. Unlike that file, mutation testing here found the
detection rate good (3 of 5 mutations caught) -- see below.

### CLS: vacuous or self-satisfying tests (code changes, NOT made here)

| # | test | why the assertion cannot fail |
|---|---|---|
| **CLS1** | `test_rule_text_is_present_in_report` (`TestPathBasedTestVsProduction`) | `assertEqual(report["test_vs_production_rule"], crc.TEST_VS_PRODUCTION_RULE)` compares `build_report`'s copy of the constant against the SAME constant object (`tools/change_role_classifier.py:2144`: `"test_vs_production_rule": TEST_VS_PRODUCTION_RULE`, a verbatim assignment). This can only fail if the key is dropped or renamed (a `KeyError`); it cannot detect the constant's TEXT going stale or wrong, no matter how far it drifts from what `is_test_path` actually does. Category 4 from the brief ("both sides compute the same thing"), confirmed by reading `build_report`. |

### CLS: decoy fixtures confirmed by mutation testing

Two leads found by mutating `tools/change_role_classifier.py` in an out-of-tree copy
(`rsync --exclude='.git'`, full suite each run, floor = 2 errors from
`TestSmokeAgainstRealTree` needing a real git repo). Both are the same shape: a test whose
Given/Then claims to probe a boundary, but whose fixture never actually crosses it.

| # | test | mutation | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CLS2** | `test_distant_unrelated_ancestor_named_test_does_not_force_test` (`TestTestVsProductionAdversarialCases`) | `_ROOT_TEST_CONTEXT_WINDOW` widened from 4 to 400 (removes the bound the test claims to verify) | **Zero detection** (errors=2, no new failures). Cause: `TEST_DIR_NAMES = frozenset({"test", "tests", "spec", "specs"})` is matched by exact component equality (`_root_forces_test_context`: `any(part in TEST_DIR_NAMES for part in window)`). The fixture path is `/tmp/test_unrelated_ancestor_name/workspace/scratch/deep/candidate` -- `test_unrelated_ancestor_name` is never exactly `"test"`, at ANY distance, so the assertFalse holds regardless of window size. The test never exercises the "real match beyond the window" half of the claim it makes. A fixture that would: a path with a literal `test` component more than 4 trailing components from the end. |
| **CLS3** | `test_decoy_constant_with_similar_name_but_different_string_is_not_found` (`TestSymbolClosure`) | `CLOSURE_RULE_STRING_LITERAL`'s exact-equality check (`a.source_value in subject_set`) widened to substring (`any(s in a.source_value for s in subject_set)`) | **Zero detection** (errors=2, no new failures). Cause: the fixture's decoy value is `'not_the_subject'` against subject `"mode"` -- `"mode"` is not a substring of `"not_the_subject"` either, so exact-match and substring-match agree on this input and the test cannot tell them apart. Confirmed real with a direct probe (`compute_symbol_closure` on `A_KEY = 'the_mode_extra'`, subject `["mode"]`): mutated code admits `A_KEY` (`True`), original code correctly rejects it (`False`). The test's stated claim -- "matching is on the string value, never the constant's name" -- is real and true of the code, but this specific fixture doesn't test it; a value that CONTAINS the subject as a substring is what's missing. |

### CLS: other mutations (detected, no finding)

Budget was 5 (brief asks for at least 4). Three more, each targeting a distinct mechanism, all
caught: `_ROLE_PRECEDENCE` order swapped (DECISION/WRITE) -> 1 failure
(`test_primary_role_precedence`); `_governing_role`'s `Call.args`/`func`/`keywords` case changed
from `(_CONTINUE, ...)` back to the pre-fix terminal `frozenset({ROLE_CONDUIT})` (the F1
regression, reintroduced) -> 8 failures across `TestDualRoleThroughCallsAndAttributes`;
`_find_opaque_hops_in_scope` short-circuited to always return `[]` -> 4 failures across
`TestOpaqueHops`/`TestOpaqueHopsReturnScanning`. The real repo was never touched --
`git diff --stat tools/change_role_classifier.py` before and after mutation testing shows only
the pre-existing, unrelated concurrent-sweep diff from another agent's work, not anything from
this pass; the mutated copy was rsynced outside the project and removed afterward.

### CLS: stale or false Given/Then, flagged not rewritten (settled precedent)

- **`test_distant_unrelated_ancestor_named_test_does_not_force_test`** -- Given claims `"test"
  appearing only as a DISTANT ancestor well outside the bounded trailing window`. Per CLS2, no
  component in the fixture ever equals `"test"` at any distance; the claim describes a scenario
  the fixture does not create. Left standing per the settled precedent against laundering a false
  Given/Then into what the test actually does; only the surrounding protocol-narrative paragraph
  (coordinator's N7 finding) was cut.

### CLS: false citation found and corrected

- **`TestSetLiteralIsTransparent`'s class docstring** cited `toolguard/rule_entry.py`'s
  `KNOWN_ENRICHMENT_KEYS = frozenset({ADDITIONAL_CONTEXT_KEY, ALLOW_IN_AUTO_MODE_KEY})` --
  **two members**. `grep -n KNOWN_ENRICHMENT_KEYS toolguard/rule_entry.py` shows
  `KNOWN_ENRICHMENT_KEYS = frozenset({ADDITIONAL_CONTEXT_KEY})`, **one member**;
  `ALLOW_IN_AUTO_MODE_KEY` does not appear there. Corroborates the CRC section's finding
  (queue line ~1125, CRC6) that a related fabricated citation involving `ALLOW_IN_AUTO_MODE_KEY`
  exists elsewhere in this same file family. The wrong enumeration was cut; the true, verified
  fact (an `ast.Set` bug found on that real frozenset) was kept.

### CLS: hazard note removed, verified false

- **`test_permission_denied_is_reported_not_crashed`**'s inline comment `# restore so tempdir
  cleanup can remove it` on the `target.chmod(0o644)` restore. Verified false by direct
  execution: `shutil.rmtree` on a directory containing a `chmod(0o000)` file succeeds WITHOUT
  restoring permissions first (POSIX: unlink permission comes from the containing directory, not
  the file). Deleted per the test-tier rule regardless (all non-G/W/T comments are cut), but
  flagged here because it is the kind of claim the standard singles out for verification before
  being kept, and it would have been kept-as-a-hazard under a less strict reading.

### CLS: protocol narrative cut

Every class and method docstring carrying "coordinator's F#/N# finding (second review)",
"adversarial report/review", "Requirement N", or "TOO-45 validation trees" language had that
narrative stripped, keeping only the factual regression description (class docstrings, trimmed to

`TestDualRoleThroughCallsAndAttributes`, `TestStatementSpanLineFiltering`,

(`test_subject_as_keyword_argument_value_in_branch_test_reaches_decision`,
`test_accumulated_role_with_unhandled_parent_is_surfaced_in_occurrences_with_notes`,
`test_predicate_function_call_sites_now_register_decision`,
`test_byte_identical_move_reports_no_occurrences`,

## SEC -- comment sweep of `test/unit/test_tools_security_audit.py` (2026-08-12)

Editor pass on the sole test file for `toolguard/tools/security_audit.py` (the `toolguard-audit`
aggregator). 2,103 -> 1,989 lines (29 insertions, 143 deletions). Module docstring cut from a
23-line bulleted "tests cover" list plus an outward "mirrors test_tools_danger.py /
test_tools_takeover_audit.py" claim (banned by rule 1) down to one line, matching the sibling
files' own already-swept one-liners. Every `_xxx_config()` fixture-builder docstring (7 of them)
condensed from a bulleted multi-line form to one dense sentence, following the exact style already
accepted in `test_tools_takeover_audit.py`'s concurrent sweep (`_toolguard_layer`'s docstring is
copied near-verbatim from that file, since the two are the same helper). Two internal `"S2:"` /
`"S3:"` stage-label prefixes on class docstrings cut as phase/increment labels (CLAUDE.md's "cut"
list). All non-G/W/T inline comments deleted (14 sites); section-divider comments kept per the
sibling files' established practice, with the one `(TOO-19)` ticket reference inside a divider
stripped.

### SEC: a genuine assertion-that-cannot-fail, confirmed by mutation and direct reproduction

| # | test | why the assertion cannot fail |
|---|---|---|
| **SEC1** | `test_sorted_severity_descending` (`TestSecurityAuditMixed`) | Asserts `values == sorted(values, reverse=True)` where `values` are the `severity_value`s of `_mixed_config()`'s findings. Reproduced `_mixed_config()` directly against `security_audit()`: it always yields findings `[('rule', 'arbitrary-exec-allow', 4), ('rule', 'arbitrary-exec-allow', 4), ('takeover', 'hook-not-registered', 4)]` -- **every finding is CRITICAL (4)**. `sorted([4,4,4], reverse=True) == [4,4,4]` regardless of the list's actual order, so this assertion holds for any permutation, including one produced by a broken or absent sort. Category 3/9 from the brief ("every alternative also produces this value" / vacuous control). Confirmed by mutation: flipping `-f.severity_value` to `f.severity_value` in `security_audit()`'s sort key (ascending instead of descending) produced **zero new test failures** anywhere in the 2733-test suite. The sibling test in the same class, `test_same_severity_sorted_source_then_id`, is NOT affected -- it filters to `severity_value == 4` first and checks the source/id tie-break among that subset, which is a real check independent of the primary key's direction, and it already self-guards against vacuousness with `assertGreaterEqual(len(critical), 2)`. Not fixed here (comments-only ticket); a real fix needs a fixture with findings at two different severities (e.g. add a `[regex]` unanchored-allow finding at MEDIUM alongside the two CRITICALs).

### SEC: other mutations (detected, no finding)

Budget was 4 (brief asks for at least 4), targeting `security_audit()`'s own aggregation logic --
this file is the only test coverage for that module, and ticket 21's detector-level gaps
(`danger.py`) are not reachable through this file's fixtures, which never construct `[native]*`,
DEFAULT `**`, or the destructive-command forms ticket 21 names dead. Three of four caught real
regressions: removing `f.acknowledged` from the sort key's first element -> 1 failure
(`test_acknowledged_finding_is_deprioritized_below_unacknowledged`); swapping `introduced`/
`resolved` in `_finding_delta`'s return -> 4 failures across `TestEditsReview` and
`TestFindingDeltaAndBanner`; `_acknowledgement_label` short-circuited to always return the bare
`"#NOSECURITY"` -> 3 failures in `TestAcknowledgementRendering`. The fourth (severity-sort
direction) is SEC1 above. The real repo was never touched -- `git diff --stat` on
`toolguard/tools/security_audit.py` before and after mutation testing showed only the pre-existing,
unrelated concurrent-sweep diff from another agent's work (last touched 2026-08-11, per `stat`),
not anything from this pass; the mutated copy was rsynced outside the project (`/tmp/claude-*/.../
scratchpad/mut_copy/`) and removed afterward.

### SEC: leads from the coordinator brief, checked and mostly not applicable to this file

- **Ticket 21 (danger analyzer coverage gaps)**: the one claim this file makes that ticket 21
  bears on -- `test_finding_id_matches_detector` expecting `arbitrary-exec-allow` to fire for
  `'uv run python:*'` -- is one of the *working* cases per ticket 21 ("only `uv run python`, `sh
  -c` and `bash -c` do any work"), so the test is accurate, not a false coverage claim. No other
  test in this file asserts a specific danger category fires; the rest of ticket 21's dead
  categories (blanket-allow bypasses, `rm -r`, `dd`, prefixed commands) are exercised in
  `test_tools_danger.py`, not here. **Rejected as inapplicable to this file.**
- **Ticket 22 (redundancy analyzers report unsafe deletions as safe)**: this file has no import
  of, or fixture resembling, `hierarchy.py` or `redundancy.py` (`grep -n
  "hierarchy\|redundancy" test/unit/test_tools_security_audit.py` -- zero hits). `security_audit()`
  does not aggregate those two engines' findings at all (only danger, takeover, clarity,
  environment -- confirmed by reading `security_audit()`'s body). **Rejected as inapplicable.**
- **`uninstall_readiness.py`'s "literal, single-purpose" over-claim (ticket 18 lead)**: this file
  never imports or tests `uninstall_readiness.py`. **Rejected as inapplicable.**

### SEC: hazard note kept, verified true

- **`setUpModule`'s PYTHONPATH-isolation docstring.** Verified against source, not just trimmed:
  `security_audit()` calls `audit_environment(env)` (default `None`); `audit_environment` forwards
  to `pythonpath_shadow_entries(env)`, whose docstring and default both confirm it reads real
  `os.environ` when `env` is `None`. A test in this module that omits `env=` therefore genuinely
  depends on the real machine's `PYTHONPATH` without this module-wide `patch.dict` pop. Kept,
  trimmed from 17 lines to 6.

### SEC: no false or defect-framed-as-intended Given/Then found

Read every G/W/T in the file against the code it exercises (severity/label mappings, the
`_danger_proposal` remove/anchor branches, the `#NOSECURITY` acknowledgement de-prioritization,
the JSON field set, the environment/clarity severity hardcodes). All checked true, including two

accurate: `TestSecurityAuditClarity`'s "clarity findings are LOW severity" (hardcoded in
`security_audit()`, by design, with its own justifying comment) and
`TestNoSecurityAcknowledgement`'s "acknowledges... but never hides" (confirmed: acknowledged
findings still appear in `report.findings`, only de-prioritized in sort order). No instance of a
comment calling a defect "not a bug" found in this file.

`test_appears_in_json_output`'s docstring) both point at the real file at
`.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md`. No reference to the nonexistent `test/unit/CLAUDE.md`
found (`grep -n "test/unit/CLAUDE.md" test/unit/test_tools_security_audit.py` -- zero hits).

## MIG -- comment sweep of `test/unit/test_migration.py` (TOO-45 punch-list #07, 2026-08-12)

Lines: 2820 -> 2600. All comments deleted except Given/When/Then and three verified hazard
notes (below); class/helper docstrings trimmed to one line or near it, ticket/phase narrative
and duplicated implementation-side rationale cut.

### MIG: the `_escape_toml_string` lead -- confirmed, and turned into a measurement

`test_toml_escapes_special_chars` (`TestTOMLConfigWriting`) is the only test in this file that
exercises `toolguard.rule_sort._escape_toml_string`, and only through the quote-escaping path:
it writes a plain pattern containing `"` via `write_toml_config()` against a config file that
doesn't yet exist, which routes through `generate_permissions_section` ->
`render_toml_entry` -> `_render_toml_scalar` -> `_escape_toml_string`, and asserts the embedded
quote comes out escaped. No test in this file constructs a pattern or `additionalContext`
containing a literal newline. Confirms the finding from the migration side (RSO section above
made the same finding from `test_rule_sort.py`'s side).

Turned into a measurement: mutating `_escape_toml_string` to ALSO escape `\n` (i.e. applying the
fix for the newline defect) produces **zero test failures anywhere in the 2733-test suite**
beyond the 2-error environmental floor. Nothing pins the current unescaped-newline behaviour, so
either fixing it or silently breaking it further would go unnoticed.

### MIG: mutation results (4 mutations, `rsync`-ed copy outside the tree, removed after)

1. `_escape_toml_string` -> return the value unmodified (no escaping at all): 16 failures beyond
   the floor, including `test_toml_escapes_special_chars` here and 12 in
   `test_tools_installer.py`. Detected.
2. `_escape_toml_string` -> also escape `\n` (see above): 0 failures beyond the floor.
   **Zero detection.**
3. `create_backup`'s collision disambiguator (the `while backup_path.exists()` loop) deleted: 2
   failures, both in this file (`test_colliding_timestamp_does_not_overwrite_earlier_backup`,
   `test_third_collision_on_same_timestamp_gets_its_own_file`). Detected.
4. `migrate()`'s `file_lock.exclusive()` acquisition bypassed (lock never taken): 4 failures, all
   in this file (2 in `TestMigrationLocking`, 2 in `TestMigrationLockingAcrossProcesses`).
   Detected.

### MIG: assertions that cannot fail meaningfully

- `TestSimilarPatternDetection.test_identical_pattern_not_similar` asserts only
  `assertIsInstance(similar, list)`. `detect_similar_patterns()` returns a list on every code
  path for this input (nothing raises), so the assertion tests "didn't crash," not "handles the
  identical-pattern case gracefully" as the docstring's Then claims -- it is satisfied by any
  list, including a wrong one.
- `TestImprovedSimilarityDetection.test_similarity_returns_ranked_results` wraps all three real
  assertions in `if len(similar) > 0:`. If `detect_similar_patterns()` ever returned an empty
  list for this fixture, the test would pass having checked nothing about ranking or ordering --
  the property named in the Then is never actually pinned.

### MIG: hazard notes -- kept, each verified before keeping

- "migrate() prints progress to stdout; the exit code this script prints is always its last
  line" (`TestMigrationLockingAcrossProcesses`): verified against `permission_migration.py` --
  `migrate()`/`_run_migration()` call `print()` at 30+ sites. Kept, lightly trimmed.
- "Migration only moves rules for governed tools, so this config must govern Read..."
  (`TestMigration.test_migration_adds_to_existing_toml`): verified against
  `_find_skipped_ungoverned_patterns()`, which filters native patterns by
  `extract_tool_name(pattern) not in governed` -- true. Dropped the "issue #1" label, kept the
  fact.
- "project_root IS home here; the mixin's default project is home's sibling, so
  find_project_root must be re-pointed" (`TestMigrationTargetLevel`): verified against
  `_config_isolation.py`'s `isolate_config_environment()`, which creates `project` as `home`'s
  sibling by default. Kept, trimmed.

No hazard note in this file was found false or removed.

### MIG: false/vague Given-Then

None found. All docstring trims removed ticket narrative or implementation-side rationale
duplicated elsewhere (e.g. the "W1 headline regression guard" paragraph, whose claim about

docstrings). Kept zero hazard-note comments -- everything that named a hazard duplicated its
own test's Given/Then verbatim in meaning (e.g. the "Critical: rm -rf / MUST be extracted"
comments), so nothing was lost by deleting them; the hazard survives in the docstring, which
is exactly what the test-tier rule expects. `_leaf`/`_leaf_and_resolve` test helpers had no
docstrings before and still don't -- the carve-out says not to add missing ones.

### CMP: assertions that cannot fail (12 of 223 tests), confirmed by execution

`extract_commands`'s own docstring says it "FAILS OPEN": when the grammar rejects the input or
extraction raises anything, the whole line comes back as `[command_line.strip()]` -- a
length-1 list. Ran all twelve fixtures through `extract_commands` directly (not inferred from
reading): every one triggers a real `ParseError` and returns exactly the one-element fail-open
list. Because both the success path (a real command was extracted) and the failure path
(fail-open) produce `len(result) >= 1`, `assertGreater(len(result), 0)` cannot distinguish
correct behaviour from the safety net -- Shape 3 from the sweep's list, verified rather than
assumed:

- `TestEdgeCases.test_consecutive_operators` -- `"cmd1 && && cmd2"`, fail-open, len 1.
- `TestEdgeCases.test_leading_operator` -- `"&& git status"`, fail-open, len 1.
- `TestEdgeCases.test_trailing_operator` -- `"git status &&"`, fail-open, result is exactly
  `["git status &&"]`. The test's `assertTrue("git status" in result or "git status &&" in
  result, ...)` passes only through the second disjunct (`"git status" in result` is a list
  membership check against a single non-matching element, so it is False) -- also
  cannot-fail, given the fail-open contract.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_unmatched_open_paren` -- `"(cmd"`, fail-open, len 1.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_unmatched_close_paren` -- `"cmd)"`, fail-open, len 1.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_unmatched_dollar_paren` -- `"echo $(cmd"`, fail-open, len 1.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_unmatched_backtick` -- `` "echo `cmd" ``, fail-open, len 1.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_unmatched_brace` -- `"{ cmd"`, fail-open, len 1.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_only_operators` -- `"&& || ; |"`, fail-open, len 1 -- and this one
  has **no assertion at all**, only the bare function call. The weakest test in the file: it
  can only fail via an uncaught exception.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_very_deep_nesting` -- 10-deep `(((...pwd...)))` actually parses
  successfully (11 elements, confirmed) rather than failing open, but `assertGreater(len, 0)`
  is still guaranteed regardless of which path runs.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_mixed_very_deep_nesting` -- `"($(($(($(pwd)))))"`, fail-open,
  len 1.
- `TestParserRobustness.test_whitespace_only_subshell` -- `"(   )"`, fail-open, len 1.

Each test's own Given/Then already describes exactly this weak check ("does not crash and
returns at least one command") -- the docstrings are not false, the assertions are just
tautological given the documented fail-open contract. Per the settled precedent, left standing
rather than rewritten to something more specific (which would require deciding what the
*correct* graceful-degradation shape should be, which is a code decision, not a comment one).

### CMP: parser-idiom reliance

Zero tests in this file use any of the three `test_bash_parser.py` idioms
(`assertIsNotNone(tree)`, `hasattr(node, label)`, `assertIn("$(", tree.text)`) -- this file
calls `parse_command_line`/`extract_commands`/`get_command_breakdown`/
`check_compound_permission`, never `bash_parser.Parser.parse` directly, so there is no `tree`
object to smoke-test. Its own weak idiom is the fail-open tautology above instead.

### CMP: per-part-matching claims against ticket 19 -- none found, one coverage gap flagged

Grepped every Given/Then for unqualified per-part-matching or full-decomposition claims;
found none. The one `while`/`if`-`else` test
(`TestUndecidableFallbackSegment.test_complex_control_structure_under_each_fallback`) claims
the construct is "a second undecidable shape, distinct from process substitution" -- verified
by running `extract_structured` directly: a `while` loop with a *nested control structure* in
its body genuinely returns an `UndecidableSegment` with reason `"loop with nested control
structure cannot be statically decomposed"`, a different code path from ticket 19's P1. Ran
`extract_structured('while rm -rf /tmp/x; do :; done')` for comparison and confirmed it takes
the OTHER path P1 describes (`LeafCommand(text=':')`, condition silently dropped, classified
`'plain'` not `'undecidable'`). So this test's claim is accurate and does not exercise P1.

**Gap, not a false claim**: this file -- the natural home for compound-decomposition tests --
has zero coverage of ticket 19's P1 (a bare `while`/`until` whose *condition*, not its body,
carries the dangerous command). Ticket 19's own "Test obligation" section calls for exactly
this. Flagging per the ticket's instruction not to write the test in this pass.

Lines: 3340 -> 3185. Deleted all pure-comment and trailing `# ...` lines except the ones
flagged as hazard notes below; trimmed 15 multi-line class/module/helper docstrings to one
paragraph each, cutting ticket/phase labels (`TOO-15`, `TOO-19`, `TOO-45 R1e`, `TOO-45 R3`,
`TOO-45 punch-list #04/#10`, etc.) while keeping every load-bearing fact those labels were

### HKM: the fail-open lead (`proposed-tickets/23`) -- confirmed uncovered, by mutation

`error_log.log_crash` builds `errors_dir = Path.home() / ".toolguard" / "errors"` above its
own `try` (verified: `toolguard/error_log.py:142`). `hook.main()`'s three `except` clauses
call `log_crash(...)` before `_report_crash_fault`/`_emit_decision` (verified:
`toolguard/hook.py:1311/1322/1335`, all three calls precede the `_emit_decision` call in the
same clause). No test in this file, or found via grep of `pathlib.Path.home` in this file,
ever makes `Path.home()` raise -- every crash-path test patches it to a valid tmpdir. So no
test drives the scenario where `log_crash` itself fails.

**Mutation probe** (per the brief: make `log_crash` raise, see whether anything notices stdout
went empty). In an `rsync`-ed copy outside the tree, replaced
`errors_dir = Path.home() / ".toolguard" / "errors"` with an unconditional `raise OSError(...)`
and ran the full suite: 9 new errors beyond the 2-error environmental floor (baseline 2733/2,
mutated 2733/11), 3 of them in this file's `TestHookCrashCapture`
(`test_unexpected_exception_writes_crash_report`, `test_json_decode_error_writes_crash_report`,
`test_value_error_missing_field_writes_crash_report`), the rest in `test_error_log.py` and
`test_hook_error_reporter.py`.

**But the detection is incidental, not targeted.** Traceback inspection of the failures in this
file shows `main()` itself raises the injected `OSError` uncaught -- it propagates straight
through `with self.assertRaises(SystemExit): main()`, which fails the test only because
`OSError != SystemExit`, not because any assertion checked stdout content (the test body never
reaches its own `assertNotIn`/`assertTrue(stdout_text.strip(), ...)` lines -- the exception
fires before them). In production this is exactly the filed bug: `main()` exits via Python's
default uncaught-exception handler, prints a traceback to stderr, and **stdout carries no
decision at all** -- the exact silent fail-open the ticket describes. No test anywhere in this
file has an assertion of the shape "stdout still carries a verdict when log_crash itself
fails" -- the distinction the brief asked to check for. Confirmed rather than assumed:
zero-detection-of-the-actual-property, positive-detection-of-a-different-symptom.

**Control mutations** (to confirm the harness itself works and this file's coverage is real,
not that the suite is broadly broken): reverted M1, then ran three more, each in the same
isolated copy, each reverted and confirmed via `git diff`/`grep` against the real tree after
every run:
- M2: `_emit_decision`'s `print(json.dumps(output))` replaced with `pass` -- 8 failures + 47
  errors beyond the floor (widespread, as expected: this breaks the one place every decision
  reaches stdout).
- M3: `create_hook_output`'s `if verdict.additional_context:` replaced with `if False:` -- 4
  failures + 6 errors, including `TestHookOutput.test_non_empty_additional_context_is_included_inside_hook_specific_output`
  and both `TestAdditionalContextThroughMain` tests. Detected.
- M4: `hook.FILE_PATH_TOOLS` hardcoded to drop `"Write"` -- caught by
  `TestFilePathTools.test_file_path_tools_constant` and
  `TestFilePathToolsInMain.test_write_tool_asks_on_no_match_by_default`, plus two
  `test_verdict_corpus.py` tests outside this file. Detected.

So: 4 mutations run, all four "detected" by some failure signal, but only 3 of the 4 detect the
property they were mutating *on purpose* -- M1's 3 in-file failures detect "log_crash is now
broken", not "stdout still carries a decision when log_crash fails", which is the actual
question and remains unanswered/uncovered. No comment was added to the test file pointing this
out (comments-only rule, and the standard says not to add speculative content); this section
is the record.

### HKM: settled-precedent false Given/Then -- flagged, left standing

`TestHookCrashCapture.test_unexpected_exception_writes_crash_report`'s Then clause claims
"stderr is empty". False: the test's own assertion is
`self.assertNotIn('"permissionDecision"', mock_stderr.getvalue())` -- narrower than "empty" --
and the adjacent (kept, verified) comment on the same lines explains why: `log_crash`/`log_error`
unconditionally echo `"[CRASH]"`/`"[ERROR]"` lines to stderr as part of writing (verified:
`toolguard/error_log.py:100,176`). The other two tests in the same class with near-identical
Then clauses (`test_json_decode_error_writes_crash_report`,
`test_value_error_missing_field_writes_crash_report`) do NOT make this "stderr is empty" claim
-- only this one test's prose is wrong. Per the settled precedent, left the prose standing
rather than rewriting it to match the code; ticket-label trimming was still applied elsewhere in
the same class where the prose was accurate.

### HKM: assertion that cannot fail (shape #10 -- guarded, never taken)

`TestStartupValidation.test_validation_ignores_settings_local_json`: every real assertion
("WebSearch"/"WebFetch"/"mcp__unknown__tool" not in the log content) is nested inside
`if log_files:`, with no assertion that `log_files` is non-empty and no else branch. Traced
`Configuration.validation_issues()` (`toolguard/config.py:1688-1711`): permission validation
iterates `self.layers` and explicitly does `if layer.is_native: continue`, so the
`settings.local.json` native layer this test builds is skipped entirely, and the
`toolguard_hook` layer's config (`Bash`/`Read`, valid patterns) produces zero issues. So
`_run_startup_validation` never calls `log_warning`/`log_error`, no log file is ever written,
`log_files` is always `[]`, and the guarded block never executes. The test currently asserts
nothing beyond "does not crash." Not fixed (code/test-design change, out of scope for a
comments-only pass) -- flagged here per the primary-deliverable instruction.
