### RSV: assertions checked against the 14 shapes -- no defects found, one type-check noted as benign

Read every assertion in the file against the shape list, with special attention to shape 11
(satisfied by a fail-open floor rather than the mechanism under test) per the brief's steer that
`resolve.py` sits on the floor/fallback path. None of the weak-looking candidates held up as
real defects on inspection:

- `TestNoDrift`'s `self.assertIsInstance(bash_result, RuntimeVerdict)` calls are shape-9-shaped
  (the function's return annotation already guarantees the type), but each is immediately

  `violated_rules == [result.reason]` looked like shape 4 (both sides computing the same value)
  until traced against `hook.py::_log_non_allow_decision`'s deny branch (lines ~905-953): the
  `[reason]` fallback only fires on the conditional path where `matched_rule is None` and the
  reason matches no recognised escape-hatch marker -- a real branch, not an unconditional
  identity, so the assertion can fail (e.g. if a colon-containing reason took the wrong branch).

  "every decision is clamped" universal (checked per the brief's known-wrong pattern); every
  place this file discusses the floor scopes the claim to "the outer command's resolution" or

scratchpad directory outside the tree; created an empty `logs/` there too (excluded by rsync,
otherwise adds 2 environmental failures of its own). Baseline: 2733/OK. Each mutation applied to

then reverted via `cp` from a saved pristine copy and `diff`-confirmed clean before the next:

- **M1** (floor interaction): deleted the compound-boundary re-application of
  `apply_parse_failure_floor` in `resolve_bash_permission_detailed` (the fix for the
  `UndecidableSegment`-bypasses-the-per-leaf-floor bug this file's
  `TestParseFailureFloorCoversUndecidableSegments` class targets). 4 failures: both of that
  class's core tests in this file, plus 2 in `test_verdict_corpus.py`. Detected.
- **M2** (more-specific-wins / floor interaction, the already-deny exemption): removed the
  `or decision == "deny"` clause from `apply_parse_failure_floor`, so a broken config would clamp
  an already-deny decision to 'ask' too. 5 failures, including this file's
  `test_broken_config_undecidable_segment_stays_deny_under_deny_fallback` (the exact test
  guarding the exemption) plus 4 in `test_verdict_corpus.py`. Detected.
- **M3** (undecidable_fallback threading): hardcoded the fallback passed to `judge_unit` in
  `resolve_bash_permission_detailed` to `"ask"`, ignoring `config.resolved_undecidable_fallback()`.
  44 failures across 6 files, 26 of them in this file (most of
  `TestUndecidableFallbackThreading`, `TestUndecidableFallbackMultiLeafWarningParity`,
  `TestAuditLogMatchedRuleNeverFabricated`, `TestAuditLogViolatedRuleNeverFabricated`,
  `TestFallbackWarningField`, `TestParseFailureFloorCoversUndecidableSegments`). Detected, hard.
- **M4** (hard-deny-first): passed an empty list instead of `hard_deny_deny` into `check_hard_deny`

**Mutation testing.** `rsync`-ed the repo (excluding `.git`) to a scratchpad directory outside the
tree; baseline `python -m unittest test.unit.test_tools_installer`: 127/OK. Six mutations applied
to `toolguard/tools/installer.py` one at a time, reverted via `cp` from the real repo between each
(confirmed via `git status --porcelain -- toolguard/tools/installer.py` on the real tree showing
only its own pre-existing, pre-session changes -- never touched by this mutation run):

- **M1**: dropped `shlex.quote()` from `_hardened_hook_command`'s interpreter path. 2 failures,
  both in `TestHardenedHookCommandSpacePathQuoting`, both for the right reason (traceback shows
  the quoting assertion itself failing, not a downstream effect). Detected.
- **M2**: made `_skip_env_wrapper` an unconditional no-op. 3 failures, all in
  `TestSkipEnvWrapper`, for the right reason. **But `test_env_wrapped_hardened_command_with_existing_interpreter_is_not_missing`
  (`TestHookRegistrationFindingsInterpreterIdentification`) did NOT fail**, despite its Then
  explicitly claiming to test this exact mechanism ("token 0 being 'env' (the wrapper), not the
  interpreter, must not be misread as missing"). Re-ran that test alone under the mutation: still
  passes. Cause: with `_skip_env_wrapper` neutered, token 0 stays `"env"`, and `env` itself is a
  real, `PATH`-resolvable binary on any POSIX dev/CI box, so `_interpreter_missing` reports
  `False` anyway -- for the wrong reason. **Confirmed instance of shape #14** ("the Then names one
  mechanism, a different one produces the outcome"). Queued below.
- **M3**: removed `candidates.sort(...)` from `cmd_discover_projects`. 1 failure,
  `test_output_sorted_by_path`, right reason (traceback shows the literal unsorted vs. sorted
  list mismatch). Detected.
- **M4** (fix-reversion, per "mutate in both directions where a fix is known"): reverted
  `cmd_seed_self_perms`'s membership check from `_entry_pattern(entry)` back to a naive
  `isinstance(entry, str)` filter -- the exact pre-fix bug `TestSeedCommandsWithStructuredEntries`
  exists to guard against. 2 failures (`test_seed_self_perms_does_not_duplicate_a_structured_self_permission`
  and, as a side effect, `TestSeedSelfPerms.test_running_twice_does_not_duplicate_rules`), right
  reason. Detected -- the regression guard works.
- **M5**: bypassed `verified_write_config` in `cmd_seed_hard_deny` with a raw `_atomic_write_text`
  call. 1 failure, `test_seed_hard_deny_refuses_write_that_would_drop_existing_pattern`, right
  reason (exit code 0 where non-zero was expected -- the write guard's absence, not the mocked
  renderer, is what the assertion catches). Detected -- this test exercises the real wiring, not
  just the renderer mock.
- **M6**: same naive-membership reversion as M4, applied to `cmd_seed_hard_deny`'s
  `existing_deny_patterns` instead. 1 failure, `TestSeedHardDeny.test_running_twice_is_idempotent_noop`
  (journal gained an unexpected 3rd entry on the second run). Detected, but **only by the general
  idempotency test** -- there is no `test_seed_hard_deny_does_not_duplicate_a_structured_pattern`
  sibling to `test_seed_self_perms_does_not_duplicate_a_structured_self_permission` in
  `TestSeedCommandsWithStructuredEntries`, so the structured-entry-specific case for hard-deny is
  covered only incidentally. Minor coverage gap, not a comment defect -- flagged, not acted on
  (out of scope for a comment sweep).

## CFG: test_configuration.py (punch-list #07)

4063 -> 3824 lines (96 insertions, 335 deletions per `git diff --stat`).

was verified and kept, or dropped entirely where the class docstring collapsed to one line and
the citation's clause no longer fit. `TestParseFailureAskFloor`, `TestAdditionalContextResolution`,
and `TestEntryForPatternLookup` each kept a corrected one-line pointer; the fourth citation (inside
`TestRulesDirectoryDiscovery`'s now-deleted RED-phase historical block, see below) went with the
block it was embedded in.

docstring had it half-right already: "clamps every decision to 'ask' ... an explicit 'deny' ... is
preserved unchanged" -- internally contradictory but not a clean false universal. Rewrote it to
match the hedged form at `config.py:1526` ("every decision except an already-'deny' one clamps to
'ask'") while trimming to one line per the carve-out.

**Hazard notes: 5 kept (trimmed, 3 with corrected citations), 4 deleted as duplicates/false,
1 corrected.**
- Kept: the `Path.home()`-impurity note above `TestRulesDirectoryDiscovery`'s first three tests

  built as separate temp-dir siblings, so a path-prefix filter genuinely would miss the
  rules-dir layers). Kept: three ConfigIsolationMixin-not-needed notes on
  `TestParseFailureAskFloor`, `TestAdditionalContextResolution`, `TestEntryForPatternLookup`
  (all three classes build `Configuration` from hand-constructed layers with zero file I/O,
  confirmed by reading every test body in each class).
- Corrected (not deleted): `test_structured_entry_is_not_silently_dropped`'s "regression guard for
  the W1 defect" -- verified real via `git log -S "isinstance(perm, str)"` (commit `5dc4816`,
  "TOO-19 Phase 0: structured rule entries...") and confirmed the same hazard is still documented

- Deleted as duplicates: a "Positive control" comment restating a parenthetical already in its
  test's own Then clause; a "confirms the fixture set..." NOTE trimmed to one line rather than cut
  outright (kept as a genuine self-check explainer, since the following assertion looks redundant
  without it); an "~/.toolguard/rules is deliberately left absent" NOTE restating its test's own
  Given clause; three "# unterminated array" inline comments restating a visibly-unclosed `[` in

**Stale ticket narrative deleted wholesale:** a 40-line block above `TestRulesDirectoryDiscovery`
describing a "NOT-YET-IMPLEMENTED" TOO-30 RED-phase contract and the `config_module.<name>`
indirection pattern used to avoid import-time failures on not-yet-existing attributes. Verified

`_merged_rules_by_stem`, `_shadowed_rules_stems`, etc.) exists in `config.py` today -- this is
long since GREEN, not RED, so the indirection's original rationale no longer applies (the pattern

Also deleted: a `TestEntryForPatternLookup` class-docstring paragraph narrating a deleted sibling
test class (`TestEntryForPatternDrift`) and the two tests removed with it -- pure "claims about
the past," git's job not the docstring's.

**Class/helper docstrings trimmed to one line (carve-out):** ~14 classes, matching the pattern
throughout -- e.g. `TestResolvedNoMatchFallback`'s docstring dropped a full enumeration of the
four legal fallback values (verified against `config.py`'s `_VALID_NO_MATCH_FALLBACKS` /
`_VALID_UNDECIDABLE_FALLBACKS` frozensets before cutting, per the standard's own warning that this
enumeration has been silently dropped-a-value twice before) in favor of pointing at the resolver
function itself, since the frozensets are the canonical source and duplicating them here is
exactly the kind of fact that drifts.

**Ticket-tag stripping:** 34 bare `TOO-\d+` references at session start, 0 at the end. Most were
parenthetical tags on otherwise-accurate GWT clauses or hazard notes (stripped the tag, kept the
substantive claim, e.g. "(TOO-19, the canonical no-warning spelling)" -> "(the canonical
no-warning spelling)"); a few were entire sentences of pure ticket narrative or cross-references
to other tests/deleted tests, which were cut outright rather than trimmed.

**Stale/false Given/When/Then:** none found. Every test docstring, after removing narrative
beyond the three GWT clauses, still accurately describes its test body on re-read.

**Assertions that cannot fail / structurally weak:** one candidate, flagged not fixed (comments-
only scope) -- `TestImmutability.test_configuration_frozen` and `test_provenance_frozen` both use
`with self.assertRaises(Exception):` around a frozen-dataclass attribute reassignment (shape 5:
broad enough that an unrelated exception, e.g. from a typo'd attribute name, would satisfy it too,
without actually exercising frozen-ness). Real regression risk is low since dataclass
`frozen=True` raises unconditionally regardless of attribute validity, but the assertion doesn't
pin that. No other instances of shapes 1-14 found after reading every test body and running an AST
scan for assertion-free test methods (none) and asserts nested in an `if <collection>:` with no
`else` (none).

**Mutation testing: 4/4 detected, all for the right reason.** `rsync`-ed the repo (excluding
`logs/`, regenerated an empty one to avoid the environmental-floor false failures) to a scratchpad
directory outside the tree. Baseline: 2733/OK.
- **M1** (layer-precedence): `scalar()`'s `for layer in self.layers:` -> `reversed(self.layers)`.
  2 failures, both in this file (`test_scalar_dotted_more_specific_wins`,
  `test_config_sync_conflict_is_project_wins`), traceback confirms `'user/backups' !=
  'proj/backups'` -- detected for the resolution-order reason the tests name, not a side effect.
- **M2** (discovery precedence): `_rules_dirs()`'s `return (xdg_dir, legacy_dir)` ->
  `(legacy_dir, xdg_dir)`. 4 failures, all in `TestRulesDirectoryDiscovery`, all precedence-order
  assertions. Detected.
- **M3** (layer-precedence, legacy alias): `_resolve_fallback_setting`'s `if raw is None and
  legacy_alias is not None:` -> `if legacy_alias is not None:` (legacy alias always overrides the
  top-level key). 38 failures across the suite -- 10 in this file (the
  `top_level_wins_over_legacy_alias`/`top_level_key_honored` family), the rest a legitimate ripple
  into `test_hook.py`/`test_resolve.py`/`test_verdict_corpus.py` since `no_match_fallback`
  resolution feeds the whole cascade. Detected, and the in-file failures are the right ones.
- **M4** (discovery/symlink guard): `_shadowed_rules_stems`'s resolve()-based same-real-file
  exclusion (`if winning_path.resolve() == shadowed_path.resolve():`) -> `if False:`. 2 failures,
  both in this file (`test_shadowed_rules_stems_excludes_stem_resolving_to_same_real_file`,
  `test_symlinked_same_file_across_dirs_is_not_reported_as_shadowed`), traceback shows the
  symlink-collision fixture wrongly flagged as shadowed. Detected.

Zero-detection count: 0 of 4.

**Process incident, disclosed in full: one mutation briefly landed in the real working tree.**
M1's first application, in a Bash call that relied on cwd persisting from the *previous* call
rather than `cd`-ing explicitly inside that call, wrote to `toolguard/config.py` at the session's
actual default cwd (`/home/arnon/projects/toolguard`) instead of the rsync copy -- the mutation
copy's file was untouched (confirmed unmutated immediately after, which is what surfaced the
problem). Every subsequent mutation (the working M1 retry, M2, M3, M4) explicitly `cd`-ed inside
the same Bash call as the mutation and was correctly applied to and reverted from the copy only,
confirmed by `diff` after each revert. The stray M1 change went undetected until the *final*
project-wide `unittest discover` run at the end of this task, which failed 2 tests in this file
for exactly the M1 signature. Found via `git diff toolguard/config.py`, confirmed as the only
stray mutation (grepped the real file for all four mutations' signature strings; only M1's was
present), fixed with a single-line `Edit` reverting `reversed(self.layers)` back to
`self.layers`, and reverified: full suite 2733/OK, `git diff toolguard/config.py` no longer
contains "reversed", `comment_hygiene.py` back to reporting only the two expected files. No other
file was touched by this incident. Lesson for future mutation-testing passes in this repo:
**never rely on cwd persistence across separate Bash tool calls for a mutation write -- `cd`
inside the same call that writes, every time**, since a still-successful-looking prior call
inconsistently sets the baseline the next call assumes.

**Leads rejected:** none. The brief's "check for a fourth 'clamps every decision' copy" and "check
for a fourth `test/unit/CLAUDE.md` citation cluster" leads were both confirmed present as
described, not rejected.

**Verification gates (final, after the mutation-testing fix above):**
- `uv run python tools/comment_hygiene.py --compare-against HEAD`: only `toolguard/__init__.py`
  and `tools/architecture_fitness.py` report; `test_configuration.py` reports nothing.
- `uv run ruff format --check test/unit/test_configuration.py`: unchanged. `uv run ruff check
  test/unit/test_configuration.py`: all checks passed.
- `uv run python -m unittest discover -s test -t .`: Ran 2733 tests, OK.

  real toolguard binary, `test_guard_canaries_only_runs_on_real_repo`, does still fail under this
  mutation, but not because it detects an empty rule set: its `if report.canary_results: ... else:
  assertEqual(len(report.warnings), 1)` treats "ran with zero results" the same as "skipped", so
  it takes the `else` branch and fails on `0 != 1` -- an accidental catch, not a designed one.
  This is a production fail-open (`run_guard`/`run_guard_canaries` in `tools/architecture_fitness.py`),
  not a test-file defect, so nothing was changed here (comments-only scope); flagging it as a
  code-level finding below.

**Mutation testing: 2/2 detected, both for the right reason, floor stated.** `rsync`-ed the repo
(excluding `.git`) to the scratchpad, confirmed the floor first: 2 errors
(`test_compute_metrics_runs_on_real_repo`, `test_run_guard_runs_on_real_repo_without_lint`, both
`TestSmokeAgainstRealTree` -- the class is the floor, as flagged in the brief; these need the copy
to itself be a proper git checkout with real history, which a `.git`-excluded `rsync` copy isn't).
- **Mutation 1** (`check_layers`, `tools/architecture_fitness.py:379`): `if target_layer not in
  allowed:` -> `if target_layer in allowed:` (inverts the direction-violation check). 4 failures
  beyond the floor, all direction-logic tests (`test_direction_flags_upward_import`,
  `test_direction_allows_downward_import`, `test_render_layers_text_produces_readable_output`,
  `test_check_layers_runs_on_real_tree`) -- detected for the right reason.
- **Mutation 2** (`_r6_guarded_modules`, `tools/architecture_fitness.py:1866`): body replaced with
  `return frozenset()`. 11 failures beyond the floor, all in `TestFindPrivateImports` (8 of its
  real-violation tests) and `TestComputePredicates`/`TestR6GuardedAndCheckedModules` (the
  guarded-modules assembly and membership tests) -- detected for the right reason. Tests asserting
  *absence* of a violation (dunder exemption, unguarded module, parser exclusion) correctly did not
  fire; they don't depend on the guarded set being non-empty.
- Reverted both mutations were applied only inside the scratchpad copy, confirmed via `git diff
  --stat` on the real working tree showing only `test_architecture_fitness.py` (my comment edits);
  the copy was `rm -rf`'d afterward.

**Code-level finding, flagged not fixed (comments-only scope):** `run_guard`/`run_guard_canaries`
in `tools/architecture_fitness.py` report a clean, `ok=True` guard result when `GUARD_CANARIES` is
empty, without distinguishing "checked N cases, 0 mismatches" from "checked nothing." No test in
`test_architecture_fitness.py` exists specifically to catch an accidentally-emptied
`GUARD_CANARIES`; the one test that happens to fail under this mutation does so via an unrelated
branch condition (`if report.canary_results:` treating an empty-but-not-skipped result the same as
skipped), not because it checks that at least one canary ran.

**Verification gates:**
- `uv run python tools/comment_hygiene.py --compare-against HEAD`: only `toolguard/__init__.py`
  and `tools/architecture_fitness.py` report (both pre-existing, from other punch-list work on
  this branch, not touched here); `test_architecture_fitness.py` reports nothing.
- `uv run ruff format --check test/unit/test_architecture_fitness.py`: unchanged. `uv run ruff
  check test/unit/test_architecture_fitness.py`: all checks passed.
- `uv run python -m unittest discover -s test -t .`: Ran 2733 tests, OK.

Applied the general standard (not the test-tier delete-everything rule) to all files except
`test_tools_rule_apply.py`, per the brief. Cut ticket tags (`TOO-19`, `TOO-45 R2`, `TOO-45 D1a
review debt item E`, `Phase 0a increment N`, etc.) throughout; kept verified design rationale.

**Hazard notes -- keep/delete/correct, with verification:**

- **Corrected**: `_once_per_isolation.py`'s module docstring claimed "One copy, used by every
  test module that needs a fresh, isolated claim store per test method." False as of right now:
  `test/unit/test_once_per_store.py` still defines its own byte-identical local
  `_IsolatedStoreMixin` (line 15) rather than importing this shared one -- grepped all four files
  the old docstring named as "previously duplicated" (`test_auto_migrate.py`,
  `test_config_divergence.py`, `test_session_warnings.py`, `test_once_per_store.py`); only the
  first two actually import `IsolatedStoreMixin` from here, `test_session_warnings.py` no longer
  touches `once_per_store` at all, and `test_once_per_store.py` kept its own copy. `git log -S`
  shows `test_once_per_store.py` was added fresh (1040 new lines) in the same commit that created
  this shared module, so "previously four copies, now deduplicated" was never true for it. Deleted
  the "used by X" claim outright (also required by comment-standard rule 1) rather than trying to
  state the current, messier reality in a docstring. **Flagging below as a real, unfixed
  duplication** -- not a comment defect, a code one.
- **Kept, verified true**: `_config_isolation.py`'s three-anchors-plus-a-fourth-anchor claim
  (`Path.home()`, `find_project_root()`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`/`CLAUDE_SETTINGS_PATH`, plus
  `env_config`'s own separate `find_project_root()` reading `TOOLGUARD_LOG_DIR`/cwd) -- grepped
  `toolguard/config.py` and `toolguard/env_config.py` directly; matches
  `.claude/rules/test-config-isolation.md` verbatim. Trimmed narrative, kept the claim.
- **Kept, verified true**: `_real_log_dir_guard.py`'s claim that `install()` wraps exactly
  `log_writer.log_command`, `log_writer.log_discovery`, `error_log.log_conflict/log_error/log_warning`,
  and `once_per_store.reap` -- read `install()`'s body, matches exactly. The "patch at defining
  module so a later `from X import Y` binds to the wrapper" mechanism is real Python import
  semantics, kept (design genuinely needs the explanation; it's the kind of thing someone would
  "fix" back to patching at the importer otherwise). Deleted the "sole caller of `once_per_store.reap`"
  claim (a "used by X" claim, rule 1).
- **Kept, verified true**: `_real_once_per_home_guard.py`'s distinction between `_STORE_PATH` (an
  override flag, `None` by default) and `_resolve_store_path()` (what's actually opened) -- read
  `once_per_store.py`, confirmed `_STORE_PATH: Optional[Path] = None` and `_resolve_store_path()`
  falls through to it. Cut the specific cross-reference to `test_tools_decision_ledger.py`'s
  technique (verified true via grep, but it's a claim reaching outside this file -- rule 0).
- **Kept, verified true**: `test/unit/__init__.py`'s claim that `check_and_warn_divergence`/
  `run_auto_migration` key claims by `project_root` not `logs_dir` -- read both signatures in
  `toolguard/config_divergence.py`/`toolguard/auto_migrate.py`, confirmed.
- **Left alone (out of scope, flagged)**: `_real_log_dir_guard.py:114` has
  `except TypeError, ValueError, OSError:` -- Python 3.14's PEP 758 unparenthesized-except, and it
  parses fine (`ast.parse` verified). But this is the exact THREE-name, parenthesis-free shape
  `toolguard/install_provenance.py`'s own comment (and `test_static_analysis_coverage.py`) name as
  what silently gets a file EXCLUDED from `pyscn`'s analysis with no error, only a stderr warning.
  Comments-only scope means I can't change the code; flagging here since it's the same defect
  `install_provenance.py` was already fixed for, in a file that's central safety-net machinery for
  the whole suite.

No `test/unit/CLAUDE.md` citations found in any of these ten files (grepped all of them).

**`test_tools_rule_apply.py` (ordinary test tier):** removed 7 section-divider comment blocks
(`# --- Helpers ---` etc., not used anywhere else in already-swept sibling files), 2 comments that

`toolguard/rule_sort.py:453-458`'s `parse_permissions_section_with_comments` docstring): a
comment_block preceding the FIRST rule in a subsection anchors to the section top rather than that
rule, which is why `TestStructuredEntrySurvivesUnrelatedEdit` deliberately lists `Bash(ls:*)`
first.

and `toolguard/rule_sort.py`. No false or stale Given/When/Then found -- all descriptions matched
